# Sachivji — Task List

Tracks implementation work against `docs/thouthts.md`. Every requirement below has a stable number; check items off as they land, but don't renumber — the Work Order section refers to these numbers.

## Work Order

The order we'll actually build in, referencing item numbers below. Everything still ships together — this is build sequence, not feature phasing.

1. Persistence foundation — **22**
2. Minimal key input (enough to call a provider) — **21**
3. Single-agent chat loop, end-to-end (streaming text, no tools yet) — **53**
4. Session list wired to real data + new-session flow — **26**, **23**
5. First tool + human-in-loop confirmation — **31**, **34**, **39**, **54**
6. Cross-platform CI (build/typecheck/test on mac/win/linux) — **10**
7. Orchestrator (routing + agent handoff) — **27**, **28**
8. Test harness locked in against the skeleton above — **57**, **58**, **59**
9. Widen tools — **33**, **35**, **36**, **37**, **38**, **32**
10. Widen providers — **16**, **17**, **18**, **19**, **20**
11. Widen tools further: git & GitHub CLI — **60**, **61**
12. MCP client + config — **42**, **43**, **44**, **45**
13. Remaining config UI — **40**, **41**, **55**
14. Context management & token economy — **46**, **47**, **48**, **56**, **49**, **50**, **51**, **52**
15. Cancellation & steering — **29**, **30**
16. Session lifecycle extras — **24**, **25**
17. Ship readiness (branding, versioning, signing, auto-update, release validation) — **7**, **8**, **9**, **11**, **12**, **13**
18. Generic API access tool (introspection, authenticated calls, pagination, async job-status polling) — **70**
19. Structured data-processing tools (HTML/CSV/JSON/XML) — **71**
20. Redesign on branch `design_change` (roles as data, typed handoff, managed Python runtime, MCP Operator, prompt caching) — **110**–**119** and **126** done; open work **120**–**125**, **127**–**136**; see the Redesign section

## Versioning & CI/CD
- [x] 9. Adopt semver policy (major.minor.patch), CHANGELOG convention — policy documented in `README.md`'s Versioning section, `docs/CHANGELOG.md` added (Keep a Changelog format)
- [x] 10. CI pipeline: build + typecheck + test on macOS/Windows/Linux runners
- [ ] 11. Code signing (Windows Authenticode) + notarization (macOS) wired into CI — blocked on 63-65 (external credentials the user needs to obtain). **Decision (Deepak, 2026-10-14): deliberately deferred, not forgotten.** Apple Developer Program enrollment and a Windows Authenticode certificate both cost real money ($99/yr Apple, $100s/yr Windows); not worth paying for while distribution is a handful of named people (the sponsor, etc.) who can click through SmartScreen/Gatekeeper once with instructions. Revisit when distributing to a large number of people, where an unsigned-binary warning on first run stops being an acceptable one-time step. Not a 0.3.0 blocker under today's distribution scope.
- [ ] 63. Apple Developer Program enrollment ($99/yr) + generate a "Developer ID Application" certificate — user action, needed before macOS signing/notarization can be wired up. **Deferred per #11's 2026-10-14 decision** — do this when distributing at scale, not before.
- [ ] 64. Apple notarization credentials (an app-specific password or App Store Connect API key) generated and added as CI secrets — depends on 63. **Deferred alongside 63.**
- [ ] 65. Windows code-signing (Authenticode) certificate obtained (e.g. DigiCert/SSL.com/Sectigo — OV is cheaper than EV, EV avoids SmartScreen reputation warm-up) — user action. **Deferred per #11's 2026-10-14 decision** — same reasoning as 63.
- [ ] 66. Wire real signing + notarization into `electron-builder.yml` and `release.yml` once 63-65 exist — sets `mac.identity`/notarize config and `win.certificateFile`/`certificatePassword` (or CI secret equivalents), replacing the interim `mac.identity: null` / unsigned-Windows defaults. **Deferred alongside 63-65.**
- [x] 12. Auto-update feed (e.g. GitHub Releases) with signature-verified updates — check-only, not silent auto-apply: `electron-updater` checks the GitHub Releases feed and notifies the user with a link; `autoDownload` stays off since an unsigned build (see #11) can't reliably self-replace, especially on macOS
- [x] 13. Release packaging pipeline (`dist:mac`/`dist:win`/`dist:linux`) validated end-to-end — real `release.yml` run against the `v0.1.0` tag, all 3 platforms green, draft GitHub Release has all 9 expected assets (dmg+blockmap+latest-mac.yml, exe+blockmap+latest.yml, AppImage+deb+latest-linux.yml). Found and fixed two real bugs along the way: electron-builder needs `author.email` in `package.json` for `.deb` packaging (fixed with a GitHub no-reply address, not a real personal email, since it's public package metadata) and, even with that, the CI environment specifically still failed the same check for a reason never fully root-caused (a real Linux container reproduction on this machine built fine with the same fix) — resolved more robustly by setting `deb.maintainer` directly in `electron-builder.yml`, bypassing that code path entirely. Draft release is unpublished — user reviews and publishes manually.

  **Follow-up, discovered while adding `sqlite-vec` (#52)**: "validated end-to-end" above meant the CI *build* succeeding on all 3 platforms — it never actually *launched* a packaged app, which turned out to hide a real, launch-blocking bug. First-ever launch of a packaged build (`electron-builder --mac --dir`, run manually on this machine) hung behind a native "uncaught exception" dialog: native binaries (`.node` addons, `sqlite-vec`'s platform `.dylib`) can't `dlopen()` from inside the asar archive, and neither `require.resolve()` nor `import.meta.resolve()` redirect to `app.asar.unpacked` for that case (Electron's asar-unpack redirect is special-cased to its own native-addon `require()`, not to a resolved path a caller then hands to something else). Fixed with `asarUnpack` in `electron-builder.yml` (necessary but not sufficient alone) plus an explicit path rewrite in `src/main/db/index.ts` before calling `loadExtension()`. Verified live on macOS only (no Windows/Linux binaries runnable on this machine) — the fix is platform-symmetric in principle, not empirically confirmed on those two. **Real gap, not fixed here**: neither CI nor this process actually launches a packaged build automatically — this bug would have shipped in the real `v0.1.0` release silently. Worth a future CI step that launches the packaged app and checks for a clean start, not just a successful build.

- [x] 105. Delegated turns no longer wedge a session on the next send — Implemented 2026-09-25 (0.2.1 hotfix)
  - **Found live, reported by the user, not assumed**: session "How many customers are there in northwind database?" failed on every send after its first delegation with `400 Invalid parameter: messages with role 'tool' must be a response to a preceeding message with 'tool_calls'` — the same error the #103 repair was meant to end. Replaying every real session through the actual `toOpenAIMessages` with a pairing checker (both directions) showed 4 of 6 sessions failing, 11 violations in all.
  - **Root cause**: a specialist runs in its own nested conversation (`dispatchDelegate`), but every message its `runLoop` yields is persisted to the same flat session table as it happens (`ipc/register.ts` `insertMessage` per orchestrator `message` event). Stored order is therefore: parent `tool_use delegate_to_x` → the specialist's own assistant/tool_result messages (`agentRole` set) → parent `tool_result` for the delegate. The parent's *live* conversation is correct (its delegate result follows its call; the nested run never enters it), so a turn works — only replay from SQLite is wrong: the delegate call looks unanswered and its real result looks like a `tool` message answering nothing. `repairDanglingToolUses` (#103) made it look half-fixed by adding a synthetic result after the call while leaving the real one orphaned.
  - **Fixed** in `src/main/context/prepare-context.ts`: `repairToolPairs`, three stages run first in `prepareConversation`. (1) `dropNestedDelegateMessages` removes the specialist's messages between a delegate call and its result — only when provably nested (every assistant message in the gap carries `agentRole`; every result in it answers a call made inside it; no real user message in between), since the parent never saw them and the delegate result already carries the specialist's final text. Handles nested-in-nested and parallel calls. (2) `synthesizeMissingResults` (the old #103 logic) for genuinely dangling calls. (3) `dropOrphanResults`, a final invariant: a `tool_result` must answer a `tool_use` in the assistant message right before it, else it is dropped — the backstop for any shape the first two didn't anticipate. Only affects what's sent; SQLite and the UI transcript are untouched.
  - **Verified**: 11 new tests (real-shape reproduction asserting the raw history is wire-invalid, then repaired; two-level nesting; parallel delegate + ordinary tool; sequential delegations; the untagged-fallback case for sessions saved before `agentRole` markers existed; turn-boundary safety; orphan drop; idempotence; no mutation), plus a reusable OpenAI-wire pairing checker in the test file. One pre-existing test fixture (a bare `tool_result` with no call) was itself the orphan shape and was corrected. 866/866 full suite, typecheck clean. Also run through the full `prepareConversation` for all 6 real sessions, including with a tiny context budget forcing the sliding window to drop turns: 0 violations in both, against 11 raw.
  - **Not covered / next**: the root cause — persisting nested messages interleaved in the parent's stream — is unchanged; this repairs at replay. A proper fix (tagging nested messages so the transcript and replay both know, or the handoff contract in `docs/design-discussion.md` §5.1) belongs to the redesign. Not yet re-verified live in the running app (needs a restart).

- [x] 107. Hard-wire the workspace Python venv into `shell_execute`; stop `write_file` corrupting binary files — Implemented 2026-09-25
  - **Found live, user-reported, session "I need you to create a word document that shows bar charts for top 10 customers every year from the Northwind database…"**: the job never ran end to end. The workspace (`~/Documents/Sachivji Workspace`) already had a working `venv/` — matplotlib 3.11, numpy, pillow, python-docx — that an agent had improvised on 9/23, but **nothing in the app mentioned it** (grepped `src/main`: zero references). The only `shell_execute` in the whole session was `python documents/bar_chart_script.py` → exit 127 "python: not found" (this machine has only `python3`, and the system Python has no matplotlib). The Terminal/DevOps role concluded "Python is not available in the current environment" and stopped without trying `python3` or `venv/bin/python`; later the top level told the user it "cannot execute code" although it has `shell_execute`. A separate, data-destroying bug in the same session: `write_file` overwrote a real .docx (made by `office_create_document`) with 174 characters of markdown, corrupting the deliverable.
  - **Fixed, in code — not as prompt instructions** (per the project's "enforce guardrails in code" rule): (1) new `tools/python-env.ts` + `shell.ts`: whenever `<workspace>/venv/bin/python` (or `Scripts\python.exe` on Windows) exists, every `shell_execute` runs with `VIRTUAL_ENV` set, the venv's bin folder first on `PATH` and `PYTHONHOME` cleared (what `activate` does), so a bare `python`/`pip` resolves to it with its packages — the model no longer has to know or choose. An empty/broken `venv/` folder doesn't count. When `python` is missing AND there is no usable venv (exit 127 / 9009 naming python), the result gets a hint that running Python IS possible and how to create the venv, so the model bootstraps one instead of concluding it can't. (2) `write_file` refuses text writes to binary formats (.docx/.doc/.xlsx/.xls/.pptx/.ppt/.pdf/.odt/.ods/.odp/images/.zip/.gz), case-insensitively and before touching anything, naming the right tool (`office_create_document`/`office_fill_template`, `xlsx_write`, `pptx_*`, or a script run via `shell_execute`).
  - **Verified**: 14 new tests (`python-env.test.ts` covers both platforms' branches with injected `exists`/`platform`; `shell.test.ts` runs a fake `venv/bin/python` through the real tool on POSIX; `file-ops.test.ts` checks a real .docx's bytes survive a refused overwrite) — full suite and typecheck clean. Also ran the real `shell_execute` against the real workspace: `python` now resolves to `…/Sachivji Workspace/venv/bin/python` and imports matplotlib + python-docx + numpy (exit 0) — the exact command that failed in the session.
  - **Not covered / honest limits**: a venv isolates *packages*, not the filesystem — `shell_execute` can still read/write anywhere (its own description says "not a sandbox"); a real sandbox is a separate, larger item. Nothing creates the venv automatically when a workspace has none (only the hint) — a managed workspace runtime is in `docs/design-discussion.md` §4.3. The planner/role issues from the same session (no run step in the first plan, a coding role that says it "can't execute", unrequested MCP writes by the Data role, repeated work) are structural and belong to the redesign; this session's prompt is now Appendix D's acceptance scenario. Not yet re-verified live in the running app (needs a restart).
  - **Later updates:** "nothing creates the venv automatically" is superseded by #110 (managed workspace runtime) and #111 (onboarding gate); `python_run` (#115) is the proper way to run scripts.

- [x] 110. Track A1 — managed workspace runtime: `ensureWorkspace(root)`, Python venv created with the workspace, Settings > Runtime — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md 4.3 / section 10 Track A)
  - **Decision (Deepak, 2026-09-25; defaults approved 2026-09-26):** Python is a prerequisite and the venv is created with the workspace and its folders, not improvised by an agent. New `src/main/runtime/workspace-runtime.ts` (+ shared `src/shared/runtime.ts`): idempotent `ensureWorkspace(root)` creates `code/ documents/ resources/`, then the venv; a state machine `missing-python | creating | ready | failed | disabled` (+ a `packages` sub-state) with change notifications. Called at startup and from `WorkspaceSetRoot` (a venv embeds absolute paths, so each root needs its own).
  - **Detection:** `python3`/`python` (`py -3` first on Windows) probed with a 15s timeout; the probe imports `venv` **and** `ensurepip` (Debian ships them separately) and requires Python >= 3.10, with a specific reason for each failure.
  - **Base packages are pinned and chosen to fit Python 3.10** — checked against PyPI `requires_python`, not assumed: the newest numpy (2.5.3) needs 3.12 and matplotlib 3.11.2 needs 3.11, which would have silently raised the floor. Pins: matplotlib 3.10.9, numpy 2.2.6, pillow 12.3.0, python-docx 1.2.0, openpyxl 3.1.5. Always `<venv python> -m pip`. Install is background, non-fatal, and retried by the next ensure via a `venv/.sachiv-runtime.json` marker (so "we made this and packages never finished" is distinguishable from "the user's own venv").
  - **Never destroys user content:** an existing working venv is adopted untouched (packages reported as `adopted`); a broken one, or a non-venv `venv/` folder, is reported and left alone until the user chooses Repair (which deletes it, behind a confirm); a failed creation removes only the partial venv that same call just made. A 600 MB free-space check runs first.
  - **Surfaces:** IPC `runtime:get-state / ensure / repair / set-enabled` + push `runtime:state-changed`; Settings > Runtime shows state, Retry, Repair… (confirm), Turn Python on/off (`pythonRuntimeDisabled` setting → `disabled`). The `shell_execute` no-venv hint now points at Settings > Runtime instead of telling the model to build a venv itself.
  - **Verified:** 21 new tests (fake filesystem + scripted runner, both platforms, every branch above; the stale-root test was mutation-checked — fails with the guard removed); full suite 905/905, `typecheck` clean (node + web). **Real end-to-end run** against the machine's Python 3.12.3 in a scratch workspace with a space in its path: folders + venv + all five packages installed in 8s, all five import, a second launch reported `ready/installed` without redoing anything.
  - **Not covered / next:** the Settings panel and the startup wiring have not been exercised in the running Electron app (needs a restart and a look — only type-checked and unit-tested); no cross-process lock (two app instances could race; the app has no single-instance lock); the onboarding gate (A2), README/installer prerequisite text (A2), the runtime state in `describe_capabilities` (A3) and `python_run` (A4) are still to do.

- [x] 111. Track A2 — Python onboarding gate, README prerequisite, `.deb` dependencies — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md 4.3 / Track A)
  - **Onboarding gate:** first run now has a second step after the secret-storage choice (`FirstRunSetup.svelte`). It follows the runtime state live; **Continue is enabled only when the runtime is `ready`** (packages may still be installing in the background). When Python is missing or setup failed there is no Continue — only **Retry** and **Continue without Python** (persists `pythonRuntimeDisabled`, state `disabled`). Existing installs are unaffected (their onboarding flag is already backfilled); they use Settings > Runtime. A new `onboardingSecretsChosen` setting means quitting mid-way doesn't re-ask (or re-switch to file storage) on the next launch.
  - **README:** new prerequisite bullet — Python 3.10+, per-OS install notes (python.org / `py` launcher / Homebrew, macOS Command Line Tools' Python is too old, Debian/Ubuntu need `python3-venv`), what the app creates, that it needs network once, and that a venv is not a sandbox.
  - **`.deb`:** `electron-builder.yml` now declares `python3` and `python3-venv`. Setting `depends` **replaces** electron-builder's defaults, so the documented 26.15.3 defaults are repeated verbatim (read from `FpmTarget.js`/`linuxOptions.d.ts`) with the two added. dmg / AppImage / NSIS cannot declare this — covered by README + the gate.
  - **Verified live in the real Electron app** (isolated `--user-data-dir`, CDP, real network): fresh first run → keychain → Python step showed "Creating…" with Continue disabled, then Ready with a working new workspace (folders + venv + packages, imports OK); relaunched with no Python on PATH → "Python 3.10 or newer was not found", Retry + Continue without Python only, no Continue; choosing it persisted `disabled` + `onboardingCompleted`, and Settings > Runtime then showed "Turn Python on", which re-detected (not found) and switched to Retry / Repair… / Turn Python off. Also confirmed the running app adopts the real workspace's existing venv (`ready/adopted`). A badge bug found this way (green "Ready" shown while packages were still installing) was fixed. 905/905 tests, typecheck clean.
  - **Not covered / honest limits:** the `.deb` was not actually built (dependency list is from electron-builder's own source, not a produced package — build with `npm run dist:linux` and `dpkg -I` the result before releasing). The runtime is created at *startup*, before the user reaches the Python step, so "Continue without Python" sets `disabled` but leaves a venv that may already exist, and `shell_execute` still activates it — `disabled` only becomes meaningful to the model once A3 puts it in `describe_capabilities`. The first real launch creates any missing managed folders (`code/ documents/ resources/`) in an existing workspace, e.g. an empty `resources/`. No unit tests for the two Svelte components (the repo has none for components; covered by the live run above).

- [x] 112. Redesign step 1 — role manifest, `describe_capabilities`, permanent prompt-budget test — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md section 10, step 1; on `design_change`)
  - **Role manifest** (`src/shared/agents.ts`): `ROLE_MANIFEST` is now the single source for id, label, routing `summary`, listing `description`, `pickable` / `plannable` flags and `examples` / `notExamples` per role. `AgentRole`, `AGENT_ROLES` (mode picker), the new `PLANNABLE_ROLES` (classifier / `VALID_STEP_ROLES`) and `AGENT_ROLE_LABELS` are all derived from it; `roles.ts` reads each summary from it (only prompts and tool lists remain there). **No behaviour change to routing:** summaries are word-for-word what they were; ids and order are unchanged so persisted `agentRole` values stay valid. Adding a role is now one manifest entry plus its prompt/tools.
  - **`describe_capabilities`** (`agents/capabilities.ts`, registered in `ipc/register.ts`, read-only): generated from the manifest, the live registry, the configured DB / API / email connections, MCP servers and the Python runtime state (A3 folded in — `ready` says "Python: AVAILABLE … You CAN write a script and run it", `disabled` says don't offer it, missing/failed says why). Also lists which tools always vs sometimes ask for confirmation. A short sentence in the router's `SYSTEM_PROMPT` (moved to its own module `agents/system-prompt.ts` so tests can measure it) tells it to call the tool instead of answering "what can you do / do you have tools" from memory — the "I don't have the necessary tools" failure and the "cannot execute code" claim from D1/D2. Output for the real registry is ~580 words.
  - **Prompt-budget test** (`agents/prompt-budget.test.ts`): measures the fixed per-request cost with the app's own estimator (tiktoken o200k_base) — baseline **router prompt 1,228 + tool schemas 9,391 = 10,619 tokens**, classify prompt 778, roles 59 / 2,803 / 3,808 / 755 / 2,982 / 241 / 795 / 535 — and fails if any grows past its ceiling (~2% headroom) **or** drops well below it (so a saving must be locked in by lowering the ceiling). Print live numbers with `PRINT_BUDGET=1`. This step itself added ~59 tokens (prompt sentence) plus the tool's schema to the router; that cost is inside the baseline.
  - **Role lint** (`shared/role-lint.ts`): `lintRoleStructure` (id format/uniqueness, non-empty fields, >= 2 examples, >= 1 non-example) is enforced on the real manifest; `lintRoutingSummary` (<= 30 words, names a non-scope AND a hand-off role, no "anything/all tasks", no near-duplicate sibling) is built and unit-tested but **not yet applied to the real summaries** — they predate the contract; step 2 rewrites them and turns it on.
  - **Verified:** 45 new tests (manifest derivations, lint rules both ways, capabilities output per runtime state / connections / MCP collapsing / confirmation lists, budget ceilings — the budget test was seen failing at ceiling 0 before the real values were set); full suite 950/950; `typecheck` clean (node + web). Read the real generated output end to end.
  - **Not covered / next:** not verified live in a running chat — that needs a model and provider key, and I did not spend the user's real API budget or write into their real sessions; please ask "what tools do you have / can you run Python?" after a restart. `AgentRole` is still a literal union derived from the manifest — widening it to a validated string (approved default 3) is the next small step and is what enables file-loaded user roles (section 11). Tool names appear in the generated text but not their per-tool descriptions, by design (size). Step 2 (narrow roles, summaries as contracts, classifier digest, and removing the hand-written "coding writes / terminal-devops runs" rule that produced the D2 plan) is next.
  - **Later updates:** "widening `AgentRole` … still pending" is done (#119); step 2 shipped as #113. Track A3 (runtime state in `describe_capabilities` / the planner digest) was delivered here and in #113 — it has no task of its own.

- [x] 113. Redesign step 2 — narrow roles, summaries as contracts, generated classify digest, router prompt diet — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md section 10, step 2; on `design_change`)

  - **Old sessions keep working:** `terminal-devops` / `distribution` remain as display labels only (`LEGACY_ROLE_LABELS`), and `migrateLegacyDelegateNames` renames their `delegate_to_*` tool calls to `delegate_to_coding` when history is replayed (stored transcript untouched), so no provider is shown a call to a tool that no longer exists.

Largest roles 2.5K / 2.2K / 2.1K — skill decision gate: not warranted yet.

- [x] 114. Redesign step 3 — typed handoff between specialists (artifacts derived in code, data by file, status, redirect / pause) — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md 5.1 / 5.2 / 5.3, section 10 step 3; on `design_change`)
  - **Why (Appendix D2):** the Data step queried 30 real rows, but the next step only got the Data step's prose summary, wrote "Sample data structure assumed", and charted invented customers for years the database doesn't contain; the run then reported success over a chart-less Word file. New `agents/handoff.ts` + orchestrator integration make the channel between specialists carry facts, not prose.
  - **What changed:** (1) **Data by file** — the full result of a data-producing tool call inside a delegate (`db_execute_sql`, `db_query_*`, `db_list_rows`, `db_find_rows`, `parse_csv/json/xml/html`, `api_call`, `xlsx_read`, `web_search`) is saved to `.sachiv-handoff/<turn>/NN-<tool>.json|txt` and the path + size is appended to every later delegate's brief ("Inputs from earlier in this turn … read and use these files; never invent, assume or retype the data"), in plans and in ordinary router delegation; folders older than 7 days are pruned at turn start. (2) **Artifacts derived in code** — a before/after workspace snapshot diff lists what the delegate really created or changed (including files a Python script wrote), shown as "Files created or changed (verified by the app, not self-reported)"; nothing the model merely claims is listed. Measured on your real workspace: 3 ms, 36 files, venv skipped, empty diff when nothing changed. (3) **Typed status** — `failed` set in code; `needs_input` / `out_of_scope` only via a trailing `[handoff: …]` tag (instruction appended to every specialist prompt, ~79 tokens each); no tag = `done`, never a parse error. (4) **Plan behaviour** — `out_of_scope` with a real, different suggested role redirects the step once per turn (no ping-pong); `needs_input` pauses the plan and the final round asks the user; step labels show NEEDS INPUT / OUT OF SCOPE; only the specialist's own text is truncated (2,000 chars), never the app-derived sections. (5) **Unknown-tool redirect** — a tool a specialist owns returns "belongs to <role> — call `delegate_to_<role>`" (needed before the router is slimmed).
  - **Verified:** 33 new tests — 21 unit (tag parsing incl. quoted-earlier and unknown-status cases, snapshot diff incl. same-size rewrite / ignored dirs / capped scan / missing root, data capture incl. unwritable workspace and pruning, formatting incl. "an ordinary reply is EXACTLY unchanged") and 12 orchestrator integration tests reproducing D2 with a mocked model (rows arrive as a file with all 30 rows; script-written files listed; a *claimed* file is not; needs_input pauses; out_of_scope redirects once and not twice; nonexistent suggested role ignored; failed stays failed; router-path handoff; unknown-tool redirect). The D2 test was mutation-checked (fails with capture disabled). All 89 pre-existing orchestrator tests pass unchanged. Full suite 1,009/1,009, `typecheck` clean. Prompt budget: specialist prompts +~79 tokens each (ceilings raised deliberately, recorded in the test); router and classify unchanged.
  - **Not verified / honest limits:** not run against a real model — whether models emit the tag correctly and use the input files is exactly what the routing evaluation must show; please re-run the Northwind D1/D2 prompt. **Per-provider history-replay verification (risk gate 1) is NOT done** — it needs real provider keys and only matters once the router stops carrying every tool (step 4): do it first thing there. Context scoping per hop (open question 2) and the #105 root cause (nested messages persisted interleaved) are unchanged. Artifact detection is a size+mtime scan capped at 20,000 files. `python_run` (A4) still to do — it would report produced files natively.
  - **Later updates:** `python_run` shipped as #115. The per-provider history-replay check is #120 (probe run on Ollama only); per-hop context scoping and the #105 root cause are #125.

- [x] 115. Track A4 — `python_run`: run Python without a shell, report the files it produced — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md 4.3)
  - **Why (Appendix D2):** after "you can execute Python" the model sent Python through `python -c "…; with …; for …"` (invalid: compound statements cannot follow a semicolon) and then pasted multi-line source as the shell command, ~10 near-identical failures, never writing a `.py` file. New `tools/python-run.ts`: takes `code` (the source — saved to `code/run_<time>.py`, kept so it can be re-run) **or** `script` (an existing `.py`), + `args`, + `timeoutSeconds` (default 120, max 600); runs `<workspace>/venv`'s python via `execFile` (no shell, nothing to quote); `MPLBACKEND=Agg`, unbuffered UTF-8 output; returns exit code, stdout, stderr (last 20,000 chars each) and **the files the run created or changed**, found by a before/after workspace diff (shared `tools/workspace-snapshot.ts`, moved out of `agents/handoff.ts`), excluding the generated script itself; says "none — if you expected output, the script didn't write any" when nothing appeared.
  - **Safety/UX:** mutating → the confirmation dialog shows the exact code (first 2,000 chars) or script path + args; honours the runtime state via a new optional `ToolContext.runtimeState` (Python off / still being set up / missing / failed → a specific error pointing at Settings > Runtime, never builds a venv or installs Python); script path must be a `.py` inside the workspace; a user Stop is rejected (not reported as a timeout); timeout is reported explicitly. **A venv is not a sandbox** — the tool description and every message say so.
  - **Developer prompt** now says: use `python_run` (source as `code`, or a `script` path), never `shell_execute` / `python -c`; check the files list against what you expected; don't repeat the same failing run; if Python is unavailable say so and point at Settings > Runtime. `describe_capabilities` names `python_run` in the Python line.
  - **Verified:** 20 new tests incl. real-interpreter runs on POSIX (multi-line compound-statement code that broke the shell in D2; multi-line `code`; generated script saved under `code/` and re-runnable; script by path with args; exact files-created list and the "none" case; traceback returned as a result not an error; headless backend; venv activated for the child; timeout; Stop; tail of very long output; path safety) and the no-Python / disabled / creating paths; Developer-prompt tests rewritten for the new guidance; ownership test asserts `python_run` belongs to Developer only. Full suite 1,029/1,029, `typecheck` clean. **Real end-to-end run:** fresh scratch workspace → real venv + packages (8 s) → `python_run` built a Word document from matplotlib charts with `python-docx`; `unzip -l` shows `word/media/image1-3.png` (three embedded images), `plt.show()` did not hang, and the four produced files were listed by the app. (The chart data in that check was sample data typed for the test, not Northwind data.)
  - **Cost / limits:** the tool schema adds ~243 tokens to the router (10,563 → 10,806) and Developer goes 2,211 → 2,455 (budget ceilings raised deliberately, recorded in the test); trimmed the description once already. Windows: the real-interpreter tests are skipped there (they rely on a POSIX symlinked venv); the Windows branch (`Scripts\python.exe`) is exercised by the existing python-env tests and the runtime tests, not by an actual Windows run. Not run against a real model — whether the Developer reliably picks `python_run` over the shell is what the D1/D2 re-run must show; the shell still works and still has the venv active, so nothing regresses if it doesn't. No cap on a script's own resource use beyond the timeout and output limits.

- [x] 117. Redesign step 5 — MCP Operator role, third-party text containment, user-written server descriptions — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md 3.4, section 10 step 5; on `design_change`)
  - **Why:** MCP tools were attached to five roles (#99). A 144-tool server (gbrain) put every schema in every one of those roles' prompts, and in Appendix D1 a Data specialist answering a SQL question wrote three `energy_wiki` pages nobody asked for, because it could reach the wiki's write tools.
  - **What changed:** new role `mcp-operator` (manifest, pickable / plannable, `requiresAnyOf: ['mcp']`) owning every `mcp__*` tool live; **removed** from Researcher / Developer / Data Access; `selectRouterTools` drops `mcp__*` by rule (the slim router with a 144-tool server is byte-identical to without). Its prompt is a live getter (`mcpOperatorPrompt`): connected servers with the user's description, tool counts and up to 12 tool names, an "untrusted server text" warning, and a "do not create / edit / delete anything not asked for" rule. **New per-server `description`** (`McpServerConfig`, DTOs, `normalizeServerDescription`: trimmed, whitespace-collapsed, capped at 200 on every write): Settings > MCP servers has a field on the add form and an inline **Edit description**; `mcp_create_connection` takes an optional `description` so the Configurator can pre-fill it. **Containment** (`mcp/client.ts` `containToolDescription`): tool descriptions capped at 400 characters, labelled untrusted, whitespace-collapsed, with a labelled fallback when empty; tools carry `Tool.mcp = { server, serverDescription }`. `describe_capabilities` lists servers with their descriptions; the classifier digest marks the operator UNAVAILABLE when none is connected (`ConfigurationCounts.mcp`).
  - **Verified:** 11 net new tests after rewriting the old "MCP visible to five roles" tests to assert the opposite (operator has every MCP tool, no other role has any, live add/remove, live prompt with description and tool-name cap, hostile-description cap and label, description normalisation, slim router identical with or without 144 MCP tools). Full suite 1,074/1,074, `typecheck` clean. **Live, in the running app with a real stdio MCP server** (`scripts/manual-test-mcp-server.mjs`: one tool with a 5,000-character prompt-injection description): it connected (2–3 tools); the injected description arrived at the registry as 489 characters starting with the untrusted label; the user description was stored trimmed and capped at 200; the Settings row and inline editor render.
  - **Cost:** the 11th role adds one delegate tool and one digest line — router schemas 9,944 → 10,098, slim router 2,847 → 2,939, classify 833 → 887; the operator's own prompt is 344 tokens with no server connected (ceilings raised deliberately). What it buys: MCP schemas are now in exactly one role.
  - **Not done / limits:** no lazy schema loading (a very large server makes the operator heavy — the natural next increment); no "include this server's instructions" toggle because nothing consumes handshake instructions; not run with a real model (does the router send MCP requests to the operator? does the operator obey the hostile text? — `docs/manual_testing.md` section 9); the description is text the user must write (an unset one leaves the operator with tool names only).
  - **Also added:** `docs/manual_testing.md` at the repo root — a prioritised manual test plan for everything on `design_change` (steps 1–5, Track A), with pass criteria and a report template.

- [x] 118. Redesign step 7 — schema diet, per-tool budget, Anthropic prompt caching, byte-stability — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md section 6 / section 10 step 7, Appendix E run 2; on `design_change`)
  - **Schema diet (modest, honestly):** measured every built-in tool (44 tools, 9,060 tokens). Most heavy descriptions are dense API docs with little fat; the two with real waste were trimmed — `office_create_document` 794 → 569 (dropped an incident narrative and the triple-stated table/list rule; kept every rule; added "cannot embed images or charts") and `office_extract_template` 520 → 395. **-350 tokens on the full router (10,960 → 10,610) and on Document Creator (2,295 → 1,945); the slim router is unchanged** (it carries neither). New **per-tool budget test** (`prompt-budget.test.ts`): every tool ≤ its ceiling (default 320; tight ceilings for the nine heaviest, with the same "well under ⇒ lower it" ratchet) and the built-in registry ≤ 8,900 in total.
  - **Caching:** `providers/anthropic.ts` `buildAnthropicParams` — `cache_control` on the last tool, the system prompt and the last block of the last message (3 of 4 allowed), no marker on empty text blocks, input not mutated. Usage accounting: cache writes + reads are **summed into `inputTokens`** (else the context meter would under-report nearly everything once caching works); `cacheReadTokens` is surfaced as an optional field on the usage event, for Anthropic and for OpenAI-compatible providers (`prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, automatic prefix caching). **Anthropic cost estimates are now an upper bound.**
  - **Byte-stability** (provider-independent; caching only pays if the prefix never varies): tests assert the router's and a specialist's system + tool list are identical on every round, and identical across separately built orchestrators — the cross-turn case. The cross-turn test was mutation-checked (an injected `performance.now()` in the router prompt fails it; interestingly the *within-turn* tests do not, because the prompt is built once per run — the cross-turn test is the one that matters).
  - **Measured:** the token savings above; and a real latency probe on the local Ollama model (`scripts/cache-probe.mts`, Appendix E run 2): slim 3,494 tokens — 5.1 s uncached / 4.4 s repeat; full 12,313 tokens — 7.4 s uncached / 5.8–15.0 s repeat. On a local thinking model, prompt size is within the noise of generation time — **no latency case for slimming or caching there**; the case rests on token count / cost / tool-selection quality.
  - **Verified:** 58 new tests (per-tool budget `it.each` over all 44 tools, breakpoints incl. empty-block and no-mutation, deterministic params, cache-aware accounting incl. null fields, OpenAI cached-token reporting, three stability tests); full suite 1,132/1,132, `typecheck` clean.
  - **Not done / limits (correction 2026-10-19: the real-API check was done 2026-10-10, see #121):** Anthropic caching was verified against a **mocked SDK only** at the time — not yet the real API (the probe script does it with a key); no cost model that prices cached input; the `SYSTEM_PROMPT` diet (mostly the router-only PowerPoint flow and rule text that cannot be validated without a model); lazy schema loading; a Settings report of tool usage; Google / Groq / Mistral caching not touched (automatic where they support it, not measured). Model-side quality of the two trimmed tool descriptions is unverified — `docs/manual_testing.md` section 11b.

- [x] 119. Widen `AgentRole` to a validated string (groundwork for user-defined roles) — Implemented 2026-09-26 (docs/design-discussion.md section 11.3–11.4; on `design_change`)
  - **What changed:** `AgentRole` is `string` (was a closed union of built-in + retired ids); `BuiltinRoleId` and `LegacyRoleId` keep the exhaustive types where code needs them. New in `shared/agents.ts`: `isValidRoleId` (shape only), `explainInvalidRoleId(id, takenIds)` (why an id cannot be used for a NEW role: shape, length, reserved sentinels `auto/router/orchestrator/default/none/all`, the `delegate*` namespace, built-in and retired ids, duplicates), `MAX_ROLE_ID_LENGTH` (40), and `roleLabel(id)` (real label, else a humanized id, never blank). `role-lint.ts` uses the shared validator. All 17 direct `AGENT_ROLE_LABELS[…]` lookups in the orchestrator, session export, chat and print views now go through `roleLabel`.
  - **Behaviour changes:** (1) a pinned role that does not resolve **falls back to Auto with a visible, persisted warning** ("The pinned "Sql Reporter" agent is not available … using Auto instead") — it used to throw and fail the turn; the old throw-test was replaced, not deleted. (2) `ipc/register.ts` treats anything that is not a well-formed role id as "no pin" (the renderer is a separate process). (3) `describe_capabilities` labels an unknown role via `roleLabel`.

Full suite 1,167/1,167, `typecheck` clean (node + web). **Live in the running app:** a seeded session with messages tagged `sql-reporter` (never existed), `terminal-devops` (retired) and `mcp-operator` renders the chips "Sql Reporter agent", "Terminal/DevOps agent", "MCP Operator agent", with no "undefined" anywhere.
  - **Not done (section 11 proper):** the role file format and loader, a **runtime** role registry — the manifest, `AGENT_ROLES` and `PLANNABLE_ROLES` are still static constants, so a user-defined role could not yet appear in the picker or as a plan target — the Settings "Roles" panel, load-time lint with token-cost warnings, and the extend-only / reserved-id checks are only *available* (`explainInvalidRoleId`), not yet applied by any loader. The pinned-role fallback was not exercised with a real model (needs only a stale pin, which cannot occur until user roles exist).

  - **What exists:** one Markdown file per role (frontmatter + the role's prompt as the body) in `<userData>/roles/`; a purpose-built frontmatter parser and validator (`shared/role-file.ts`, no YAML dependency); a folder loader re-run **every chat turn** (`main/roles/user-roles.ts`: size + mtime cache, keeps the **last working version** of a file whose latest edit has errors, ignores `_*` / `.*` files, first 20 files only); a roster builder with per-role token cost and a budget warning (`main/roles/roster.ts`); `createAgents(registry, userRoles)`; a runtime path for everything that used to be a static list — the orchestrator reads plan targets, labels and the classifier's role list from the agents it was given (`Agent.custom/label/pickable/plannable/meta`), `parsePlan` takes the valid set as a parameter; IPC `roles:get` / `set-enabled` / `open-folder` / `create-example`; the Agent picker, chat and print chips and Settings > Roles (folder, Open / Create example / Reload, enable checkbox, ~tokens per request, inline warnings, per-file errors, read-only built-ins).
  - **Decisions taken (the recommended defaults, section 9 q8):** **extend-only** (a user role can never take a built-in or retired id; `createAgents` refuses one too); folder `<userData>/roles/` (not configurable yet); roles placed in the folder load **enabled** (a disable list is kept in the `disabledRoles` setting); no import / share path; the format is not shared with skills.
  - **Safety:** a role only selects existing tools and supplies a prompt — confirmation and the workspace guard are enforced per tool, so it cannot grant itself anything. A user role is never treated as a tool's owner, so naming a router-only tool (`pptx_add_slide`) cannot make the slim router drop it. Hard caps: file 32 KB, prompt 8,000 chars, summary 500, 40 tool patterns, `maxRounds` 1–30, 20 roles; ids are validated (no `delegate*`, no reserved sentinels, no duplicates). Quality problems (over-broad summary, no named hand-off, overlap, < 2 examples, no tools) are **warnings** — the role still loads.
  - **Verified:** 64 new tests — parser (scalars / lists / quotes / comments / CRLF + BOM / multi-line rejection / duplicate keys / unknown-field warnings), validator (18 error cases, warnings, defaults, one error not a cascade for a file with no frontmatter), loader against **real temp directories** (sorted load, ignored files, shipped example is valid and inert until renamed, invalid files reported by name, duplicate ids, disable list, edit applies on next load, broken edit keeps last-good, deleted file, size and count caps), roster (token cost, budget warnings), runtime (tool resolution exact + prefix + live, notes appended, extend-only defence, slim-router protection, capabilities listing) and the orchestrator end to end with a mocked model (delegate tool, delegation runs the role's own prompt and tools with its id on the chip, a plan may target a plannable user role and is refused for a non-plannable one, pin and pin-fallback). Full suite 1,231/1,231, `typecheck` clean. **Live in the running app** (isolated profile with four real role files): the roster listed 11 built-ins + the good role + a role with an over-broad summary (with its six warnings), reported `broken.md` (one error) and `hijack.md` (`"coding" is a built-in role id`) by name; the Agent picker cycled the built-ins then "Greedy" and "SQL Reporter"; unchecking a role dropped it from the picker and from the token total and persisted `disabledRoles`; a mid-edit break kept the last working version, marked stale with the error and a warning; Create example wrote `_example-role.md`. A layout bug found this way (warnings rendered as a row of narrow columns) was fixed.

Local suite 1,268 passing; CI re-run 36243875895 on `15c5811`: macOS, Linux and Windows all green. **Triggers restored 2026-09-26** (`push` on `main`, `pull_request`, plus `workflow_dispatch`) — done.

  **2026-10-14 — the gap this restoration left: `push` was restored for `main` only, and the entire redesign (2026-09-26 onward, #134 through #211) was done as direct commits on `design_change`, merged to `main` only a few times. Since `design_change` never triggers `ci.yml`, "CI green" went untested against almost a month of real work until today's `design_change` -> `main` merge for 0.3.0 — not a regression from that merge, a blind spot this whole time.** First real run on `main` (`38525670966`) failed on `windows-latest` only (mac/Linux green) with real, previously-latent bugs, found and fixed on a short-lived `fix-windows-ci` branch, each confirmed via a real `windows-latest` CI run before merging (not guessed — this dev machine has no Windows to test against directly):
  - `external-access.ts`: `BLOCKED_ABSOLUTE` entries (`/etc` etc.) have no drive letter. `path.relative`/`resolve` fill a missing drive from `process.cwd()`'s own drive, not from the other path in the comparison, so a driveless pattern and a path resolved against the workspace root's own drive could land on different drives and never match — confirmed live: `isBlocked('/etc/hosts')` returned `false` on Windows, both directly (`external-access.test.ts`) and through the orchestrator's read-file confirmation flow (`orchestrator.test.ts`). Fixed: `isUnder` strips a resolved path's drive letter before comparing against a driveless pattern, win32-only.
  - `environment.ts`: "Common folders" was joined with the default `node:path` import, which is always host-native — a test simulating a mac environment (`platform: 'darwin'`, POSIX-style `home`) got backslashes back when actually run on Windows. Fixed: picks `posix.join`/`win32.join` from the `platform` field itself, not the host.
  - `db/index.ts`: `setDbPath()` discarded the previous `Database` connection without closing it — a real resource leak POSIX tolerates (unlinking an open file works) but Windows doesn't (`EBUSY: resource busy or locked` deleting the temp dir in `db/index.test.ts`'s `afterEach`, confirmed live). Fixed: closes the previous connection before swapping.
  - Three test-only fixes, each a real assumption never checked against a genuine Windows run: `setup.test.ts` assumed "the Documents default" means `app.getPath('documents')` unconditionally, but #202's own OneDrive-avoiding win32 branch (home/Documents) genuinely applies once `process.platform` really is `win32`, not faked; `shell.test.ts` used `1>&2` (bash/cmd redirection) as if it were valid PowerShell syntax ("the '1>&2' operator is reserved for future use"); `shell.test.ts` also assumed `echo > file` writes UTF-8, but PowerShell's `>` redirect writes UTF-16LE with a BOM by default (confirmed, a genuine Windows PowerShell 5.1 quirk — not changed in product code, since the fix would need the same unverifiable-without-real-Windows guessing this whole exercise was trying to avoid; logged here rather than silently patched over).
  - Verified via `gh workflow run CI --ref fix-windows-ci` + `gh run watch`, iterating twice (first iteration fixed everything except the `setDbPath` leak, which only showed up once the first 5 bugs stopped masking it) until all three platforms passed (run `38527986611`), then merged to `main` (`389e77a`) and confirmed the automatic `push`-triggered run on `main` also goes green.
  - **Not a concern going forward**: per the 2026-10-14 decision that both machines now work from `main` directly (not `design_change`), every future push already triggers `ci.yml` automatically — the blind spot was specific to the now-retired `design_change` workflow, not a remaining gap to close.

(b) **The full router does not fit Groq's free tier** (~9.2K tokens vs 8,000 per minute): on that tier the app's default router cannot run at all. Consider selecting the slim router automatically for a provider that answers "request too large", or warning in Settings. (c) A Groq account is limited to 200,000 tokens/day per model — about 60 slim-router calls. (d) **Sachivji's configured Mistral model, `mistral-medium-3.5`, has a request quota of 0 on this account** (every call is HTTP 429 code 1300, as are `mistral-small` and `mistral-medium-latest`; `mistral-large` is 403 `tier_not_allowed`) — the app shows only "429 Rate limit exceeded". Improve that message (read `x-ratelimit-limit-req-minute: 0` / `tier_not_allowed` and say the account has no access to the model), and consider defaulting to a model the account can use (`codestral-latest`, `ministral-8b-latest`). Provider-enforced structured output for the planning call — 2026-09-26 (follows #138). `CompletionRequest.structuredOutput {name, schema}`; `buildPlanSchema(roleIds)` (roles limited to the plannable ones); adapters: OpenAI-compatible (OpenAI, Ollama, Groq, Mistral) `response_format` json_schema (strict), Gemini `responseMimeType` + `responseJsonSchema`, Anthropic a forced tool call whose JSON is streamed back as text (stop mapped to `end_turn`). `classifyAndPlan` sends it by default; a 400/404/422 rejection retries the same call as a plain prompt and remembers the provider:model for the session (auth, rate-limit, network and 5xx errors are not blamed on the schema). **Measured with `scripts/plan-probe.mts`** (8 requests, 4 compound + 4 simple; valid plan out of calls, prompt-only → structured): OpenAI gpt-4o-mini 16/16 → 16/16; Gemini flash-lite 16/16 → 16/16; Mistral ministral-8b 16/16 → 16/16 but only 8/16 were strictly valid JSON before (the rest fenced or wrapped in prose — the loose parser from #138 rescued them; structured: 16/16 strict); **Ollama `gpt-oss-custom`, repeated 2026-09-26 (5 reps × 8 requests = 40 calls per mode, 4096-token budget, 120 s cap; raw output kept out of the repo): prompt-only 36/40 valid plans (35 the expected class, 29.6 s average), structured 33/40 valid (32 the expected class, 26.2 s average) — no benefit, and the difference is within noise (7 vs 4 failures out of 40).** Every failure in both modes was an *empty reply* (no text at all, 6–60 s), never malformed JSON — i.e. exactly the empty-response shape from the PLF session, and provider-enforced structure does not prevent it on Ollama (the constraint covers the visible answer, while gpt-oss's hidden reasoning can still use up the budget; not confirmed — the probe does not yet record the stop reason). The earlier one-sample impressions (7/8 → 8/8, and a `simple` label on the PLF question) were noise: over 5 reps the PLF question was labelled `compound` in 4/5 prompt-only and 3/4 structured replies, and each mode gave one `simple`. Which role the first step went to varied within a mode (data vs research), so the wiki step is not reliably planned by either. **Conclusion:** structured output stays on by default (it removes the prose/fence variance on Mistral and is free on OpenAI/Gemini) but does not fix the Ollama failure. **Still open (next steps, in order):** (a) record `stopReason` and output tokens for empty replies in `plan-probe.mts` to confirm the reasoning-budget cause; (b) retry the planning call once on an empty reply (mirrors the round-loop recovery from #138), or give it a larger output budget; (c) if the PLF question keeps missing its wiki step, rework the planning prompt's definition of compound (two independent sources), independent of format.
