- [x] 142. Planning prompt: name "domain knowledge + data" as its own compound pattern (docs/design-discussion.md §13.2(a)) — Implemented and measured 2026-09-27. In the #139 runs the PLF question ("…you may use the information from wiki and database") was planned as compound in most replies, but its first step went to `data` in some and `research` in others — **never `mcp-operator`** — and each mode gave one `simple`; "two independent sources" alone was not a strong enough rule for a local model to reliably act on.
  - **Built:** `buildClassifySystemPrompt` (`orchestrator.ts`) gained a new named sentence — "Domain knowledge (rules/constraints, or how to interpret something — from a wiki/MCP server, or the web if none is connected) together with the data those rules apply to (database/API) is always compound, one step per source, even in a single sentence" — mirroring the existing named "write code AND run it" / "gather info, THEN save" patterns rather than leaving this shape to be inferred fresh each time. `scripts/plan-probe.mts` gained an `expectRoles` field per case (checked as a set, order-independent) alongside the existing simple/compound check, since `complexity: "compound"` alone doesn't catch a wiki step going missing or landing on the wrong role — added to the PLF case (`['mcp-operator', 'data']`), the existing web+database case (`['research', 'data']`), and one new similar case (wiki + database, phrased differently) — plus a role-set tally in the script's summary output.
  - **Measured** (`npx vite-node --config vitest.config.ts scripts/plan-probe.mts -- ollama gpt-oss-custom:latest --reps=2`, 9 cases × 2 reps × 2 modes = 36 calls, no Anthropic/cloud key available so Ollama only — real numbers, not extrapolated): both modes scored 4/6 on the role-set check overall, but **`mcp-operator` now actually gets selected for a domain-knowledge step** — it appeared in 4 of the 8 non-empty role-checked attempts on the two wiki-shaped cases, including the *original PLF question itself* once (structured, rep 1: `[data, mcp-operator]`, exactly right). That's the real change: #139's own account of its baseline run states the wiki step's role "varied within a mode (data vs research)" — `mcp-operator` is not recorded as ever being chosen before this fix. It is still not reliable (the PLF question also came back `[data]` only, and empty twice — the empty-reply failure is #140/#141, unrelated to this change and still open), so this is a real, measured improvement, not a full fix. The new similar-shaped case (wiki + database, no "wiki"/"database" nouns literally paired the same way as the PLF question) scored 3/4 across both modes, better than the PLF question's 1/4 — suggesting phrasing still matters and the PLF question's specific wording remains the hardest case.
  - **Not done:** a cloud-model comparison (no Anthropic key, #137) and a true "before" run against the unmodified prompt (would need reverting this change and re-running; skipped as unnecessary next to #139's own documented baseline showing `mcp-operator` was never chosen, which this measurement already refutes). Full suite 1,313/1,313 (includes a new orchestrator-level test asserting the classify prompt contains the new sentence), `typecheck` clean. `classifyPrompt`'s token-budget ceiling raised 910 → 950 (887 → 942 measured), documented in `prompt-budget.test.ts`'s baseline comment.
- [x] 143. A model-emitted "found nothing relevant" handoff status (docs/design-discussion.md §13.2(b)) — Implemented 2026-09-27. A third self-reported tag, `[handoff: no_information]`, alongside the existing `needs_input`/`out_of_scope`, for an MCP Operator step where **at least one server is connected** but either none of them fits the request, or one fits but its actual content has nothing relevant to it — a judgment call only the model can make (contrast #145, the zero-servers-connected case, decided in code before the step ever runs, which never reaches this tag).
  - **Built:** `HandoffStatus`/`ParsedTag`/`TAG` (`handoff.ts`) extended to accept `no_information`, parsed exactly like the other two tags (case-tolerant, only counts near the reply's tail). `mcpOperatorPrompt` (`roles.ts`) now ends both its "no connected server fits" branch *and* a new "a server fits but its tools return nothing relevant" branch in the same tag, so a later fallback (#144) can react to either cause the same way. `formatDelegateResult` (`handoff.ts`) gets a fourth branch naming a `no_information` result plainly, with whatever the specialist said it searched. The plan-step marker in `executePlan` (`orchestrator.ts`) gets a fourth branch, `(NO INFORMATION FOUND — nothing relevant in the connected source)`, alongside FAILED/NEEDS INPUT/OUT OF SCOPE — unlike `needs_input`, it does **not** pause the plan; later steps still run.
  - **Scope note:** this item is the tag, prompt wording, and marker text only — the marker does not yet grant permission to fall back to `web_search`/`delegate_to_research`, or carry a provenance sentence forward; that consequence is #144, which depends on this tag existing.
  - **Verified:** the `mcp-operator` role prompt grew past its recorded token-budget ceiling (`prompt-budget.test.ts`, 360 → 380 measured) — raised deliberately to 390 with a dated reason in the file's own baseline comment, per that test's documented two-way policy (trim or raise-with-reason). New tests: `handoff.test.ts` (tag parsing, case tolerance, `formatDelegateResult`'s new branch with and without a detail), `roles.test.ts` (both MCP Operator branches end in the tag), `orchestrator.test.ts` (a `no_information` step shows the marker, keeps the original specialist text, strips the tag itself from what later rounds see, and does not pause or redirect the plan — the next step still ran). Full suite 1,306/1,306, `typecheck` clean.
- [x] 144. Deterministic wiki→web fallback branch, with an explicit opt-out (docs/design-discussion.md §13.2(c), §13.5) — Implemented 2026-09-27, depends on #143. When a plan's domain-knowledge step returns `no_information`, the orchestrator's next round is now told, via the same code-inserted step message, that it may call `web_search` / `delegate_to_research` to fill the gap — this used to only happen by chance (TASKS #96: the top-level holds the full tool registry directly, but nothing directed it to use it this way).
  - **Built:** `detectsSourceRestriction` (`orchestrator.ts`) — a deliberately narrow, conservative regex check on the turn's original request (computed once, before `executePlan`'s loop pushes any step message onto `conversation`) for an unambiguous restriction signal ("only use the wiki", "don't/without ... web/internet", "no web search"). Conservative in the *opposite* direction from `evaluateClassifyGate` (#95): a false negative here (missing a real restriction) is worse than a false positive, since it lets an unwanted web fallback through, so it only fires on a clear phrase and defaults to "not restricted" otherwise. `executePlan`'s step-message construction gets a `fallbackNote`, appended after the existing marker/result text: when `no_information` and not restricted, it names the miss, grants permission to use `web_search`/`delegate_to_research`, and requires plain disclosure if that source switch happens ("say so plainly... came from the web instead of presenting it as verified from the wiki"); when `no_information` and restricted, it explicitly says no web fallback should be attempted. Both live in the same code-inserted text a later step or the final round is built from, per §13.2(d) — not a separate instruction hoping it's remembered.
  - **Deliberately not done here:** `plan-probe.mts` / Appendix D acceptance-scenario cases for this — that needs a real model call to measure (mirrors #142's own "measure before/after on Ollama and one cloud model," itself still open) and Deepak has no Anthropic key (#137); `detectsSourceRestriction` is a first-cut heuristic with no calibration/logging built (unlike the classify-gate's own phase-1/phase-2 pattern, #95) — if false negatives turn out to matter in practice, that's the natural next step, not built speculatively now.
  - **Verified:** unit tests for `detectsSourceRestriction` (fires on each phrase shape; defaults false for an ordinary request and for "only" without a wiki/web mention) plus 2 new orchestrator-level tests (`no_information` grants the fallback with the disclosure requirement when unrestricted; grants neither when the request said "only use the wiki"). Full suite 1,310/1,310, `typecheck` clean.
- [x] 145. Plan-time, config-driven substitution when no MCP server is connected at all (docs/design-discussion.md §13.3) — Implemented 2026-09-27, raised by Deepak the same day. §13.2 assumed an MCP/wiki server exists to be queried; `ConfigurationCounts.mcp === 0` is a fact known before the classifier runs, not a judgment call, so it shouldn't depend on the classifier reliably honoring `buildRoleDigest`'s "UNAVAILABLE right now" note in its own plan — confirmed live: `parsePlan` (`orchestrator.ts:655`) only ever validated role-id membership, never live availability, so nothing stopped a step from targeting an UNAVAILABLE `mcp-operator` and simply wasting itself.
  - **Built:** `isRoleAvailable(agent, config)` extracted from `buildRoleDigest`'s inline check into its own exported function (`capabilities.ts`) — one source of truth for "is this role's connection requirement satisfied," reused by both the digest text and the new code check. `Orchestrator.applyMcpAvailabilityFallback` (`orchestrator.ts`) runs once per compound plan, before the `plan` event is even emitted (so the live checklist shows the real specialist from the start, not a step that will be silently redirected): any step targeting `mcp-operator` while `isRoleAvailable` says no is rewritten to `research`, with the task text prefixed "No MCP/knowledge-base server is connected right now, so treat this as an open-web substitute for domain or process knowledge... Say plainly if nothing genuinely relevant turns up, rather than guessing. Original task: ..." — echoing Researcher's own existing no-Tavily-key honesty wording rather than inventing new phrasing.
  - **Verified:** 4 new orchestrator-level tests (a 2-step plan's `mcp-operator` step rewritten to `research` with the note, when `config.mcp === 0`; left untouched when `config.mcp > 0`; the rewritten step's delegate call actually ran as Researcher, confirmed via the mock's own captured request) plus 4 new `isRoleAvailable` unit tests (`capabilities.test.ts`: unavailable only when no required kind is configured, available when `config` is undefined per the same convention `buildRoleDigest` already used, always available with no `requiresAnyOf`, available if *any* of several required kinds is configured). Full suite 1,302/1,302, `typecheck` clean.
  - **Known, deliberately out of scope here — found while testing, not yet fixed:** (a) the sourcing-restriction opt-out from §13.5/#144 ("only use the wiki") is **not implemented** — there is no existing mechanism anywhere in this codebase to detect that a user restricted sourcing, and building a keyword heuristic for it now would be a guess with no test data behind it; deferred to land alongside #144, which needs the same detection. (b) **A plan with only one step never reaches `executePlan`** (`orchestrator.ts:872`, `if (!plan || plan.steps.length <= 1)` falls through to the ordinary top-level round with delegate tools instead) — so a single-step "look up X in the wiki" request, or a `simple` classification where the top-level model itself calls `delegate_to_mcp-operator` as an ordinary tool, is **not covered by this fix** and can still waste a call when no server is connected. This is the more common shape of a plain wiki lookup, so it's a real gap, not a hypothetical one — worth its own TASKS item if Deepak wants it closed (the top-level's own system prompt/delegate tool descriptions carry no live availability information today, unlike the classify prompt's digest). **Filed as #146.**
- [ ] 146. **TODO (Claude) — same MCP-availability substitution for the single-step / direct-delegate-tool path (follow-up to #145):** #145 only covers a *plan* step (`executePlan`, 2+ steps). A plan with `steps.length <= 1`, or a `simple` classification, never reaches `executePlan` at all — it runs through the ordinary top-level round with delegate tools (`buildDelegateToolDefinitions()`), where the model can call `delegate_to_mcp-operator` as an ordinary tool call with no live-availability information in front of it at all (unlike the classify prompt, which gets `buildRoleDigest`'s "UNAVAILABLE right now" line). This is plausibly the *more common* case in practice — a plain "look this up in the wiki" one-off request — so the gap likely matters more than the multi-step one #145 already closed. Both paths converge on `dispatchDelegate` (`orchestrator.ts`, called from `executePlan` per step and again from the top-level round's own delegate-tool handling around line 1759) — the fix likely belongs there, as one shared check, rather than duplicated per call site. Two design questions to resolve before implementing, not obvious from #145 alone: (1) if `dispatchDelegate` itself redirects `mcp-operator` → `research` when unavailable, the already-emitted `tool_use_start` event for the model's own `delegate_to_mcp-operator` call still names `mcp-operator` — decide whether/how the transcript's tool-call attribution should reflect the redirect, or whether it's acceptable for the tool name and the actual specialist that ran to differ (unlike #145, where the plan-array rewrite happens *before* anything is shown, so no such mismatch exists there). (2) whether to also adjust `delegate_to_mcp-operator`'s own tool description to mention live availability (mirroring the classify digest) so the top-level model is less likely to attempt it in the first place, as a cheap first line of defense in addition to the code-level redirect. Tests: a top-level round that calls `delegate_to_mcp-operator` via a tool_use with `config.mcp === 0` ends up actually running Researcher with the same code-inserted note as #145; unaffected when `config.mcp > 0`; the existing single-step and `executePlan` test suites still pass unchanged. **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.3.2, option A, MCP Operator only: do not offer `delegate_to_mcp-operator` when no MCP server is connected, using `isRoleAvailable` (verified: `buildDelegateToolDefinitions()` currently offers every role).**

## Provider adapter layer
- [x] 14. Unified `ModelProvider` interface (messages, streaming events, tool schema)
- [x] 15. Anthropic adapter (streaming, token counting, model listing)
- [x] 16. OpenAI adapter (Chat Completions streaming + tool-call mapping; Responses API migration left for later)
- [x] 17. Google (Gemini) adapter (Tools mapping)
- [x] 18. Ollama adapter (local models, tool format mapping)
- [x] 19. Provider registry: add-a-provider plugin mechanism
- [x] 20. Runtime model/provider switcher (UI + persisted preference via `app_settings` table)
- [x] 21. API key registration UI (uses keychain store)
- [x] 100. Fix Anthropic input-token accounting (was hardcoded to 0) — flagged by an external code review (`review.md`), confirmed and worse than "untested": `providers/anthropic.ts` emitted `inputTokens: 0` on every `usage` event derived from `message_delta`, and `message_start`'s own `usage.input_tokens` (where Anthropic actually reports it) was never read. `anthropic.test.ts` had been *asserting* `inputTokens: 0` as correct, so the bug was locked in by a passing test rather than just uncovered — session usage, cost estimates (#47), and the context-usage meter (#48) were all wrong for every Anthropic-backed session. Now captures `message_start.message.usage.input_tokens` into a local var and emits it on every subsequent `usage` event this round. Test fixtures updated to include `message_start`; kept a defensive test confirming it still reports 0 (not a crash) if `message_start` is somehow missing.
- [x] 90. Groq provider support ([console.groq.com/docs/overview](https://console.groq.com/docs/overview)) — confirmed against Groq's own current docs (`.../docs/openai`, `.../docs/api-reference`, `.../docs/tool-use`, `.../docs/models`), not assumed: base URL `https://api.groq.com/openai/v1`, a standard Bearer API key, `/v1/models` listing, SSE streaming and tool-call request/response shapes identical to OpenAI's own wire format. Groq's documented OpenAI-compatibility gaps (`logprobs`/`logit_bias`/`top_logprobs`, `messages[].name`, `N != 1`, `temperature: 0`→`1e-8`) are never triggered — `toOpenAIMessages`/`streamOpenAICompatible` (`openai-compatible.ts`) never send any of those fields — so `src/main/providers/groq.ts` extends the shared adapter exactly like Ollama does, not a from-scratch one. No `/v1/embeddings` endpoint exists on Groq, so unlike OpenAI/Ollama it implements only `ModelProvider`, not `EmbeddingProvider`. Registered as a new `ProviderRegistry` entry (`requiresApiKey: true`, `supportsEmbeddings: false`) in `providers/setup.ts` — confirmed the Settings provider list/IPC layer (`ipc/register.ts`'s `ProvidersList` handler) is fully registry-driven, so this needed zero renderer changes. `model-metadata.ts` gained real context-window/max-output-token entries for Groq's current models (`llama-3.1-8b-instant`, `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `openai/gpt-oss-120b`/`20b`, all confirmed against `.../docs/models`) — pricing deliberately left unset (`pricePerMillionTokens` correctly falls back to "cost unknown," not a guess): Groq's self-serve pricing moved materially as recently as 2026-08-26 per third-party sources (some Llama models reportedly gated to enterprise-only "contact sales" pricing), and no current first-party numbers were confirmed directly from Groq's own pricing page at implementation time. Not yet live-verified against a real Groq API key/account (user will obtain one); all coverage here is against mocked responses matching Groq's documented wire format, same rigor `openai.test.ts` already applies to real OpenAI.
- [x] 101. Mistral provider support ([console.mistral.ai/api-keys](https://console.mistral.ai/api-keys)) — confirmed against Mistral's own current docs (docs.mistral.ai/api, `.../api/endpoint/chat`, `.../api/endpoint/models`, `.../api/endpoint/embeddings`, `.../resources/migration-guides`): base URL `https://api.mistral.ai/v1`, a standard Bearer API key, and the migration guide states plainly the Chat Completions API "follows the same request structure as OpenAI," usable by pointing an OpenAI-compatible client at Mistral with just a base-URL/model-name change — no library replacement required. `src/main/providers/mistral.ts` follows the same pattern as Ollama/Groq (points the `openai` SDK at Mistral's base URL, deliberately bypassing Mistral's own native SDK, whose higher-level `client.chat.stream()` wrapper has a different, non-OpenAI-shaped event model). Unlike Groq, Mistral genuinely has a `/v1/embeddings` endpoint (`mistral-embed`, confirmed request/response shape matches OpenAI/Ollama's existing `embed()` handling), so this implements `EmbeddingProvider` too — registered in `getEmbeddingProvider()` (`providers/setup.ts`) alongside openai/google/ollama. **A real, provider-reported context window, not a guess**: Mistral's own `GET /v1/models` response includes a genuine `max_context_length` field per model (confirmed against its docs) — implemented as `loadedContextWindow` (a raw `fetch`, since the `openai` SDK's type has no such field) rather than a static `model-metadata.ts` table entry, and deliberately named `loadedContextWindow` rather than `configuredContextWindow` specifically because `orchestrator.ts`'s `resolveMaxOutputTokens` only ever reads the former — this way both the input-side trim (`prepare-context.ts`) and the output-token budget benefit from the real number. No static context-window/max-output-token/pricing entries were added to `model-metadata.ts` for Mistral at all: couldn't get a clean, confident current table for the whole model family (Mistral's docs pages didn't render full model/context/pricing tables via this session's fetch tooling, and third-party aggregator numbers weren't trusted per this table's own "real, confident, or omitted" policy) — an unrecognized Mistral model id safely falls back to the generic cloud estimate for max-output/pricing display ("cost unknown"), while context-window sizing gets the real live number regardless via `loadedContextWindow`. **Not yet live-verified against a real account** — genuinely more uncertain than Groq's confirmed-live compatibility: Mistral's docs didn't expose the exact streamed `tool_calls` delta JSON shape directly, only that the request/response structure matches OpenAI's; the native SDK's own streaming wrapper handles tool calls differently at a higher level, which doesn't necessarily reflect the raw wire format this adapter actually talks, but wasn't independently confirmed either way. User has a real API key ready — first real turn (including a tool call) should be run before this is treated as trustworthy as the existing providers.
- [x] 147. Show real LLM thinking/reasoning content in the UI, instead of a synthesized "Thinking…" placeholder — user question 2026-09-28, prompted directly by a slow Ollama session (`qwen3.5:9b-custom`, a 27B model running CPU-only at ~6.5 tok/s) where the static placeholder gave no visibility into a multi-minute wait. Confirmed this is a deliberate current gap, not an oversight: `orchestrator.ts`'s `roundStatusMessage()` doc comment states no provider in this app streams reasoning as a separate signal; `openai-compatible.ts` (shared by Ollama/Groq/OpenAI) only reads `delta.content`, never `delta.reasoning`/`reasoning_content`; `anthropic.ts` never requests extended thinking (`thinking: { type: 'enabled', ... }`) or handles `thinking_delta` SSE events; `google.ts` only handles the opaque `thoughtSignature` (required for tool-call continuity, not human-readable) — never sets `thinkingConfig.includeThoughts` or reads `part.thought`. Confirmed **Ollama and Groq** are genuine candidates (both host models — `qwen3.5:9b-custom`, `openai/gpt-oss-120b` — that report real reasoning content via the shared `streamOpenAICompatible` path) and **Gemini** is a candidate but needs an opt-in request-shape change (`thinkingConfig`) plus a live check of whether the configured model (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) actually returns thought parts. **OpenAI is excluded**: OpenAI's own reasoning models don't stream raw chain-of-thought via the Chat Completions API this app uses (policy-level, not a wiring gap), and the configured OpenAI model (`gpt-4o-mini`) isn't a reasoning model anyway. Architecturally cheap to add once designed: `ContentBlock`/`StreamEvent` (`shared/protocol.ts`) are already block-type unions with no DB migration needed (`messages.content` is a JSON blob) — needs a new `thinking` block/event type, a `DisplaySegment` kind (`App.svelte`), and a render branch in `ChatView.svelte` (likely collapsible, same pattern as #72's tool-output collapse). **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak):** collapsed-by-default, reusing the #72/#88 tool-output collapse pattern rather than a new convention; `streamOpenAICompatible`'s delta parsing checks both `reasoning` and `reasoning_content` field names (different OpenAI-compatible-shaped providers use one or the other) and normalizes whichever is present into the internal `thinking` block type; scope is Ollama/Groq/Gemini only — **Anthropic extended thinking is explicitly deferred, tracked separately as #159** (bigger lift: opt-in `thinking` request shape, `thinking_delta` SSE handling, redacted-thinking-block rules for multi-turn context — and unverifiable anyway until #137's Anthropic key blocker clears). Ready to implement (Ollama/Groq/Gemini scope).
  - **Implemented.** `shared/protocol.ts` gained `thinking` (`ContentBlock`) and `thinking_delta` (`StreamEvent`), both carrying `agentRole` exactly like `text`/`text_delta` already do. `openai-compatible.ts` checks both `delta.reasoning` and `delta.reasoning_content` per chunk (cast, since neither is in the official OpenAI SDK type) and normalizes whichever is present into `thinking_delta` — used by both Ollama and Groq since they share this one streaming function. `google.ts` sets `thinkingConfig: { includeThoughts: true }` on the request and routes any `part.thought === true` part to `thinking_delta` instead of `text_delta`. `orchestrator.ts`'s `runLoop` gained an `assistantThinking` accumulator alongside the existing `assistantText` one, streams each `thinking_delta` live as it arrives, and — only if anything was actually accumulated — pushes a `thinking` block onto `assistantBlocks` **before** the `text` block, so a persisted/reloaded message replays reasoning-then-answer in the same order a live stream showed it; never pushes an empty thinking block when a round streamed no reasoning at all. `sessions/export.ts`'s `blockToMarkdown()` gained an explicit `thinking` branch (its fallback previously assumed a `tool_result` shape and would have thrown a type error the same way it did twice already for `user_note`). Renderer: `App.svelte`'s `DisplaySegment.kind` gained `'thinking'`; the old single-purpose `appendTextSegment` was generalized to `appendDeltaSegment(msg, kind, text, agentRole)` so `text_delta` and `thinking_delta` share the same "append to trailing same-kind segment, else start a new one" logic. `ChatView.svelte` renders a collapsible `.thinking-block` (🧠 icon, "Thinking (click to expand)" head, truncated-preview body with a "Show more" toggle) reusing the exact `toolDetailsExpanded`/`toolBlockOverride` global-toggle-plus-per-segment-override pattern #72/#88 already established for tool output, via a new `lastThinkingSegmentId`/`isThinkingBlockExpanded`/`toggleThinkingBlock` trio mirroring the existing tool-block ones — most recent thinking block defaults open, older ones default collapsed, both independently overridable by click.
  - New tests: `openai-compatible.test.ts` (thinking_delta for `reasoning`, for `reasoning_content`, and correct ordering against a real `text_delta` in the same stream); `google.test.ts` (the request actually carries `thinkingConfig`, a `part.thought` part routes to `thinking_delta` not `text_delta`, and a response with no thought parts is a graceful no-op); `orchestrator.test.ts`, a new `describe('real reasoning/thinking content (#147)')` block with 3 tests (a `thinking` block persists before the `text` block in the same assistant message, and the live `thinking_delta` stream event fires too; a round that streams no reasoning never persists an empty `thinking` block; a delegated specialist's own reasoning is tagged with its `agentRole`, same as `text_delta` already is). `test/support/mock-provider.ts`'s `ScriptedTurn` gained an optional `thinking` field, yielded as a single `thinking_delta` before `text` — matching every real provider's own ordering (reason first, answer after). Full suite 1359/1359 passing, typecheck clean.
  - **Live-verified against a real running Ollama model** (`qwen3.5:9b-custom`, isolated Electron instance, real UI interaction over CDP — not mocked). First confirmed live at the transport level that this exact model streams a real `reasoning` field per-chunk over Ollama's OpenAI-compatible `/v1/chat/completions` endpoint (`curl … -d '{"stream":true,...}'`), including with a system prompt and a `tools` array present (ruling out "only works for a bare request" as an explanation). Two initial UI runs showed *no* thinking block at all despite that — genuinely alarming, since the transport-level check said the field should be there. Root-caused, not shrugged off: a temporary debug build (`console.error` of the outgoing request shape and the first few raw chunks, `openai-compatible.ts`, removed before commit) proved the orchestrator/IPC/renderer plumbing was never the problem — a subsequent run against the same debug build, given more time to finish (this CPU-only model takes over a minute per full-system-prompt round), showed `thinking_delta` events arriving correctly and a real "🧠 Thinking (click to expand)" block rendering in the transcript with genuine multi-paragraph reasoning content inside. Conclusion: the two earlier "failures" were the model itself choosing not to emit a separate `reasoning` field for that particular turn (observed live: for a trivial "what is 2+2" prompt it sometimes answers directly with no chain-of-thought at all, or inlines brief reasoning into the answer text itself rather than the separate field) — a genuine, expected model-behavior variance, not an app defect. When the model does stream reasoning, the app now threads it through correctly end-to-end, confirmed live.

- [x] 91. Rate-limit detection + wait-and-retry, across every LLM provider — motivated directly by a real session this cycle (repeated Groq calls that produced nothing, hypothesized as a rate limit; see #102's own investigation). Confirmed against each SDK's real error types rather than guessed (`src/main/providers/rate-limit.ts`): Anthropic's and OpenAI's (shared by OpenAI/Ollama/Groq/Mistral via `openai-compatible.ts`) `APIError`/`RateLimitError` both expose a real `status: number` and `headers: Headers`; Google's `@google/genai` `ApiError` has `status` but *no* `headers` at all, so a Google-sourced rate limit always falls back to backoff, never a real `Retry-After`. Duck-typed on `.status === 429` — a genuinely shared, stable field across all 3 SDK families here, not per-SDK shape-guessing. Important finding along the way: all 3 SDKs already retry a 429 internally (~2 attempts, short capped backoff) before ever throwing up to this app's code at all — confirmed by reading `openai`'s own `client.js` (`shouldRetry`/`retryRequest`); this app's own retry (`orchestrator.ts`'s `handleRateLimitError`) is a deliberate *second*, longer-patience layer for when that wasn't enough (the common real case on a free/low tier, where the actual rate-limit window outlasts the SDK's own short backoff), not a duplicate of it. **Detect and inform**: a distinct, honest warning ("The provider says you've hit its rate limit — waiting Ns before retrying (attempt N/5)"), mirroring #80's own wording lesson — never a generic failure that reads like a bug. **Wait and retry**: up to `MAX_RATE_LIMIT_RETRIES = 5`, respecting a real `Retry-After`/`retry-after-ms` header when present, exponential backoff (2s, doubling) otherwise, every single wait capped at `RATE_LIMIT_MAX_WAIT_MS = 2 minutes` even if the provider asks for longer. **Real, separate accounting, not bolted onto #80's mechanism**: a new `state.consecutiveRateLimitRetries` field, entirely independent of `consecutiveRoundTimeouts`; critically, `state.deadlineAt += <actual time waited>` after every rate-limit wait, so a deliberate wait is never silently counted against — or mistaken for — the same 15-minute wall-clock budget built for a genuinely hung round. A Stop click during the wait ends the turn immediately via the same `stopped_by_user` path #102 built (refactored the 3 existing call sites of that pattern into one shared `emitStoppedNoAnswer` helper along the way, now used at all 4). Retries exhausted → a clear, specific thrown message ("...still in effect after 5 retries — try again in a few minutes"), not the raw SDK error text — flows into #102's `communication_failure` handling once it reaches `ipc/register.ts`. `classifyAndPlan`'s one-shot probe (and the round-loop's final wrap-up call) deliberately do **not** get this retry treatment — both already documented themselves as "no retry, falls open" one-shot calls before this, and a rate limit there now falls open the same way a timeout already did, rather than building a second retry mechanism for a already-last-resort path. 8 new orchestrator-level tests: retry-then-succeed, exact `Retry-After` timing, backoff fallback, the 2-minute wait cap, exhausting retries with the specific message, Stop-during-wait, `classifyAndPlan` falling open, and — the one that actually proves the deadline-extension logic rather than just trusting it — a scenario combining near-total round-timeout deadline exhaustion with rate-limited retries that only succeeds because each wait pushes `deadlineAt` forward.

## Multi-agent system
- [x] 27. Implement `Orchestrator.run` (routing step -> agent handoff -> result; tool-call loop below moves here once it lands)
- [x] 28. Define system prompts/tool sets per agent role (Planning, Research, Coding, Terminal/DevOps, Data, Distribution)
  - **2026-09-22 — two real live prompt-quality bugs, user-reported ("the DB Agent has done a very bad job," "the research is not done well").** Data: asked for a Plant Load Factor, fetched four separate raw-row batches (a whole year of one table, then another) to manually work through by hand instead of writing one aggregate query, never used most of what it fetched, and still landed on the wrong number by summing a similarly-named column instead of the one actually asked about (the earlier, correct session had used `SUM(actual)/SUM(aep_target)` directly) — both traced through the real session transcript. Fixed: the Data prompt now requires planning the query up front (one SQL aggregate via `db_execute_sql`, never fetching raw rows to hand-total/compare — a table's individual rows are for inspecting a few real examples only) and matching a question's stated quantity to the column whose real name/semantics actually mean that, checked via `db_introspect_schema` rather than guessed. Research: two broad `web_search` calls were treated as complete research for a presentation request, and every downstream slide inherited that thinness. Fixed: the Research prompt now sets an explicit depth bar for a report/presentation-shaped request — search results are candidates to investigate, not the research itself; read the real content of the several best sources via `parse_html` before calling it done.
  - **2026-09-23 — a real "write code, run code" role-boundary bug, user-reported ("why did the app generate the Bar Chart multiple times when there was a single request").** Traced through the real session transcript (`sqlite3` query against the persisted `messages` table, with each block's `agentRole` tag, not just the rendered UI): asked to "create a bar chart," classify-and-plan routed the whole "generate a bar chart visualization using Python and save it as an image file" step to `coding` alone. `coding` has `write_file` but no execution tool at all (`shell_execute` is `terminal-devops`-only) — it wrote a real `generate_chart.py`, tried `shell_execute` itself, got `Unknown tool "shell_execute"`, and then reported success in confident prose ("I have created a Python script... to generate and save a professional bar chart visualization") without the chart ever existing yet. The orchestrator then repeated the identical mistake — `delegate_to_coding` again instead of `delegate_to_terminal_devops` — rewriting the same script a second time for no benefit. The file only came into existence several rounds later, once the *top-level* orchestrator (which holds every tool directly, unlike a specialist) gave up delegating and ran `shell_execute` itself, then worked through a bad relative path, an `externally-managed-environment` pip restriction, and finally a `venv`-based install to actually produce `northwind_orders_1998.png`. Only one final image ever existed — the "multiple times" was the script being written twice and execution attempted four times before one succeeded, which is what made a real, single request look like repeated, chaotic regeneration. **Fixed in two places**, mirroring #73's "gather info, then save a file" fix for the same underlying shape (an implicit two-role task collapsed into one step): (1) `coding`'s system prompt now says plainly it has no shell/execution access at all, must never call an execution tool, must never report a task done because it wrote code that *would* produce a result if run, and that a task needing code to actually run should be written, then hand back a result saying so — the orchestrator's job is to `delegate_to_terminal_devops` for the execution step, never assumed to happen invisibly. (2) classify-and-plan's prompt now names "write code AND run it" as its own compound pattern requiring two separate steps (`coding` writes, a later `terminal-devops` step runs), never one, with this exact incident as the named example. 2 new tests (`orchestrator.test.ts`: classify prompt contains the new guidance; `roles.test.ts`: coding's prompt contains the no-execution-access wording and `delegate_to_terminal_devops`, and coding's own tool list genuinely excludes `shell_execute`) — 687/687 full suite passing, typecheck clean. Not yet re-verified live against a real running session with the fix in place.
- [x] 29. Cancellation & steering: AbortController plumbed from UI -> orchestrator -> provider stream — cancels the in-flight streaming response; does not interrupt a tool already executing or auto-dismiss an open confirmation dialog (the existing Deny button covers that)
  - **2026-09-22 — two real round-loop reliability bugs, found live in the same investigation.** (1) **Stop clicked during the classify-and-plan pre-pass did nothing**: that call's own catch swallows an abort and returns `undefined` (the same shape as "no plan needed"), and `run()` fell straight through into starting a brand-new full round instead of ending the turn — silently discarding the cancellation and spending an extra model call before anything acknowledged Stop. Fixed with an explicit post-classify abort check, scoped to the planning-enabled path only (the already-correct non-planning abort behavior is untouched). (2) **A round that finished cleanly but produced no text and no tool call ended the turn in total silence** — nothing shown, nothing persisted, indistinguishable from the app doing nothing; found live as three separate unanswered user turns in a row before the actual cause (a model producing genuinely empty output on a long, tool-heavy conversation) was traced. Now surfaces a warning, mirroring the existing output-token-limit-cutoff warning. Both are `runLoop`/`run()` changes with new regression tests; the second scripts a real, successful response the bug would have let slip through as "nothing happened," and the fix must never reach it.
- [x] 30. Mid-run steering: edit a pending tool action before it executes — was already implemented via the confirmation dialog's editable JSON, just never checked off; live-verified this pass (edited a write_file call's content, confirmed the edit — not the model's original — landed on disk)
- [x] 93. Thread cancellation into `Tool.execute()` — flagged by an external code review (`review.md`), confirmed: `TOOL_EXECUTE_TIMEOUT_MS` (`orchestrator.ts`) already bounded how long the orchestrator *waited* on a tool call, but `Tool.execute()` never received any `AbortSignal`, so a mutating tool kept running after Stop or a round timeout regardless. Added an optional third `signal?: AbortSignal` param to the `Tool.execute` contract (`tools/types.ts`) plus a `cancellable?: boolean` flag; `executeWithTimeout` now builds its own `AbortController` (aborted from either a real Stop or its own timer) and passes that signal into every `tool.execute` call — additive, so a tool that ignores the param is completely unaffected. Wired real cancellation into `shellExecuteTool` (`cancellable: true`, passes `signal` straight into Node's `exec()`, which kills the child process on abort — live-verified in `shell.test.ts` that an aborted `sleep 5 && echo done > out.txt` never actually writes the file). Every other tool (file writes, DB mutations, email, deploy) stays non-cancellable for now — the timeout/stop message is honest about it ("may still be running in the background") rather than implying it was safely stopped, matching the review's own fallback recommendation. New tests: a cancellable tool's `execute()` genuinely observes `signal.aborted`, and a non-cancellable one's timeout message doesn't claim it was stopped.
- [x] 94. Cap classifier-generated plan length — flagged by `review.md`, confirmed: `parsePlan()` (`orchestrator.ts`) validated each step's role/description but never capped `steps.length`. Now truncates (doesn't reject outright) to `MAX_PLAN_STEPS = 5`, keeping the first 5 steps rather than discarding a mostly-good plan, and `classifyAndPlan` surfaces a warning (same persisted-warning pattern as the existing max_tokens/empty-response cases) when truncation happens. New test scripts a 7-step classifier response and confirms only 5 steps actually dispatch, with the warning present.
- [x] 95. Deterministic pre-gate for `classifyAndPlan`, phased — flagged by `review.md`: planning is on by default and the classifier ran an extra full LLM call before *every* turn, even trivial ones, with no cheap pre-check first. Per explicit direction ("start with simple heuristics and then enhance it with feedback and learning" — not confident a heuristic alone would be accurate enough to trust blindly): built in two phases. **Phase 1** (`agents/classify-gate.ts`, `evaluateClassifyGate`) — a conservative deterministic gate that only returns `'skip'` (never decides "compound" itself) for a short (≤50 char), single-sentence message with no compound-connector word (`and`/`then`/`also`/`after`) or `;`/newline; anything even plausibly compound falls through to `'run'`, the real classifier. **Phase 2** (`db/classify-gate-log.ts`, a new `classify_gate_log` table) — every time the real classifier actually runs (`'run'` verdict, or a `'skip'` verdict overridden by `shouldSampleForCalibration`'s 15% sampling rate purely for measurement — the real result is still acted on normally either way), its output is logged alongside the gate's own verdict via an optional `logClassifyGateSample` constructor option (defaults to a no-op, keeping `orchestrator.ts` itself DB-free per its own design), wired to the real DB in `ipc/register.ts`. `getClassifyGateAccuracy()` gives a queryable count of samples/skips/false-skips (gate said skip but the request was actually compound) for later analysis — no consuming UI built yet, deliberately out of scope here. New tests: the gate's own heuristic (`classify-gate.test.ts`), the log repository (`classify-gate-log.test.ts`), and orchestrator-level integration (classify call genuinely skipped/not skipped, sampling override, and correct/absent logging) — several existing classify-and-plan tests that used a bare `'hi'` trigger message were updated to a gate-bypassing message, since they test `classifyAndPlan` itself, not the new gate.
- [x] 154. A third confirmation option, "Talk about it," Implemented 2026-09-29. alongside Approve/Deny on every tool-call confirmation dialog — user request 2026-09-28. Today's confirmation flow (#39; `ChatView.svelte`'s `onApprove`/`onDeny` props feed a confirmation dialog around line 484-491) only has two outcomes for a pending `mutates: true` tool call. Wanted: a third button that hands control back to the user to type a comment/question/correction instead of a bare approve/deny, and the app then acts on that input — user's own framing: "the Orchestrator may pause the flow to take additional input before the confirmation step, may ask for clarification or followup or continue the processing." This is an orchestrator-level design question, not just a UI addition: `runLoop`'s tool-execution loop (`orchestrator.ts`, the round's tool-dispatch section) currently either executes a tool (approved) or synthesizes an immediate "The user denied this tool call" `tool_result` (denied, #102's `[Rejected by user]` prefix) and continues the round; a genuine mid-flow pause needs a new state that holds the pending tool call, feeds the user's free-text comment back to the model as new input while keeping the paused call in play (not silently discarded), and lets the model decide whether to revise the call, ask a follow-up, or abandon it. **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak), building directly on #158's mechanism:**
  - **Built as a thin UI layer over #158's steering queue**, not a second independent state machine — "Talk about it" writes into the same non-blocking queue #158 builds, fired specifically at the confirmation-pause moment, and the paused tool call itself stays held (not silently discarded) until the user picks a resolution.
  - **Auto-approve (#40) interaction**: not actually a design tension — an auto-approved call never shows a confirmation dialog at all, so there's no pause moment for "Talk about it" to attach to; it simply never applies there, same as Approve/Deny don't either.
  - **Relationship to edit-before-execute (#30)**: separate, mutually exclusive resolutions to the same paused call, not competing mechanisms — Edit means "I'll fix the exact call myself," Talk about it means "let the model reinterpret based on my comment." Four possible resolutions to a paused call: Deny / Approve / Edit-then-approve / Talk about it.
  - **Transcript rendering**: reuses #158's own visual treatment for a mid-turn continuation-marked `role: user` note, inserted where the pause happened, with the model's next response following normally in the same turn — no second UI pattern invented for this specifically.
  - Ready to implement, after #158's queue mechanism lands (hard dependency, not just related work).
  - **Implemented.** True to the design: no orchestrator-level state machine, no new backend mechanism at all — `ToolConfirmDialog.svelte` gained a third button ("Talk about it") that reveals an inline textarea; submitting it calls a new `onTalkAboutIt: (comment: string) => void` prop, which `ChatView.svelte` composes from two things that already existed: `onSteer(comment)` (#158's own steering queue) and `onDenyTool()` (the existing deny path, unchanged — same `[Rejected by user]` `tool_result`, #102). The round loop's own natural timing does the rest: the denial resolves this round's tool dispatch, the round loop proceeds to the next round, and drains the queued comment right at its top (#158) — so the model sees the rejection and the reason together, in the right order, with zero new orchestrator code. `showTalkAboutIt`/its draft text reset in the same `$effect` that already resets the rest of the dialog's state on a new `toolUseId`, so a stale draft can never leak into a later confirmation. No dedicated test file exists for this component (matches the established pattern — Svelte components here are live-verified, not unit-tested); full suite 1335/1335 passing (no regression), typecheck clean. **Live-verified end-to-end against a real running Ollama model with a real tool call requiring confirmation** (not mocked): clicked "Talk about it" on a real pending `write_file` confirmation, typed "use goodbye.txt instead," submitted — the first call was denied and the note appeared inline exactly as #158 already renders it, and, strikingly, **the model itself picked up the note on its very next round and made a corrected second `write_file` call targeting `goodbye.txt`** — real proof the full loop (deny → inject → drain → model reasons over both) works exactly as designed, not just that the plumbing fires.
- [x] 158. Let the user add a fresh comment mid-run, Implemented 2026-09-29. while the orchestrator is actively working, not just while a tool confirmation dialog happens to be paused — user request 2026-09-29. Today, once a request is sent the input area is fully frozen (`ChatView.svelte:507`, `disabled={sending}`, with a second guard at `ChatView.svelte:237`, `if (!text || sending) return`; `App.svelte:308`'s `sendMessage` has its own `if (runtime.sending) return`) and only a Stop button shows (`ChatView.svelte:521-525`) until the whole turn ends — the user cannot add anything until then. User's own stated logic: if the user can tell mid-run that the model is heading down a wrong path, they should be able to drop in a correcting comment without waiting for the full turn to finish (a softer, non-destructive alternative to Stop), and the orchestrator should pick that comment up "between step completions" and fold it into context as additional input before continuing — not a full second turn, and design/discuss further before building. Confirmed exactly where this would have to hook in and why it isn't trivial: `runLoop` (`orchestrator.ts:1523-1880`) loops one round per iteration (`while` condition `orchestrator.ts:1548`) over a single shared `conversation` array built once in `runChat` (`register.ts:589-721`) and passed by reference into `orchestrator.run()` (`register.ts:721` → `orchestrator.ts:822`); each round mutates that same array in place (`conversation.push(...)` at `orchestrator.ts:1843`/`1879`) but **the loop never re-reads any external/mutable source between rounds** — no DB re-query, no queue poll — so there is currently no checkpoint at all for injecting new text mid-run. The only two existing places anything external can interrupt an in-flight run are narrower and don't fit: (1) `AbortController`-based Stop (#29) only cancels the in-flight provider stream, it doesn't feed new content in; (2) the per-tool-call `confirmOrAbort` gate (`orchestrator.ts:2001-2011`, backed by the `pendingConfirmations` Promise map, `register.ts:156`/`545-546`/`496-504`) and the round-limit `askToContinue` gate (`orchestrator.ts:2022-2031`, `pendingLimitDecisions`, `register.ts:157`/`559-560`/`506-511`) are the closest existing precedent for "an IPC-fed Promise gate the orchestrator awaits mid-run," but both are scoped to one pending id at one specific pause point, not a general mid-round injection channel — and neither fires while the model is actually mid-stream (`orchestrator.ts:1585-1592`), which has no pause point of any kind today. Also confirmed there's no session-level send lock on the main-process side at all (`ChatSend`, `register.ts:454-488`, unconditionally starts a new `runChat`/`Orchestrator.run` with no in-flight check) — the renderer's `sending` flag is the *only* thing preventing a second concurrent send today, which matters because any fix has to either keep relying on that (a single associated "add a note" affordance while `sending` is true, distinct from a second real send) or introduce real session-level coordination. Overlaps but is distinct from #154: #154 is specifically about the tool-confirmation pause point; this is about steering the *model-call* portion of a round, which #154's fix wouldn't reach. **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak, discussed):**
  - **Checkpoint scope**: between rounds only, not mid-stream or mid-tool-call — matches the user's own "between step completions" framing. A wrong path is visible through what a round's tool calls did, which is exactly the between-rounds boundary; interrupting a model mid-token isn't meaningful, and cancelling a running tool is a separate, harder problem out of scope here.
  - **Mechanism**: NOT the existing `pendingConfirmations`/`pendingLimitDecisions` Promise-gate pattern — those are *blocking* (the orchestrator can't proceed until resolved), and a steering comment must be *non-blocking* (round loop proceeds immediately if nothing's pending). Instead: a simple mutable per-stream/session queue, a new lightweight IPC action (e.g. `ChatSteer`, distinct from `ChatSend`) that appends to it, and a non-blocking drain-and-check at the top of each `runLoop` round iteration. This also avoids the concurrency gap noted above — no second `runChat`/`Orchestrator.run` spins up, so there's no race with the missing session-level send lock.
  - **Presentation to the model, with a critical correction**: the comment still needs to read as a `role: user` block to the model (so it's naturally legible as fresh instruction) — **but it must NOT become a new top-level turn boundary at the persistence/context layer.** `splitIntoTurns` (used by the sliding window, #51) treats every `role: user` message as a fresh turn boundary; if a steering comment landed as an ordinary new user message, `search_history`'s embedding (#52) would index it as its own standalone searchable fragment (context-free, e.g. "no, try the other table" — useless or misleading later), and every turn-scoped mechanism (the sliding window's drop logic, #149's own per-turn tool-result capping, #102's `collapseFailedTurns`) would see a spurious extra turn boundary mid-request — the same class of turn-scoping bug #149 is already about. **Fix**: tag the injected message with a continuation marker (e.g. `partOfTurn`) that `splitIntoTurns` and the `search_history` indexer both check and treat as "still inside the currently-running turn," not the start of a new one. The queue-drain step must write this marker when it pushes into `conversation`, not push a bare user message.
  - **Relationship to #154**: sequence, don't duplicate — build #158's queue as the general substrate first; #154's "Talk about it" becomes a thin UI entry point writing into the same queue at the confirmation-pause moment, not a second independent state machine.
  - **Stop button**: unchanged — stays destructive (abort, discard in-flight work); "add a note" is a separate, additive, non-destructive affordance alongside it, not a replacement.
  - Ready to implement, with the turn-continuation marker treated as a required part of the design, not a follow-up.
  - **Implemented.** New `ContentBlock`/`StreamEvent` type `user_note` (`shared/protocol.ts`) rather than a message-level flag — a DB-schema migration would have been needed for the latter (`insertMessage`'s signature only ever took `content: ContentBlock[]`, no per-message metadata field), while the block-type-union approach needs none (matches #147's own note that this shape of change is architecturally cheap here). The model sees a `user_note` as ordinary text (`normalizeUserNotesForWire`, `context/prepare-context.ts`, applied right before every `provider.stream()` call, both the normal round loop and the `MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS` wrap-up path) — the type distinction is purely for turn-boundary and UI purposes. `isPureToolResult`/`splitIntoTurns` (same file) now also treat a `user_note`-only message as a turn continuation, not a new turn boundary — and the same fix was needed in **two other independent copies of this exact check** found while implementing: `App.svelte`'s `mapMessagesToDisplay` (reload path) and `sessions/export.ts`'s Markdown-export `isPureToolResult` (a third place this convention had been copy-pasted, not just the two `splitIntoTurns` already knew about).
  - **Mechanism**: `pendingSteeringComments: Map<string, string[]>` in `ipc/register.ts`, keyed by `streamId` exactly like the existing `activeStreams`/`pendingConfirmations` maps; a new `ChatSteer` IPC channel appends to it (rejects silently if the streamId isn't active — same "harmless if stale" shape as `ChatCancel`); cleared in `ChatSend`'s own `.finally()` alongside `activeStreams` so nothing leaks. `Orchestrator` gained an optional `drainSteeringComments?: () => string[]` constructor option (defaults absent = no-op, same pattern as every other optional callback there), called non-blockingly at the top of every **top-level-only** round in `runLoop` (never a nested specialist's own rounds — delegation is structurally one level only, and routing a comment down through an active delegate call was explicitly out of scope). A drained comment is pushed onto `conversation` as a `user_note` message and — matching the exact `yield {kind:'stream'}` + `yield {kind:'message'}` pair already used elsewhere in this file for a warning — both streamed live (so the UI shows it immediately) and persisted through the *exact same generic* `insertMessage`/`enqueueMessage` handling every other message already goes through in `register.ts`'s event loop, needing zero new persistence code. Persisted only when actually drained (not at submission time) — a comment submitted but never reached before the run ends is a known, accepted gap for this first pass, called out explicitly in the option's own doc comment rather than left implicit.
  - **UI**: `ChatView.svelte`'s composer, while `sending`, no longer just shows a disabled textarea + Stop — it becomes a real, enabled steering-note input (separate `steerDraft` state, so it can't collide with the next real message's own draft) with its own Enter-to-send handling and a new outlined "add note" button (💬) alongside the existing solid Stop button (⏹) — Stop's own meaning and behavior are completely unchanged. `App.svelte` wires a new `steerGeneration()` (mirrors `stopGeneration()`'s own session-scoping-by-`currentStreamId` exactly) calling `window.sachiv.steerChat`. The live/reload rendering paths both gained a `kind: 'user_note'` `DisplaySegment` (in `App.svelte` and, separately, `ChatView.svelte` — a fourth pre-existing duplicated local type definition found and fixed along the way), rendered as its own accent-colored bordered note, visually distinct from both the assistant's own text and a `warning`.
  - New tests: `prepare-context.test.ts` (a `user_note`-only message stays in the same turn as the round it was injected into; an ordinary new user message still correctly starts a new turn — the marker isn't overbroad; `normalizeUserNotesForWire` converts the type without touching anything else, input left unmutated); `orchestrator.test.ts`, two real integration tests (a drained comment reaches the model's own next request as plain text while the persisted/streamed form keeps the real `user_note` type; a nested specialist's rounds never call `drainSteeringComments`, only the top-level orchestrator's 2 of 4 total rounds do). Full suite 1335/1335 passing, typecheck clean. **Live-verified end-to-end against a real running Ollama model** (`qwen3.5:0.8b-custom`, isolated instance, real UI interaction over CDP — not mocked): the composer correctly swaps to the steering-note input + both buttons the moment a turn starts sending; typing and submitting a real note through the actual textarea makes it appear live in the transcript, correctly positioned right before the next round started ("round 2/20" in the live status line, confirming it landed between rounds as designed, not mid-stream), styled as a distinct accent-bordered "💬 Note added mid-run" box.

Default-tooling guidance for diagrams/scripts, Implemented 2026-09-29. written into the system prompt so agents stop searching for alternatives — user request 2026-09-29: diagrams/schematics should default to matplotlib (Python) or Mermaid.js (markdown); ad-hoc programs should default to Python unless another language is explicitly requested; "create a script" should default to a shell script targeting whatever OS Sachivji is actually running on. All defaults explicitly overridable by naming a different language. Confirmed current state (there is no shared per-role prompt template — `roles.ts` gives each role, e.g. `coding` a.k.a. "Developer" (absorbed the old Terminal-DevOps/Distribution roles, see the roles.ts:70-79 doc comment and `LEGACY_ROLE_LABELS` in `shared/agents.ts:256-266` — TASKS.md's own #99/#28/#153 still say "Terminal-DevOps" from before that merge), its own hand-written prompt string, so any change here touches each role's text individually, not one shared block):
  - **Matplotlib** is already named as the default charting library, but only inside the `coding` role's own prompt (`roles.ts:198-201`) — not in the top-level `SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`agents/system-prompt.ts:17-56`), so it isn't visible to the orchestrator's own routing/planning reasoning, only once a task actually reaches `coding`.
  - **Mermaid.js is never mentioned in any prompt at all** — it exists purely as a renderer-side feature (#62, `renderer/src/lib/markdown.ts` + `mermaid-render.ts`) that auto-renders any ```mermaid fence a model happens to produce; no role is ever told this capability exists or told to prefer it for a diagram request the top-level orchestrator could just answer in text, with no delegation or tool call at all.
  - **No default-language statement exists anywhere**; Python is only the default de facto because it's the one language with first-class tool support (`python_run`, `roles.ts:196-204`) — `shell_execute` is generic and untargeted, so a "write me a script" request has no stated language default to fall back on.
  - **No OS/shell-dialect targeting exists for `shell_execute`** — its tool description (`tools/shell.ts:10-14`) says only that it runs "a shell command" in the workspace root, with no platform framing, so a "write a shell script" request has no way to know whether to target bash or PowerShell/cmd. **A real gap, not just missing prompt text**: `process.platform` is read today (`python-env.ts:22,27-38,53` for venv path branching; `index.ts` for Electron window chrome) but is never exposed to any role/tool — the only place it reaches outside the main process at all is `AppGetInfo`'s `AppInfo.platform` (`ipc/register.ts:249`), which is UI-only, not injected into any prompt. Implementing the shell-script default needs this OS signal actually surfaced to the `coding` role's prompt or as tool-call context, not just a prompt sentence assuming Linux/bash.
  - **`show_media` (#82, `tools/show-media.ts`) is confirmed library-agnostic** — it displays any workspace image file matching a fixed extension list (png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp/svg, 5MB cap), with zero dependency on matplotlib specifically; the matplotlib association is pure prompt convention, so this guidance doesn't need any tool-level change, only prompt text (plus the OS-signal gap above).
  - **Considered and deferred: a dedicated "Diagram Creator" role.** Rejected for now — diagram output isn't one coherent capability domain, it's a text-emission mode (Mermaid, no tool at all) in one case and a Python code-execution mode (matplotlib) in the other, and this project's own role-design principle (mid-redesign on `design_change`, see docs/design-discussion.md) is to gate new roles behind measured usage data rather than speculative capability slicing — there's no usage signal yet since these defaults haven't shipped. Revisit only if real usage shows diagram requests being mis-routed or mishandled after this item lands.
  - **Considered and deferred: a dedicated "Diagram Generator" tool.** Same rejection, at the tool level rather than the role level — user question 2026-09-29. Mermaid needs no tool at all (renderer auto-renders any fence), and matplotlib diagrams are already fully served by the existing generic `python_run` (generate) + `show_media` (display, confirmed library-agnostic) pipeline — a dedicated tool would wrap a pipeline that already works end-to-end, adding schema weight to the per-round tool budget the redesign is actively trying to shrink (~43 tools, ~9.3K tok/round, see [[reference-ctx-md]]) for no new capability. The real gaps are the ones already listed above (no stated default, no OS signal) — a new tool fixes neither.
  - **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak):** split placement — the general defaults (Mermaid-for-diagrams, Python-as-default-language, "script" vs. ad-hoc "program" as two *separate* trigger rules, not one blended default) go in the top-level `SYSTEM_PROMPT`, since the orchestrator itself needs them to decide whether to answer directly (Mermaid needs no delegation) or delegate; matplotlib/shell-execution specifics stay in the `coding` role's own prompt, the only role that actually runs Python/shell. The script-vs-program distinction must survive as two distinct rules in the prompt wording, not get merged into one "default to Python or shell" sentence, or the model has no way to tell which trigger word (a general request vs. the specific word "script") it saw. OS signal: a static field interpolated into the `coding` prompt at `createAgents()` time from `process.platform` (never changes mid-session, not worth a tool round-trip), not a new tool. **Windows: enforce, don't hope** — `shellExecuteTool` should explicitly pass `{ shell: 'powershell.exe' }` to Node's `exec()` on `win32` rather than relying on its default (`cmd.exe`); PowerShell over cmd.exe because it's bundled since Windows 7 (unlike `pwsh.exe`/PowerShell 7, not guaranteed installed) and matches what a modern "write me a Windows script" request expects. Once enforced, the prompt can state "PowerShell" as a flat fact rather than hedging "PowerShell or cmd.exe" — the tool now guarantees which one runs. Actual Windows runtime behavior can't be live-verified from this (Linux) dev machine — verify on CI/Windows once implemented, not a blocker.
  - **Implemented.** `SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`agents/system-prompt.ts`) gained the general defaults as two explicitly separate rules (Mermaid/matplotlib for diagrams; Python for an unlabeled ad-hoc request; the literal word "script" as its own trigger for the native shell, stated as overriding the Python default rather than blending with it). `roles.ts`'s `coding` prompt gained a `SHELL_TARGET_DESCRIPTION` computed once from `process.platform` at `createAgents()` time (never re-checked per-round — a static machine fact) and an explicit "matplotlib is the default" sentence. `tools/shell.ts` now enforces PowerShell on Windows for real: `exec()`'s own shell dispatch is left alone (still `cmd.exe`, which reliably launches an arbitrary program) and the *command string itself* becomes `powershell.exe -NoProfile -NonInteractive -EncodedCommand <base64 UTF-16LE>` — chosen deliberately over a naive `shell: 'powershell.exe'` override, which Node's own docs say would still be invoked with cmd.exe's `/d /s /c` flags PowerShell doesn't understand, silently breaking every call; `-EncodedCommand` also sidesteps all cmd/PowerShell quoting differences a manually quoted `-Command "..."` string would risk. New tests: `system-prompt.test.ts` (Mermaid/matplotlib/Python/the script-word-as-separate-trigger, all asserted directly against the real exported string), two new `roles.test.ts` cases (matplotlib-default sentence present; the OS-target sentence matches this test machine's own real `process.platform`), and `shell.test.ts` gained a platform-independent round-trip test for the `-EncodedCommand` base64/UTF-16LE transform itself (verifiable with certainty from any OS) plus fixes to two existing Windows-branch integration tests that had used cmd.exe-only syntax (`&`, bare `cd`) now incompatible with the PowerShell wrapping — corrected to real PowerShell syntax, though (as already flagged above) unverified against a real Windows machine from this session. `prompt-budget.test.ts` ceilings raised deliberately for the real new prompt content (reason recorded in-file). Full suite 1350/1350 passing, typecheck clean. A live model smoke-test (asking a real running Ollama model to draw a diagram) was attempted but inconclusive — the small local model was still generating after 90s with nothing conclusive to check yet — treated as non-blocking since this is prompt content, verified rigorously at the string level, not application logic with a deterministic pass/fail.

- [ ] 168. Ollama Cloud usage can be wrongly displayed as $0 cost — real bug found 2026-09-29 while implementing #167's provider registration, not part of #167 itself (fixing it properly needs UI prop-chain changes beyond "register a new provider"). Confirmed via a real web search (`docs.ollama.com/api/openai-compatibility`, `ollama.com/blog/cloud-models`), not assumed: Ollama Cloud model names follow the same `name:tag`-shaped convention as local Ollama model ids (e.g. `gemma4:cloud`, `qwen3.5:cloud`, `gpt-oss:120b-cloud`, `glm-5.1:cloud`). `shared/model-metadata.ts`'s `isLocalModelId()` (`:13-15`, regex `/^[\w.-]+:[\w.]+$/`) exists specifically to detect a **free, local** Ollama model by its tag-shaped id and short-circuit both `contextWindowFor()` (`:62`) and `estimateCost()` to `{input:0, output:0}` (`:155`, comment "local compute, genuinely free") — it has no awareness of *provider*, only of model-name shape, and until #167 that shape reliably meant "free local Ollama," so the heuristic was safe. **Checked the regex precisely against real cloud model names, not just eyeballed**: a plain `:cloud` suffix (`gemma4:cloud`, `qwen3.5:cloud`) matches `isLocalModelId` and would be wrongly shown as free — real Ollama Cloud usage the user is actually being billed for. A size-qualified `:<size>-cloud` suffix (`gpt-oss:120b-cloud`) does NOT match (the part after the colon, `[\w.]+`, excludes the hyphen in `120b-cloud`) and instead falls through to the generic "unknown model, cost unknown" path — not wrong, just not accurately priced either. So the bug is real but inconsistent: some cloud model names misfire, others don't, depending on whether their tag happens to contain a hyphen. **Proper fix scope** (why this wasn't just fixed inline as part of #167): `estimateCost`/`contextWindowFor` (`shared/model-metadata.ts`) are called from `ContextMeter.svelte:21`/`:18` (with only `model`, no provider id today), `orchestrator.ts:954`, and `prepare-context.ts:423` — none of these call sites currently have a `providerId` to pass, so a correct fix needs a new optional `providerId` param threaded through the shared functions (skip the local-free heuristic when it's `'ollama-cloud'`) **and** `ContextMeter.svelte`'s props extended to receive and pass it from whichever parent renders it — real, if modest, additional plumbing, not a one-line change. Do not implement until picked up alongside real Ollama Cloud usage testing (no fabricated pricing table needed — cloud model costs can stay "unknown" once correctly not-misidentified-as-free, per #47's existing "unknown cost" convention). **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.4.0, together with the Ollama block (#214).**

- [ ] 191. Reasoning-loop breaker for local models — found 2026-10-08 in manual test 8.2 (Prompt A), session "I need you to create a word document that shows bar charts for top 10 customers…" on `ollama:gpt-oss-custom`. **Not started; analysis item, decide whether to build.** **What happened:** the Data Access step had already run its query correctly (30 rows), then the model's *thinking* degenerated into one sentence repeated about 100 times ("We can produce a .docx by using a tool? Not available.", 9.7K characters) and the round took about 2 minutes. It ended with an apology instead of a result ("I don't have the capability to create Word documents"), which the orchestrator accepted as a finished step. Nothing watches for repeated text: the existing recovery paths cover an *empty* reply (`MAX_EMPTY_RESPONSE_RETRIES`), a truncated tool call, a round timeout and repeated failing tool calls, but not a stream that is full of output and going nowhere. **Purpose:** cut the wasted time and tokens on a local reasoning model that falls into a loop, and stop a garbage reply being recorded as a step result. **Idea:** while a round streams, watch the accumulated thinking and text; when the same sentence (normalised) repeats N times in a row, or a long tail is a repeat of the text before it, abort that round (the round already has an abort controller) and retry once with a short nudge ("you were repeating yourself; do your step now"), then fail the step with a clear message if it loops again. Counts as an enforce-in-code rule, not a prompt rule, since the model is the thing that is stuck. **Questions to settle before building:** how to detect a loop without cutting off legitimate repetition (a list of similar items, a table, code); the threshold (repeat count / minimum length) and whether it differs for thinking vs visible text; whether to abort the stream or let it finish and discard it (aborting saves the time, which is the point); how the retry avoids the same loop (temperature change, `think` off where the provider allows it, a trimmed conversation); where it lives (`runLoop` stream handling, shared by the top level and every specialist) and how it interacts with the round timeout and the empty-response retry; and how to test it (a mock provider that streams a repeated sentence). **Evidence to gather first:** how often this happens across sessions and models (the stored `thinking` blocks are enough to count), and whether it only happens on gpt-oss. The same run on Gemini (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) finished in about 30 seconds with no loop, so this may only be worth doing if local models are a supported path. **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.5.0 (detail). The two split-off candidates (keep partial reasoning when a turn is stopped; stop the router re-verifying a delegate's files) are handled together with this item in 0.5.0.**
  - **Second occurrence, 2026-10-10 (manual test 11d.6/11d.7, session `bae9a936-…`, `ollama:gpt-oss-custom`, full router).** "Write a script that lists the path from current environment": the Developer wrote and ran `code/scripts/print_path.sh` correctly and returned a clean answer at 11:47:43. The router's next round then reasoned "we need to verify the file exists", called `read_file` on it (the file was fine), and its following round produced no saved reply for 2 min 19 s until the user pressed Stop (stored only as "stopped before producing a response", so the reasoning text of that last round is not in the database). Two things to look at: (1) the router re-checks a delegate's finished work with its own tool call, a wasted round that invites more reasoning; consider telling the router in code (as the plan-step results already do with "verified by the app") that a direct delegate's files were created and checked, so it should pass the answer on; (2) the reasoning-loop breaker above would have cut the last round. Streamed reasoning that is cut off by Stop is not saved, so a loop like this can only be diagnosed while it is running; consider keeping the partial reasoning text when a turn is stopped.
- [ ] 192. Keep each plan step to its own job — found 2026-10-08 in the same session as #191. **Not started; analysis item, decide whether to build.** **What happened:** plan step 2 (Data Access) was worded only as "query the Northwind database … returning a structured dataset", yet the model reasoned about the *whole* request (build the Word document, no python available to it, no docx tool) because the original user message is still in its conversation. That produced the loop in #191 and a reply about Word documents from a role that has no document tools. A later step (Developer) was meant to do that part. **Purpose:** stop a specialist drifting into steps that belong to other roles, which wastes rounds and can produce misleading results ("I can't create Word documents") that later steps and the final answer then have to ignore. **Idea:** when `executePlan` hands a step to a specialist, append a fixed line to its task naming the boundary, for example "Do only this step. The remaining steps (<role: short description>, …) are done afterwards by other specialists; do not attempt, plan or comment on them." Built from the plan itself, so it is exact for each request and costs one short sentence per step. **Related, not yet an item:** when a step fails and the top-level retries, the retry started again from scratch (a fresh data query three times in total, plus `describe_capabilities`, plus a saved query that returned the overall top 10 instead of per year) even though the first query's result was already saved under `.sachiv-handoff/…/01-db_execute_sql.json`. Pointing the recovery at the existing handoff files ("read these first, do not query again") would address that; check how the final round is built before deciding. **Questions to settle before building:** whether the extra line measurably changes behaviour on gpt-oss-class models (a prompt line is only a suggestion to a weak model; compare runs with and without it, using tool-call and round counts rather than impressions); whether it is needed at all on cloud models, which did not drift in the Gemini run; the effect on the prompt-size ceilings in `prompt-budget.test.ts`; whether the boundary should also be enforced in code by restricting the tools a step may call (already true per role) and by treating a step result that talks about other roles' work as suspect; and where the user's original request should appear in a step's conversation (full text vs only the step's own task) since that is the root of the drift. **Evidence to gather first:** replay a few multi-step plans on a local model and count how often a step's thinking mentions work from a later step. **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.5.0 (detail), with #191; see also #226.**
