- [ ] 195. LLM wiki (`/Users/deepak/Lab/llmwiki`, a separate project) search fails on queries containing a hyphenated number — found 2026-10-09 in the IEC 61400-26 session (`mcp__llmwiki-docs__search`, query `IEC 61400-26 availability loss category taxonomy`) which returned `Error executing tool search: no such column: 26`. **Root cause reproduced** with plain SQLite FTS5: the user's query goes to `chunks_fts MATCH ?` unescaped (`mcp/vaultfs/sqlite.py:498`, and the same pattern in `api/infra/db/sqlite.py:755`), so FTS5 reads `-26` as a column filter / NOT operator and any query like `IEC 61400-26 availability` errors, while `IEC 61400` works and the phrase-quoted form `"IEC 61400-26" availability` returns the row. Any hyphenated standard or part number (ISO-9001, IEC 61400-26, EN-590) will hit it. **Fix belongs in the LLM wiki, not Sachivji:** build the MATCH expression from the query's tokens, each double-quoted with embedded quotes doubled (so every term is literal; join with spaces for AND, or OR for a looser search), instead of passing the raw string; add a test with a hyphenated-number query. **Effect in Sachivji today:** the MCP Operator saw the error, simplified its query and recovered, so the cost was a wasted call and a slower answer, but a weaker model may give up and report "nothing found". No Sachivji-side change planned; if the wiki cannot be fixed soon, a hint in the MCP Operator prompt to avoid hyphenated numbers in search queries is the fallback.
- [ ] 196. A PDF reader: convert a PDF to text/Markdown once, then read it in sections — user request 2026-10-09. **Not started; engine choice open.** Two needs: (1) web research keeps hitting PDFs (standards previews, white papers, papers) and the app can only say it cannot read them — in the IEC 61400-26 session four `parse_html` calls on PDF URLs each returned "is a PDF, not a web page or text data, and cannot be read here" (`tools/data-formats/source.ts` refuses them on purpose: a large PDF fed to the HTML parser stalled the app), so the answer was built from search snippets and the model had to say it could not read the standard; (2) the user may hand the app a PDF and ask questions about it — today `read_file` refuses `.pdf` and the only route is the Developer writing a one-off Python script. **Wanted:** a tool that takes a PDF (a workspace path, or a URL) and does the conversion and the content operations itself, so a large file never bloats the context. Sketch: `pdf_open` converts the file to Markdown once, stores it at a temporary location keyed to the session (not in the user-visible workspace, cleaned up with the session or after a TTL), and returns only a small summary — title, page count, size, headings/outline with page numbers, whether a text layer exists; `pdf_read` returns one section, page range or heading at a time with a per-call size cap and says plainly when it truncated; `pdf_search` finds a term or phrase and returns matches with page numbers and short snippets (so "what does it say about X" costs a few hundred tokens, not the document). A repeat open of the same file/URL reuses the conversion. **Decisions for then:** (a) engine — **researched and trialled 2026-10-09 (scratch folder, not the project; `npm view` + two real PDFs, run under Node 24, not yet inside Electron 44).** Leading candidate: **`@llamaindex/liteparse`** (Apache-2.0, v2.15, LlamaIndex) — a napi-rs native addon over PDFium with prebuilt binaries for darwin arm64/x64, win32 x64/arm64 and linux x64/arm64 (about 25 MB per platform; napi is ABI-stable, so Electron-compatible in principle, needs `asarUnpack`), per-page results, `targetPages`, `maxPages`, Markdown output with real tables/lists/headings/links, a worker pool with a hard per-parse timeout (answers the "a bad PDF must not stall the app" concern), password support, and **built-in Tesseract OCR** (so it could also close most of #183 for scanned PDFs: OCR of an image-only slide worked, about 3 s/page). Trial: a 17-page Sandia slide deck parsed in 0.56 s and a 15-page two-column paper in 0.05 s; its Markdown kept the IEC 61400-26 state table and the paper's results table as real `|` tables (header rows are sometimes mangled). Caveats: young project ("reconstruction quality varies with document complexity", its own benchmark score is modest); OCR downloads `eng.traineddata` (15 MB) on first use into the user's profile, so an offline install must bundle it and pass `tessdataPath`; each OS needs its own optional binary package at build time (the three-platform CI already builds per OS). **Fallback / pure-JS option: `unpdf`** (MIT, 2.1 MB, serverless pdf.js build, text per page, positioned items, links, no native code) — alone it gives plain text only; **`@opendocsg/pdf2md`** (MIT, built on unpdf) adds heading detection from font size and `<!-- PAGE_BREAK -->` markers but flattened both test tables into one run-on line and marks bullets as headings, so it is the weaker of the two. **Ruled out: `mupdf` (AGPL-3.0) and, in Python, `pymupdf4llm`/PyMuPDF (AGPL) — an earlier version of this item suggested `pymupdf4llm`; its licence does not fit a closed-source app.** Not worth evaluating further: `pdf-parse` (wraps pdf.js, needs `@napi-rs/canvas`), `pdf2json`/`pdfreader` (positions only, no layout or Markdown), `officeparser` (31 MB, pulls pdf.js and tesseract.js), `markitdown-ts` (jsdom/ai SDK baggage). Python-side permissive options if the JS route fails: `pdfminer.six`/`pdfplumber`/`pypdf` (MIT/BSD) — text and tables, no Markdown, so more code of our own. **Next step before building:** run LiteParse inside a throwaway Electron copy (native load from `app.asar.unpacked`, worker pool as a child process, a signed/notarised macOS build and Windows) — if that fails, fall back to `unpdf` plus our own section/heading logic; (b) URLs — download with a size cap, sniff `%PDF-` rather than trust the Content-Type, refuse private/loopback hosts and redirects to them, same confirmation and link-registry rules as `download_image` (#184); (c) scanned PDFs have no text layer — detect that and say so instead of returning empty text; it is the same gap as #183 (OCR), so do not solve it here but link the two; (d) which roles own it (Research for web PDFs, plus Developer, Data Processor and Document Creator for local files; the router core stays small) and the prompt-budget ceilings, which will need a deliberate raise; (e) the extracted text is untrusted web/third-party content — treat it as data, like MCP wiki text, never as instructions. **Knock-on changes:** the `parse_html` refusal message should name the new tool; Research's completion check lists unread search-result URLs and asks the model to "open these now with parse_html" — it must route PDF links to the new tool instead of asking for something that can only fail; the internal conversion files must never be named in answers (see `stripInternalPaths`, added 2026-10-09). **Tests:** generate small PDFs in the test (text, two columns, a table, image-only) rather than committing binaries; the 5 MB-and-up case for the section reader; the scanned case; URL guards. **Live check:** the IEC 61400-26 question again (the standards preview PDFs should now be read), and a local PDF with "what does section 3 say about X" in a throwaway Electron copy. **Integration with web research (added 2026-10-09, user request):** the model should not have to notice a PDF and pick a different tool. `parse_html` (and `read_file` on a `.pdf`) should hand a PDF to the same converter itself: instead of today's refusal it fetches the file (with the guards in (b)), converts it, and returns the `pdf_open` summary plus the first section, with the document handle and a one-line pointer to `pdf_read` for the rest — so every existing path that already opens search-result links, including Research's completion check, now works on PDFs unchanged. `web_search` itself stays a search: it only returns links and snippets, and downloading PDFs from inside a search would make every search slow and costly; when a result is a PDF, the result list should mark it as `[PDF]` so the model knows a read is worthwhile. **Keep the tool surface small** (every tool schema costs prompt tokens on each request, see `prompt-budget.test.ts`): two tools, not three — `pdf_open` and `pdf_read`, where `pdf_read` takes a page range, a heading, or a `query` (search mode returning page numbers and snippets). **Cost to plan for:** converting a 100+ page PDF takes seconds, so it needs a progress status and the per-turn deadline must be respected; the conversion must run off the main thread/in a child process so a bad PDF cannot stall the app (that is why the refusal exists today). Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): 0.4.0, size L, spike first (LiteParse in Electron 44 on Windows and macOS, fallback `unpdf`); two tools, `parse_html`/`read_file` hand-off, `[PDF]` in search results. Design addition: text goes to a temporary location and images/graphs/sketches in the PDF to a sub-folder of it, referred to while reading. A PDF the user directs to download goes to `downloads/` (#228); one opened only for reading stays transient.
- [ ] 197. Build a deck from the user's own PowerPoint template (its masters, layouts, logos, fonts) — user request 2026-10-09. **Planned, not started; engine choice to confirm.** Found when "make a presentation on AI agents, use the template in the workspace" produced a plain deck: the router found `templates/JBS_Presentation_Template.pptx`, read its colours and fonts, and said so honestly in its answer ("does not use your JBS template"), because the deck tools (`pptx_start_deck`/`pptx_compose_slide`, PptxGenJS) can only draw slides from scratch at 10 x 5.625 in; none can load a template. (`office_fill_template` fills `{placeholders}` in a document; it does not add slides from layouts.) The template made in that session (a real brand deck: 13.33 x 7.5 in, 12 layouts with oddly numbered placeholders, a "Blank" with 20 body placeholders, 148 decorative shapes on the title layout) shows what real templates look like. Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): 0.5.0 (detail), one templates family with #198 and #85; Python required is acceptable; layouts first, sample slides later.
  - **Shape of the feature.** `pptx_start_deck` takes an optional `template` path (under `templates/`, .pptx or .potx): the deck starts as a copy of the template with its sample slides removed, and the result lists the template's layouts compactly (name, and for each placeholder its index, type, size). `pptx_compose_slide` then takes `layout` (a layout name) and `placeholders` (`{ "<idx>": text | [bullets] | { image: "resources/x.jpg" } }`) and may still carry free `elements` (picture, text box, shape, table) placed in the template's own slide size, not 10 x 5.625. `pptx_save_deck` writes the .pptx. The Page Creator gets the layout list from the start result and the brief says which layout each slide uses; without a `template` nothing changes.
  - **Engine (to confirm).** Recommended: python-pptx run through the managed venv by a small bundled helper script, one call per tool (the working deck lives as a file in `tmp/`, reloaded and saved each call, so there is no state to lose). It is the mature library for layouts and placeholders and its files open in PowerPoint (we just spent a session on repair prompts from PptxGenJS output, so hand-written OOXML is the risk to avoid). Cost: python-pptx is not in the managed base packages (`runtime/workspace-runtime.ts`), so add it (pinned) and, for workspaces that already have a venv, install it on first use; template decks need Python on, and say so clearly when it is off. Alternatives weighed: **pptx-automizer** (JS, MIT) works from a template's existing slides, not its layouts, and pulls an older PptxGenJS; **our own JSZip/XML code** needs no dependency but means maintaining slide, relationship and content-type plumbing ourselves.
  - **What real templates need.** Remove placeholders the slide leaves empty (otherwise PowerPoint shows "Click to add text"); pick a layout by name, with a fallback when names repeat ("Blank" twice) or placeholders have no usable type; warn when text is far longer than its placeholder (no rendering is available here to see overflow); keep the template's fonts and colours (do not override with design-system values); copy pictures in with the same crop-to-fill the scratch decks use; slide numbers, dates and footers follow the layout.
  - **Also.** Make `python_run` keep refusing a from-scratch python-pptx deck but point to this flow for template work (its guard was loosened 2026-10-09 so a template can be derived from a finished deck); a test fixture template (layouts with placeholders, built with PptxGenJS masters) so tests do not need the user's file; tests skip when python-pptx is unavailable, as the Python tools already do. Verify live against the JBS template, opening the result in PowerPoint.
  - **Open questions for the user.** Is requiring Python acceptable for template decks? Should the sample slides of a template stay available as "slide types" (copy slide 3 of the template), or only its layouts?
- [ ] 198. Template generation from an existing document (`office_extract_template` + `office_fill_template`): research it and do it properly — user verdict 2026-10-10 after manual test 11b.4 ("some improvement, but far from acceptable"). **Research first, then design; not started.** Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): 0.5.0 (detail), templates family with #197 and #85; research first.
  - **Test case.** Turn `Jonathan Whitaker.docx` (a 98-paragraph resume, no tables) into a template, on claude-haiku-5-5 with the slim router. Session in `sachiv.db` (`9afba6a0-…`). What happened: `detect` found 2 candidates; the model applied 8 replacements and kept every project description, the summary and skills as fixed text; the name and employer did not match (text split over runs); the result was ~95% the original resume.
  - **Done 2026-10-10 (uncommitted at the time of writing, tests green, not seen live):** `apply` matches across the runs of a paragraph; the result reports paragraphs/cells still holding original text; tool description and Document Creator prompt say a template holds none of the source's own content and ask for a section-by-section list before `apply`.
  - **Still wrong / to research.** (1) Repeating blocks (jobs, projects, education, skills) need loops `{#jobs}…{/jobs}`; `apply` is plain string replacement and cannot collapse repeated paragraphs into one loop. (2) Whole-paragraph placeholders (`{summary}`) are clumsy: the model must pass the exact paragraph text. A paragraph-index or paragraph-range form is likely needed. (3) Deciding what is variable is judgement, and a weak model keeps too much; consider a structure-first pass (list the document's sections/paragraph kinds with indexes, the model marks each as fixed / field / loop) instead of free-form string replacement. (4) Only .docx/.pptx/.xlsx text runs are touched: headers/footers, text boxes, tables, tracked changes, fields and hyperlinks need checking. (5) A verification step: fill the template with sample data and compare, or render it, so a bad template is caught before the user sees it. (6) What do good products do (Word content controls, docxtemplater tags, mail merge, Google Docs templates)? Survey them and pick the placeholder syntax and loop model on that basis. (7) Does the flow suit a plan step (no chance to confirm with the user)? (8) Retest on the full router and on a stronger model; the first test was slim router + Haiku.
  - **Related.** #197 (decks from the user's own template) shares the template questions; decide the two together.

## Tools
- [x] 31. Tool registry + `Tool` interface wired into the chat loop (moves to the orchestrator when #27 lands)
- [x] 32. Web search tool — since revisited: after a real live failure traced through the session DB (a fabricated, 404ing article URL — see #71's follow-up note), investigated replacing DuckDuckGo's Instant Answer API with a real ranked-results search (a direct scrape of DuckDuckGo's own HTML results page, or Google's) and found neither viable — both actively rate-limit/anti-bot-block automated access (confirmed empirically: DuckDuckGo's `/html/` and `/lite/` pages returned a JS challenge page after just a handful of requests, escalating into a sustained block that didn't clear on retry; this is unrelated to curl vs. a real HTTP client — a plain `fetch()` hit the identical block). A real search API (e.g. Brave Search) was considered and explicitly declined. Improved within the Instant Answer API's real limits instead: surfaces `Heading` (so the model can judge whether DuckDuckGo matched the *right* sense of an ambiguous query — confirmed live it can confidently match the wrong one, e.g. "Python" resolving to a Cold War-era UK nuclear programme, not the language), `Definition`/`DefinitionURL`, and flattens a disambiguation-style grouped `RelatedTopics` shape it previously missed. Also fixed a real accuracy bug: `RelatedTopics[].FirstURL` was presented as a link, but it's a `duckduckgo.com/c/...` internal category page — confirmed live it sits behind the identical anti-bot wall (a `curl -I` on one returned the same 202 challenge), so it was never actually a fetchable source; those entries now show by name only, explicitly labeled not fetchable.

Generic API access tool (Research role) — connections configured in Settings > External APIs (name, base URL, auth: none/API key header/bearer/basic, optional OpenAPI/Swagger spec URL), same keychain-backed secret storage as data connections/MCP tokens. `api_list_connections` (cheap routing hint), `api_introspect` (fetches and parses an OpenAPI/Swagger spec — the configured specUrl, or a probe of common well-known paths — into a condensed endpoint/parameter list, caching a summary), `api_call` (method/path/query/headers/body, `mutates` computed per call from the HTTP method — GET/HEAD/OPTIONS run immediately, anything else needs confirmation; `paginate:true` follows a Link `rel="next"` header or a common "next"-style JSON field across pages up to a capped `maxPages`), `api_check_status` (bounded polling of an async job — a 202 response or a pending-status-plus-id body — within one call; still-pending returns the same statusUrl to retry on a later turn rather than blocking indefinitely). Live-verified against a real public API (JSONPlaceholder): the Research agent called `api_call` for real and correctly reported real response data; pagination/async-job detection/introspection are covered by mocked unit tests only, not a second live network round-trip.
- [x] 71. Structured data-processing tools: `parse_html` (Research), `parse_csv`/`parse_json`/`parse_xml` (Data) — so an agent processes a raw/large document itself instead of pulling it into its own context, prompted directly by a real live failure (an agent `curl`'d a raw HTML page and the local model's own request timed out trying to digest it). Each accepts exactly one of `url` (fetched directly, so raw content never has to enter the model's context), `filePath` (workspace-relative), or `text` (inline). `parse_html` extracts reader-mode article text via `@mozilla/readability`+`jsdom`, falling back to stripped plain text with an explicit note when Readability finds nothing (confirmed empirically: it returns null only for genuinely empty content, not merely sparse content). `parse_csv`/`parse_json`/`parse_xml` default to a condensed structure summary; support a query action (CSV: `where`/`columns`/`limit` mirroring the mechanical DB tools' shape; JSON/XML: a minimal dot/bracket/`[*]`-wildcard path expression, XML via `fast-xml-parser` converted to the same value shape JSON queries against); and, CSV/JSON only, a single `append` action (the only mutating one) — deliberately no update/delete for any format, and no write facility at all for XML. Live-verified for real against a live Wikipedia page (not mocked): clean extracted article text, no script/style markup, correctly capped at the 10K-char limit.

  **Follow-up, same day — URL fabrication hardened from advisory to enforced, per explicit user request ("I don't want the model or the agent to speculate the URL... if you need to harden it, do it").** A prompt-only fix (never guess an article URL) was already tried once and didn't reliably hold — found live again in the user's own real session (`sqlite3`-queried, not just observed in the UI): the model fabricated a *variant* of a real article's URL (a real slug it had already seen, with an invented numeric ID) rather than a wholly invented one, a subtler case a prompt alone can't reliably catch. Added `tools/data-formats/link-registry.ts`: a per-session, in-memory map of every URL `parse_html` has actually fetched or extracted as a real link, per origin. `parse_html` now checks this *before* fetching — a `url` whose origin already has real links on file must be the bare root or an exact match to one of them, or the call is rejected outright with a clear error, never reaching the network. The very first fetch to a brand-new origin stays unrestricted (no way to distinguish a model-invented URL from one the user typed directly at that point — blocking it would break the legitimate "here's a URL, check it" case). Live-verified by replaying the user's own real session against the live site: the real cold-start 404 behaves identically, the real root fetch finds the same real article link, a fabricated variant of that same real article's URL is now rejected before any network call, and the genuine real link still works.
- [x] 83. Workspace file organization by kind — per explicit user request 2026-09-23 ("All code generated by the app tool should be in a folder called 'code' in workspace. All documents... in the 'documents' folder... images, videos, icons, etc. in the 'resources' folder and rest could be in the root."). **Implemented 2026-09-23.**
  - Deterministic, code-enforced placement — matching this app's own general preference for a path computed in code over a convention only asked of the model in a prompt (same reasoning as the round-timeout/deadline work in #80). New `tools/workspace-layout.ts`: `classifyManagedFolder(path)` maps an extension to `code`/`documents`/`resources`/`null`, and `resolveNewFilePath(workspaceGuard, relPath)` is the one function every file-creating tool now routes a model-given path through instead of calling `assertWithinWorkspace` directly.
  - Wired into every tool that creates a new file of a classified kind: `write_file`, `office_create_document`, `xlsx_write`, `office_fill_template`'s output (not its template input, which is read-only), and `pptx_save_deck` (which also, for free, gives the #81 turn-end deck auto-save the same `documents/` placement without any separate special-casing). Each tool's result message now names the real relative path actually used, since it may differ from what was asked for.
  - Never redirects a path that already exists on disk — `xlsx_write`'s append mode, `write_file` overwriting a file that already lives somewhere else, and any other "modify what's already there" case land on the file that's genuinely there, not a silently-created second copy elsewhere because of its extension. Never double-nests a path the model already placed explicitly under a managed folder (e.g. `code/scripts/foo.py` stays put). Two pre-existing special-purpose folders — `templates/` (user-authored `office_fill_template` inputs) and `.sachiv-clones` (`git_clone_repo`'s fixed clone destination, `CLONES_DIR` in `git.ts`) — are excluded from reclassification entirely, so a file placed there is never redirected just because its extension matches a managed kind; this was found as a real test failure (`office_fill_template`'s own test fixtures at `templates/*.docx` were getting silently redirected into `documents/templates/*.docx`, breaking the tool's literal-path read of its own template) before being fixed.
  - Read-only tools (`read_file`, `list_directory`, `office_read_document`, `xlsx_read`, `office_fill_template`'s `templatePath`, an image element's source path in a deck, an email's attachment path) deliberately left untouched — they resolve an existing path, not a new one. CSV/JSON/XML (`data-formats/source.ts`) left out of scope entirely — none of those extensions fall into any of the three classified sets, so routing them through the resolver would be a no-op.
  - New `tools/workspace-layout.test.ts` unit-tests `classifyManagedFolder`/`resolveNewFilePath` directly (extension classification, no-double-nest, existing-file-wins, `templates/`/`.sachiv-clones` exclusion, workspace-boundary enforcement), on top of updated expectations in every affected tool's own test file. 702/702 full suite passing, typecheck clean (main + web).

  **Follow-up, same day, a real bug found from an actual user session, not hypothetical**: `parse_html`'s reader-mode extraction discards every `<a href>` — fine for a single article, actively harmful for a listing/topic/search-results page, whose whole value *is* its links. Traced from a real session (`sqlite3` query against the persisted `messages` table, not just the rendered UI) where the model, needing a specific article after fetching a topic page, had nothing but a headline string to go on and fabricated a plausible-looking article URL with an invented ID — a 404, twice, against two different real news sites. Fixed: `parse_html` now also extracts and lists the page's real links (absolute http(s) only, deduped, capped at 30, only ones with real anchor text) from the Readability-selected content (or the whole page on the fallback path), and both the tool description and the Research role's system prompt now explicitly say never to construct/guess an article URL. Also fixed a smaller, related robustness gap in the same pass: `resolveSource` (shared by all 4 data-format tools) now treats an empty string the same as "not provided" for `url`/`filePath`/`text` — a model that fills in every schema property rather than omitting unused ones (confirmed live: gpt-oss passed all three, two as `""`) was getting a confusing "3 given" rejection for what was, in intent, one real argument. Live-verified against the exact real page and headline from the original failure: the real link list now includes the exact article the user needed, with a genuine `articleshow` ID that resolves 200, not a fabricated one.

- [x] 73. Office document tools: read/create `.docx`/`.pptx` files, read/modify/create `.xlsx` files (Coding role — settled during implementation, no natural Data-role fit). **Implemented 2026-09-21** (`src/main/tools/office-docs/`): `office_read_document` (mammoth for docx, a custom jszip+fast-xml-parser `<a:t>`-run extractor for pptx), `office_create_document` (the `docx`/`pptxgenjs` packages, dispatched by output extension), `office_fill_template` (docxtemplater+pizzip, `inspect`/`fill` actions), `xlsx_read`/`xlsx_write` (exceljs, genuine load-edit-save). New deps: `mammoth`, `jszip`, `docx`, `pptxgenjs`, `exceljs`, `docxtemplater`, `pizzip` — all real, current versions checked via `npm view` before installing. **Real npm-audit findings fixed via `package.json` overrides, not downgrades** (same pattern as the icon-gen/sharp precedent): `pptxgenjs`'s nested `image-size` had 2 high-severity DoS advisories (confirmed the vulnerable code path is dead/commented-out in pptxgenjs's own build, so the override carries zero functional risk) and `exceljs`'s nested `uuid` had a moderate advisory — both overridden to patched majors (`image-size@^2.0.4`, `uuid@^11.1.1`); `npm audit` is clean (0 vulnerabilities). **Registered on the Coding role only** (added to its tool list and system prompt alongside `read_file`/`write_file`). 18 new tests, all real round-trips through the actual libraries (create → read back both docx and pptx; create a template with a live `{placeholder}` → inspect → fill → read the result back and confirm substitution, for both docx and pptx; xlsx write → read, plus append vs. overwrite modes proving the real load-edit-save path) — not mocked. One real bug caught and fixed by the tests, not by review: `office_fill_template`'s error handling only wrapped `doc.render()` in try/catch, but a malformed template (e.g. an unclosed `{#loop}`) actually throws from `new Docxtemplater(...)`'s constructor (it compiles/parses eagerly) — the construction call is now inside the same try/catch. Also set `errorLogging: false` on the Docxtemplater instance, since it otherwise dumps a raw JSON error blob to the console by default and this app surfaces the same information through its own error message. Full suite: 568/568 passing, typecheck clean (verified via `npm run typecheck` and `npm test`, not just the new files' own tests). **Live-verified 2026-09-21** against a real running instance (`npm run build` + `electron . --remote-debugging-port=9222`, driven via CDP, forced to the Coding role, real local Ollama `gpt-oss-custom:latest`, real tool-confirmation dialogs auto-approved): a real chat turn created `report.docx` via `office_create_document` and read it back via `office_read_document`, getting back the exact title/heading/paragraph text; a real turn wrote `data.xlsx` via `xlsx_write` and read the exact rows back via `xlsx_read`; a real turn created a `.pptx` and read back `Slide 1: ... / Slide 2: Overview Point A Point B` correctly. **One real, separate finding along the way, not a tool defect**: on one of the two pptx attempts, the local model's final answer *fabricated* a plausible-looking "I called both tools and got this result" response without actually invoking either tool (the event log showed zero tool calls that turn) — a known class of local-model unreliability already documented elsewhere in this file (narrating instead of acting), not a bug in `office_create_document`/`office_read_document` themselves; a retry of the identical scenario worked correctly with real tool calls both times. `office_fill_template` was not live-chat-tested (only unit-tested with real docxtemplater round-trips) — lower priority given its thorough non-mocked test coverage already. Test workspace files (`test-verify/`) cleaned up afterward; the verification Electron instance was killed, not left running. **Design settled 2026-09-17, revised 2026-09-17 evening — scope deliberately narrowed:**
  - **`.xlsx`**: `exceljs` handles the full read/modify/create lifecycle natively (loads a real existing workbook, edits it, re-saves) — no gap here, unaffected by the scope change below.
  - **`.docx`/`.pptx`: modifying an existing file is explicitly OUT of scope — dropped, not deferred.** Neither `docx` nor `pptxgenjs` can load an existing file, so the only way to "modify" one was always an extract → modify → regenerate flow — a content *rebuild*, not a true in-place edit. User's own call after this was flagged honestly as lossy (a document with custom styles, exact table formatting, a branded template, or precise slide layout/theme would come back looking different, not identical): not worth the real implementation cost (a docx extractor, a hand-built pptx extractor, transform logic, rebuild logic) for a result that would routinely disappoint someone expecting their actual document edited in place.
  - **What stays in scope for `.docx`/`.pptx`**: reading an existing file's content (via `mammoth` for docx → HTML/text; a small custom pptx-text extractor via `jszip` + `fast-xml-parser`, pulling `<a:t>` runs per slide — no rebuild involved, so no fidelity risk) and creating a brand-new file from scratch via `docx`/`pptxgenjs`.
  - **When a user asks to edit an existing `.docx`/`.pptx`**: say plainly that in-place editing isn't supported for this format, and offer to create a new document instead — reading the original's content first (via the read path above) to work from, if that's useful context, rather than silently attempting a lossy rebuild and calling it an edit.
  - **User-supplied templates, added 2026-09-17 evening (user's own suggestion) — a real, non-lossy way back into "editing," just scoped narrowly.** `docxtemplater` (MIT, free core; `pizzip` as its zip dependency) generates a `.docx` *or* `.pptx` from a template containing `{placeholder}`/`{#loop}...{/loop}`/condition markers, substituting directly in the template's own XML rather than rebuilding from extracted content — the original styling/branding/layout survives exactly, unlike the extract→rebuild approach dropped above. Deliberately different in kind, not a contradiction of that decision: this only works for a file *deliberately authored as a template* with known merge fields, not an arbitrary existing document someone wants tweaked.
    - **Storage**: a `templates/` subfolder inside the existing workspace root (confirmed with the user over a Settings-managed store) — no new storage mechanism, no new Settings UI; the user drops template files there and references them by name/path like any other workspace file.
    - **New tool, name confirmed 2026-09-19: `office_fill_template`**: template path + a data object (with array fields for any `{#loop}` sections) → substituted output written to a new file. Before filling, reuses the same text-extraction already planned for the read path above (mammoth/pptx-text-extractor) to scan the template for its `{...}` placeholders, so the agent knows what data it needs rather than guessing blind.
    - **Real limitation to flag honestly, not discover later**: the free/MIT core covers text substitution, loops, and conditions only — inserting an image into a template (e.g. a logo placeholder) requires docxtemplater's paid Image module. Out of scope unless explicitly requested later.
  - **2026-09-22 — real live bug: `office_create_document`'s docx output showed literal markdown, not real Word formatting**, user-reported ("the tables should look proper, the bold and italic should look proper, the text should not look like markdown"). Root cause: every paragraph/bullet was built from one un-parsed string, so `**bold**`, `- bullets`, `1. numbered lists`, and `| pipe tables |` — exactly how the model writes prose, regardless of this tool's plain-string schema — showed up as literal characters in the actual `.docx` (confirmed against the real generated `document.xml`, not just "a file exists"). Fixed: inline `**bold**`/`*italic*`/`` `code` `` now parse into real docx TextRuns everywhere (title, headings, paragraphs, bullets, table cells); a new `table` field (headers + rows) renders as a real Word table with a shaded header row, and a new `numbers` field gives real ordered-list formatting alongside the existing `bullets`; and, defensively, a markdown table or bullet/numbered list written into a plain `paragraphs` string anyway (the actual shape of the live failure) is auto-detected and converted to the real construct too. 4 new regression tests verified against real generated XML. Same investigation also added a disambiguating note to this tool's own description pointing a presentation request at `pptx_start_deck`/`page-creator` instead — see #78's own 2026-09-22 note for that half.
- [x] 74. Configuration generator tool(s) — the Orchestrator/an agent creates a Database, External API, or MCP server configuration directly from details the user supplies in chat, instead of the user having to fill out the corresponding Settings form by hand. **Implemented 2026-09-21** (`src/main/tools/config-gen/`): `db_create_connection`, `api_create_connection`, `mcp_create_connection` — each gathers non-secret details, probes the connection for real, and (only on success) writes into the *existing* stores (`data/connections.ts`/`data/api-connections.ts`/`mcp/config.ts`, via their existing `save*` functions — no new storage mechanism).
  - **One model round-trip, not two.** The agent gathers non-secret details via chat (host/port/db name, base URL/auth type, MCP command/args) and calls one tool (e.g. `db_create_connection`) — its proposed non-secret config shows up in the normal tool-confirmation dialog, same as any other mutating call.
  - **Secret entry lives in that same confirmation dialog**: a new `secretFields` capability on the `Tool`/confirmation contract (`Tool.secretFields?: string[]`) lets a tool mark certain input fields as secret; `ToolConfirmDialog.svelte` renders those as real password inputs, excluded from the editable JSON textarea entirely. **Never persisted into the tool_use content block** — confirmed this falls out for free from the existing architecture, not something new to build: `orchestrator.ts`'s `dispatchTool` already only uses a confirmation dialog's edited `input` for the actual `execute()` call, while the *persisted* `tool_use` block is built earlier, from the model's own (secret-less) proposed input.
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — `secretFields` shape**: a flat array of field names on the `Tool` definition itself, not a per-call dynamic function, per the user's own choice. Real, accepted consequence for `api_create_connection` (whose secret field genuinely varies by `authType`): it declares all 3 possible names (`apiKey`/`token`/`password`) and the dialog renders all 3 password inputs unconditionally, regardless of which `authType` was chosen — leaving the irrelevant ones blank has zero effect (only non-empty secret values get merged into the executed input). A minor UX nit, not a functional bug, and the tradeoff the user explicitly picked over a dynamic-per-call alternative.
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — failed validation**: real "retry in the same dialog," not a fresh tool call. Built as a new optional `Tool.validate?(input, ctx): Promise<void>` method, separate from `execute()`, callable repeatedly with corrected input — a new `ToolValidateInput` IPC channel lets the dialog call it directly (bypassing the orchestrator/model entirely) before ever calling `onApprove`. A validation failure shows the real error inline and keeps the dialog open exactly as designed; approving re-validates once more inside `execute()` itself (defense in depth — matters because the auto-approve-tool-calls setting bypasses the dialog, hence `validate()`, entirely, so `execute()` must never trust that validation already happened).
  - **Real correction to the 2026-09-19 role-ownership note, found while actually implementing it**: that note's "MCP tools already merge into every role's registry" premise turned out to be wrong — checked `roles.ts` directly and found MCP-discovered tools are *only* ever reachable from the un-delegated, top-level orchestrator (`toolRegistry.list()`), never from any specialist role's own `get tools()` list (which is always an explicit static/prefix-filtered subset). So "available everywhere, mirroring MCP" doesn't actually describe a real existing mechanism to copy. Landed on a corrected, still-reasonable split instead: all 3 tools are registered in the default registry (so every one is reachable from top-level/Auto mode, matching how MCP tools *actually* behave), and `db_create_connection`/`api_create_connection` are additionally added to the Data/Research roles specifically (mirroring "Data/Research already own the read-side tools for these," the one part of the original note that *was* accurate) — `mcp_create_connection` stays top-level-only, since MCP genuinely has no owning role today, same as originally noted.
  - **New, not in the original design note**: `mcp_create_connection`'s success path also live-wires the new server in immediately — a new `ToolContext.connectMcpServer?: (id) => Promise<void>` callback (same optional/test-safe pattern as the existing `registerSavedQuery`), wired in `register.ts` to `mcpManager.reconnect(id, toolRegistry)`, the exact call Settings' own MCP-server-save flow already makes. Without this, an agent-created MCP server's tools wouldn't appear until something else happened to trigger a reconnect.
  - **Validation logic per type, real probes, not stubs**: DB builds a driver directly from the raw (unsaved) input (`SqliteDriver`/`PostgresDriver`) and calls its real `introspectSchema()`; API does a lightweight authenticated `GET` against `baseUrl` via the existing `performRequest`, failing only on a genuine network error or a `401`/`403` (a `404` at the root doesn't fail validation — many APIs have no valid root endpoint, reachability + credential-rejection are what's actually being checked); MCP runs the real `connectMcpServer` handshake (the same one `McpManager` uses live) and immediately closes the probe connection.
  - **20 new tests** across `db.test.ts`/`api.test.ts`/`mcp.test.ts`, non-mocked where it matters: DB validates against a real temp SQLite file (success) and a real unreachable-host Postgres attempt (real DNS failure, `getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND`); API mocks only `fetch` itself, exercising the real `performRequest`/auth-header logic; MCP mocks only the SDK's `Client`/transports (same pattern as the existing `mcp/client.test.ts`), exercising the real `connectMcpServer` handshake code. Every failure-path test also asserts nothing got persisted. 581/581 full suite passing, typecheck clean.
  - **Live-verified end-to-end against the real running app** (not just unit-tested) — this is genuinely new UI (the dialog changes) with zero prior coverage, so this mattered more than usual: drove a real chat turn (forced Data role, local Ollama) asking for a Postgres connection to a deliberately bad host — confirmed the real rendered dialog has exactly one `password`-labeled input, confirmed the JSON textarea's real content has no `"password"` key at all (the model never saw/produced it), typed a real value into the real password field via a genuine DOM input event, clicked the real Approve button, and confirmed a real validation failure (`getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND this-host-does-not-exist.invalid`) rendered inline while **the dialog stayed open** exactly per the retry design. A second live run with a real, valid SQLite file confirmed the happy path too: Approve → dialog closes → the connection is genuinely persisted (confirmed via `listDataConnections()`) and cleaned up afterward. `api_create_connection`/`mcp_create_connection` were not separately live-chat-tested (already have the strongest non-mocked-library test coverage of the three) — noted honestly, same convention as #73's `office_fill_template` gap.
  - **Known, accepted limitation, not fixed**: auto-approving any of these 3 tools (global "auto-approve tool calls" or a per-tool rule) bypasses the confirmation dialog entirely — which means it bypasses secret collection *and* the eager `validate()` call too. `execute()`'s own re-validation still catches this cleanly (a Postgres connection with no real password will simply fail its schema probe with a clear auth error, not silently persist), so nothing unsafe gets saved — but in practice these 3 tools are not meaningfully usable through auto-approve for any connection type that actually needs a secret. This is a structural property of the existing auto-approve feature generally, not something to fix specifically for #74.
  - **2026-09-22 — real live bug: `db_create_connection`'s sqlite `filePath` failed for a genuinely correct request.** The user gave the real absolute path to an existing `.db` file; the model instead passed a workspace-relative guess (matching `read_file`/`write_file`'s own convention, which `filePath` doesn't follow), and `better-sqlite3` rejected it with a cryptic "directory does not exist" error for a file that, at the path the user actually meant, existed the whole time. Fixed at the tool level, not just by re-wording the schema description (which was also tightened to say "absolute path," but a model can't be trusted to always get this right): an exact, existing path is still used as-is (sqlite connections are deliberately not workspace-confined), but anything else now falls back to a recursive workspace search by basename before failing, so a wrong/partial path still finds the real file when it's actually sitting in the workspace. New tests cover the exact failure shape plus an ambiguous-multiple-matches case. Full details/commit: see docs/CHANGELOG.md.
- [x] 75. `.env` file as an alternative to OS-keychain secret storage — give the user a choice of where secrets (provider API keys, DB/API/MCP/email connection credentials) live, instead of only `safeStorage`-encrypted `credentials.enc.json` (`src/main/security/keychain.ts`). Flagging the real tradeoff up front since it cuts against this project's own stated security posture (`docs/thouthts.md` #7: "API keys must be stored using the OS-native secure storage... never in a plaintext config file"): a `.env` file is plaintext on disk by nature, readable by any process/user with filesystem access, and a common accidental-commit/leak vector — very different guarantees from `safeStorage` (OS Keychain/DPAPI/libsecret). **Fully implemented 2026-09-21 — the first-run setup screen (the last missing piece) landed in this pass.**
  - **First-run setup screen, implemented**: new `FirstRunSetup.svelte`, a full-screen choice between "OS Keychain" (default) and "File" shown once, the very first time the app ever launches with nothing configured. Reuses the exact same `switchSecretStorageToFile()` action Settings already exposes — no separate code path, just a different entry point into the identical mechanism.
  - **Detection, settled to protect every existing real installation**: a new `onboardingCompleted` setting, defaulting to *not* shown unless positively confirmed as a genuinely fresh install (zero sessions AND no provider key configured for anything) — any real pre-existing install (which predates this setting entirely) gets the flag silently backfilled on its next launch instead of ever seeing the screen. Verified this holds against the actual real development install, not just reasoned about: launched it for real and confirmed the screen stayed hidden, with the flag correctly backfilled.
  - **A real race condition found and fixed before it could ship**: the fresh-install check (`listSessions()`) and the *existing*, unrelated `loadSessions()` behavior (which auto-creates a default session whenever the list is empty) were both fired at top-level with no sequencing — live-tested in a fully isolated environment (a real Electron launch with `--user-data-dir` and a fake `HOME`, so zero risk to real data) and caught the race directly: the fresh-check could see `loadSessions()`'s own freshly-created session and wrongly conclude "not fresh," silently skipping the screen forever. Fixed by sequencing `checkFirstRun()` to fully resolve before `initProvider()`/`loadSessions()` even start.
  - **Live-verified both real choices, entirely in isolation** (never against real production secrets): "Continue with OS Keychain" correctly dismisses the screen and leaves mode as `keychain`; "Use a file instead" (with its own in-screen confirmation, since it's irreversible) correctly calls the real switch action, producing a real `0600`-permissioned `.env` file at the fake home directory used for the test.
  - No new automated tests for this piece specifically — this project has no established pattern for testing `.svelte` component logic in isolation (everything renderer-side has always been verified live via CDP), so this follows that same precedent rather than introducing a new one.
  - **Implemented**: `src/main/security/keychain.ts` rewritten as a dual-backend module behind its existing `setApiKey`/`getApiKey`/`deleteApiKey` functions — every one of the many existing callers (provider keys, `data/connections.ts`, `data/api-connections.ts`, `mcp/config.ts`) needed **zero changes**, since they already only went through this one choke point. New `getSecretStorageMode()`, `envFilePath()`, `switchToFileStorage()`. New dependency `dotenv` (parsing only — this module still hand-writes the file).
  - **Real, empirically-discovered adjustment to the exact plan**: values are stored **base64-encoded**, not quoted plaintext as loosely implied by earlier notes — confirmed directly (a real round-trip test, not assumed) that `dotenv.parse()`'s double-quote handling only substitutes `\n`/`\r`, nothing else, so a value containing a literal quote or backslash would NOT round-trip correctly through quoting/escaping. Base64 sidesteps every such edge case entirely rather than trusting a format this app doesn't control the parsing rules of — a secret is opaque data, not something that needs to stay human-readable in the file anyway.
  - **Location**: a dedicated `~/.sachivji/.env` (home-folder directory, not the app's `userData` folder) — confirmed via a real live IPC call against the actual running app that this resolves correctly to the real user's home.
  - **Entry points**: a one-time action in Settings (**implemented** — new "Secret storage" section, shows the current mode as a read-only indicator, a "Switch to file storage instead" button while still on Keychain, gated behind an explicit in-app confirmation step since this is genuinely irreversible). **Not implemented**: the first-run setup screen (offering the same choice the first time Sachivji launches with nothing configured yet) — deferred as a separate, smaller polish item; Settings alone is already a fully functional, complete way to reach this whole capability.
  - **One-directional as a UI action, not one-directional as a data flow — revised 2026-09-19**: switching Keychain → file is still a single one-time user-facing action with no UI path back, but the keychain is NOT abandoned afterward. Everything currently in the keychain is exported to `.env` at switch time, `.env` becomes the primary source of truth for all reads, but **every write after switchover dual-writes to both `.env` and the keychain** (new secrets included) — the keychain stays a complete, current backup indefinitely, not a frozen snapshot. If `.env` can't be read at all (missing/corrupted), the app falls back to reading from the keychain if it still has the data; only if *both* are empty does the app treat it as a fresh, unconfigured start (recreate settings via Settings, same as a new install). No case of a corrupted-but-*readable* `.env` needs special handling beyond that same fallback, since `0600` permissions already rule out another local user being the cause.
  - **Settings UI**: shows the current mode as a **read-only indicator** ("Secret storage: OS Keychain" / "Secret storage: File (`~/.sachivji/.env`)"), with the one-time switch action visible only while still on Keychain.
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — key naming**: `PROVIDER_<NAME>` / `DB_<connId>` / `API_<connId>` / `MCP_<connId>` — mirrors the existing keychain key convention (`db:<id>` etc.) 1:1, no app-name prefix (considered `SACHIVJI_`-prefixing every key, dropped it — this is a dedicated file the app reads directly into its own secret store, never merged into `process.env` or a shell session, so there's no real collision risk a prefix would guard against).
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — file permissions**: `0600` on creation (owner read/write only), consistent with any other local plaintext-secrets file (ssh keys, `.netrc`) — **implemented and tested** (`chmodSync` explicitly enforced after every write, not just relied on via the `writeFileSync` mode option, since umask interaction can't be trusted cross-platform).
  - **7 new tests** (`keychain.test.ts`, against real temp directories via a mocked `homedir()` — never the real user's home), covering both backends, the export-on-switch, the dual-write, and — the one that matters most — a real fallback proof: delete the `.env` file after switching and confirm a secret written post-switch is still recoverable from the keychain backup. **Confirmed real teeth**: temporarily removed the dual-write call, watched that exact fallback test fail with the precise expected signature, restored the fix.
  - **Live-verified the read-only surface against the real running app, deliberately not the switch action itself**: `switchToFileStorage()` is genuinely irreversible, and this machine's real Sachivji instance has real, already-configured provider keys — triggering it live would have been an unwanted, permanent change to real production data, not a safe thing to "try and see." Instead confirmed via real IPC calls against the real app: `getSecretStorageInfo()` correctly reports `{mode: 'keychain', envFilePath: '/Users/<user>/.sachivji/.env'}`; the Settings UI's new "Secret storage" section renders correctly with the switch button present; and — a real regression check on the rewritten module's default path — `hasApiKey()` for every real configured provider (openai, google) still correctly returns `true` post-rewrite, proving the keychain-mode path is fully intact. The switch mechanism itself is proven correct by the isolated, teeth-confirmed unit tests instead.
  - 603/603 full suite passing, typecheck clean.

- [ ] 76. Widen the Data tool's DB support beyond SQLite/Postgres — SQL Server, MySQL/MariaDB, Oracle as the first concrete targets; DynamoDB/Cosmos DB/MongoDB-style stores explicitly out of scope for this item (see below). **SQL Server implemented 2026-09-21 (`mssql`), MySQL implemented 2026-09-24 (`mysql`); only Oracle and the allowlisted-runtime-install mechanism remain — this item stays open until Oracle lands or that install mechanism does.**
  - **SQL Server — implemented 2026-09-21**: new `src/main/tools/db/mssql-driver.ts` (`MssqlDriver`, wraps the `mssql`/`tedious` packages — pure JS, no native install, confirmed via `npm view` before installing), registered in `driver-factory.ts`. `DbEngine` widened to `'sqlite' | 'postgres' | 'mssql'` (`shared/ipc-contract.ts`); `data/connections.ts`'s `ResolvedConnection` union and `saveConnection`/`resolveConnection` widened to a real 3-way switch (mssql's default port 1433, alongside postgres's 5432, in a small `DEFAULT_PORT` map); `SettingsPanel.svelte` gained a "SQL Server" option reusing the exact same host/port/database/username/password fields as Postgres, with the port resetting to the right default when the engine is switched. `db_create_connection` (#74) widened the same way — validates via a real `introspectSchema()` probe before saving, same as the other two engines.
  - **`param-binding.ts`'s placeholder ternary generalized into `positionalPlaceholder(engine, index)`** (`?` sqlite, `$N` postgres, `@pN` mssql) — one shared function now used both by `bindNamedParams` (saved queries) and by `crud.ts`'s own query-building (see below), instead of two independently-drifting copies of the same logic.
  - **Real, pre-existing, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed while widening this for a 3rd engine, not introduced by it**: `crud.ts`'s `db_list_rows`/`db_find_rows` had hardcoded a literal `?` placeholder regardless of engine since they were first built — **confirmed live against a real Postgres container that this never actually worked**: `syntax error at or near "OFFSET"`, since PostgreSQL's wire protocol has no `?` placeholder syntax at all (only `$1, $2, ...`), and `crud.test.ts` had zero Postgres-specific coverage the whole time this shipped. Adding SQL Server surfaced a second, deeper layer of the same problem: T-SQL has no `LIMIT`/`OFFSET` keywords at all — real pagination needs `OFFSET ... ROWS FETCH NEXT ... ROWS ONLY` (which itself requires an `ORDER BY`, satisfied here with a dummy `ORDER BY (SELECT NULL)` since row order doesn't matter for this tool), and a capped single-lookup needs `SELECT TOP n` instead of a trailing `LIMIT`. Fixed with two new, engine-aware, exported query-builders in `crud.ts` (`buildPagedSelect`, `buildFindSelect`) covering all 3 engines' real syntax, not just their placeholder style.
  - **Verified the fix for real, not just via a mocked unit test**: re-ran the exact fixed SQL text against the same real Postgres container that reproduced the original bug (confirmed both `db_list_rows`- and `db_find_rows`-shaped queries now succeed with correct rows), then ran the actual `dbListRowsTool`/`dbFindRowsTool`/`dbCountRowsTool` functions themselves (not just raw SQL) against that same real server via a throwaway, not-committed test file — all three passed against real, correctly-paginated/filtered data. The container and throwaway test file were both removed afterward; the committed suite covers this with a mocked-`pg`-equivalent regression test (`crud-sql.test.ts`, asserting the exact SQL/params generated per engine) plus the pre-existing sqlite-based `crud.test.ts`, matching this project's established "mock in the committed suite, verify live once at implementation time" convention (same as `postgres-driver.test.ts`).
  - **Verified a real risk before assuming it was fine, not after**: SQL Server's `T-SQL` normally requires `[brackets]` for quoted identifiers, not the `"double quotes"` this codebase's shared `quoteIdentifier()` already uses for SQLite/Postgres — checked `tedious`'s own source directly (`node_modules/tedious/lib/connection.js`) rather than assuming, and confirmed `enableQuotedIdentifier: true` is tedious's real default, which issues `SET QUOTED_IDENTIFIER ON` on every connection — meaning double-quoted identifiers work correctly against SQL Server through this driver with zero changes needed to `identifier.ts`.
  - **`MssqlDriver` itself is unit-tested only (mocked `mssql`/`tedious`), not live-verified against a real SQL Server instance** — no realistic way to run one on this machine (Apple Silicon has no good story for Microsoft's official SQL Server Linux container). Flagged honestly, same as the earlier project note that Windows/Linux packaged-app testing couldn't be done locally either. `introspectSchema()`'s `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES`/`INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS` queries follow the exact same standard convention already proven live for Postgres, which is the main reason for confidence here beyond the mocked tests.
  - **Live-verified the actual Settings UI** (not just the tool/driver layer) against the real running app: opened Settings, clicked "+ Add connection," selected "SQL Server" from the real dropdown via a genuine DOM `change` event, and confirmed the form correctly shows Host/Port/Database/Username/Password (same shape as Postgres) with the port defaulting to `1433` the instant the engine selection changed.
  - **14 new tests** (`mssql-driver.test.ts`, `crud-sql.test.ts`, plus additions to `connections.test.ts` and `config-gen/db.test.ts`) — 595/595 full suite passing, typecheck clean.
  - **MySQL — implemented 2026-09-24**, immediately following #89's live Postgres verification and using the exact same driver/tool live-verification precedent. New `src/main/tools/db/mysql-driver.ts` (`MysqlDriver`, wraps `mysql2/promise` — pure JS, no native install, confirmed via `npm view` before installing; `npm audit` clean afterward). `DbEngine` widened to `'sqlite' | 'postgres' | 'mssql' | 'mysql'` (`shared/ipc-contract.ts`); `data/connections.ts`'s `ResolvedConnection` union and `DEFAULT_PORT` map widened (mysql's default port 3306); `driver-factory.ts` routes the new engine; `SettingsPanel.svelte` gained a "MySQL" dropdown option reusing the same host/port/database/username/password fields as Postgres/SQL Server, port defaulting to 3306 on selection; `db_create_connection` (#74) widened the same way, validated via a real `introspectSchema()` probe before saving.
  - **A real, previously-latent bug found while adding MySQL, not introduced by it — this time in `identifier.ts`, not `crud.ts`**: `quoteIdentifier()` has always hardcoded standard-SQL `"double quotes"` around every table/column name `crud.ts` builds into SQL — which happens to work for all 3 engines that existed before this (SQLite, Postgres, and SQL Server since `tedious` defaults `QUOTED_IDENTIFIER` on, per this item's own earlier note above). MySQL is the real outlier: `"..."` is a *string literal* there unless the non-default `ANSI_QUOTES` SQL mode is enabled server-side, which this app has no way to guarantee on a connection it doesn't control — a double-quoted table name would have made every `db_list_rows`/`db_count_rows`/`db_find_rows` call against a real MySQL connection either fail outright or silently misbehave. Fixed by making `quoteIdentifier(name, engine)` engine-aware (defaulting to the pre-existing `"..."` behavior for every other engine, so all 3 existing call sites needed only a one-line addition of `driver.engine` as the 2nd argument) — MySQL gets real backtick quoting, doubled to escape a literal backtick, matching MySQL's actual identifier-escaping convention. `param-binding.ts`'s `positionalPlaceholder` needed no change at all: its existing fallback branch (anything that isn't `postgres`/`mssql` returns literal `?`) already happens to be exactly MySQL's real placeholder syntax too.
  - **Verified against a real MySQL server, not just mocked unit tests, following #76/#89's exact same precedent**: a `mysql:8` Docker container loaded with the official [Chinook sample database](https://github.com/lerocha/chinook-database)'s real MySQL dump (`Chinook_MySql.sql` — 275 artists, 347 albums, 3503 tracks, 59 customers, 412 invoices; picked over Pagila for this one specifically to also exercise Chinook's own `PascalCase`/mixed-case table and column names, e.g. `Artist`, `ArtistId`, a real-world naming style Pagila's all-lowercase schema doesn't cover). A throwaway, not-committed vitest file (`zzz-verify-76-mysql.test.ts`, deleted after the run, mocking `electron` the same way `crud.test.ts` already does) ran the actual `dbIntrospectSchemaTool`, `dbExecuteSqlTool`, `dbListRowsTool`, `dbCountRowsTool`, and `dbFindRowsTool` against the real container — all 6 assertions passed, including the specific case this item's own bug-fix exists for: `db_list_rows`/`db_count_rows`/`db_find_rows` genuinely reading real, backtick-quoted-identifier data back from a live MySQL server (an exact 3503-row `Track` count; a real join+aggregation query across `Track`/`Genre`; a real `INSERT`/`DELETE` write cycle through `db_execute_sql`'s confirmation path, confirmed to leave the table's row count exactly where it started). The container and throwaway test file were both removed afterward; the committed suite covers this with `mysql-driver.test.ts` (mocked `mysql2/promise`, mirroring `postgres-driver.test.ts`'s/`mssql-driver.test.ts`'s shape), a new `identifier.test.ts` (engine-aware quoting, all 4 engines), and 2 new `mysql`-engine cases in `crud-sql.test.ts`. 820/820 full suite passing, typecheck clean.
  - **The architecture already fits this well**: `src/main/tools/db/types.ts`'s `DbDriver` interface (`introspectSchema()`, `execute(sql, params)`, `close()`) plus `driver-factory.ts`'s connection-`engine`-keyed factory is a clean, small extension point — the existing `PostgresDriver` is ~75 lines end to end. Adding a new SQL-speaking engine is a bounded, precedented unit of work per engine, not a redesign.
  - **Real constraint, worth stating plainly**: "the connection/dialect info is on the internet, the agent can look it up" solves the *knowledge* problem, not the *dependency* problem. Each engine needs a real compiled Node driver library added to `package.json` and bundled at build time — an agent inside a packaged, signed Electron app can't fetch/install a new driver at runtime, and having it dynamically load arbitrary code based on a web search would be a real RCE-shaped risk, not a feature. This item is "we add support for a new engine ahead of time," not "the agent adds its own DB support on the fly."
  - **Per-engine, not equally easy**: SQL Server (`mssql`, pure JS, no native install) and MySQL/MariaDB (`mysql2`, pure JS) are the easy additions. Oracle (`oracledb`) is the official driver but requires the separate Oracle Instant Client native libraries on the machine — real extra install friction for the user, worth flagging up front rather than discovering at ship time.
  - **Concrete per-engine work**: a new driver class implementing `DbDriver`; widen `connections.ts`'s `engine` union (currently hardcoded `'sqlite' | 'postgres'`, also duplicated as `DbEngine` in `shared/ipc-contract.ts`) and `SettingsPanel.svelte`'s connection-type dropdown + per-engine field set; generalize `param-binding.ts`'s hardcoded 2-way `$N`/`?` ternary to a real per-engine placeholder style (SQL Server named `@name`, Oracle positional `:1`, MySQL `?` like SQLite); an engine-specific `introspectSchema()` catalog query (Oracle uses `ALL_TABLES`/`ALL_TAB_COLUMNS`, not `information_schema`, unlike Postgres/SQL Server/MySQL which all share that convention).
  - **Where the agent's own reasoning legitimately helps** (the part of the original idea that's actually right): once a driver+connection exist, the model generating SQL against it benefits from knowing the target dialect (Oracle's `ROWNUM`/`VARCHAR2`, SQL Server's `TOP`/`GETDATE()`) — mostly already in an LLM's training knowledge, reinforced via the Data role's system prompt (which connection this is, so which dialect to write) rather than needing a live web search on every query.
  - **Explicitly out of scope here**: DynamoDB and similarly-shaped NoSQL/cloud stores (Cosmos DB, MongoDB) aren't SQL at all — no `execute(sql)` concept applies, it's an item-level API (getItem/putItem/query by partition+sort key). Doesn't fit `DbDriver`; would need its own tool family closer in shape to `api_call` than to `db_execute_sql` — a separate, unsized design exercise, not a new driver plugged into this item's existing shape.
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — implementation order**: SQL Server (`mssql`) first, but designed to be added-to incrementally rather than all-at-once.
  - [x] 89. **Sample PostgreSQL database with real data, for connection + query verification** — mirrors the real SQL Server Northwind container already used to verify #76's SQL Server driver; do the equivalent for PostgreSQL and actually run sample queries through the app's existing DB tools (`db_introspect_schema`, `db_execute_sql`, `db_list_rows`/`db_find_rows`) against it. **Northwind itself is a SQL Server-native sample** — unofficial Postgres ports exist but aren't the community-standard pick for Postgres specifically; worth evaluating alongside genuinely Postgres-native options before choosing, e.g. **Pagila** (the standard Postgres port of MySQL's Sakila — DVD-rental domain, real relational data with actual row volume, widely used for exactly this kind of driver/query smoke-testing) or **Chinook** (smaller, multi-DB-format, a music-store schema). Pick one with enough real data to exercise joins/aggregation meaningfully, not just a handful of rows. Scope: spin up in Docker (same pattern as the SQL Server container), connect via Settings > Data sources, confirm schema introspection and a handful of real queries (including at least one write, to exercise the confirmation flow) succeed end-to-end — same live-verification bar #76 already set for SQL Server.
    - **Picked Pagila** over Chinook — DVD-rental domain with real row volume (599 customers, 1000 films, 16,044 rentals, 16,049 payments, 4,581 inventory rows), enough to exercise joins/aggregation meaningfully, and the community-standard Postgres-native pick as the task text already called out. Spun up a dedicated `postgres:16` container (`pagila-verify`, port 5439 — checked free first via `ss -tlnp`, deliberately not reusing any port already held by this machine's unrelated `dcmms-*` project containers, e.g. its own Postgres/pgvector container on 5434).
    - **A real gotcha found only by actually trying to load it, not assumed**: the official `devrimgunduz/pagila` repo's current `master` branch schema (`pagila-schema.sql`) has been updated to require Postgres 18-only features — `uuidv7()` as a column default (native in PG18, not PG16), `GENERATED ALWAYS AS (...) VIRTUAL` computed columns (PG18-only; PG16 only has `STORED`), and a `film_embedding` table requiring the `vector` (pgvector) extension. Loading it against `postgres:16` failed with a cascade of "relation does not exist" errors (customer/film/payment/rental never got created, their dependents then failed in turn) — confirmed by comparing `\dt` output against the schema file's own `CREATE TABLE` statements. Fixed by using the pinned `v2.1.0` release tag's `pagila-schema.sql`/`pagila-data.sql` instead of `master` — confirmed PG18-only syntax genuinely absent from that tag before loading, then loaded clean (`ON_ERROR_STOP=1`, 0 errors) into a fresh database on the same PG16 container. This is a real, current-as-of-2026-09-24 fact about upstream Pagila, not a bug in this app.
    - **Verified against the actual app tools, not raw SQL**, following #76's exact precedent: a throwaway, not-committed vitest file (`src/main/tools/db/zzz-verify-89.test.ts`, deleted after the run) mocked `electron` the same way `crud.test.ts`/`index.test.ts` already do, called `saveConnection()` with the real container's host/port/credentials, then ran the actual `dbIntrospectSchemaTool`, `dbExecuteSqlTool`, `dbListRowsTool`, `dbCountRowsTool`, and `dbFindRowsTool` — all 6 assertions passed against the real container: schema introspection reported real Pagila tables/columns; a real join+aggregation query (`film_category` joined to `category`, grouped, ordered) returned correct results; `limit`/`offset` pagination worked against the real 200-row `actor` table; an exact count (16044 rentals) matched; `db_find_rows` found a real row by column value; and — the confirmation-flow requirement the task text called out — a real `INSERT INTO category` through `db_execute_sql` actually reached the server (count went 16 → 17), followed by a `DELETE` to restore it (17 → 16, confirmed via a direct `psql` count afterward that the container's data was left pristine). The container (`pagila-verify`) and the throwaway test file were both removed afterward, matching #76's "mock in the committed suite, verify live once at implementation time" convention — no new committed test file was needed since this item is pure verification of already-tested driver/tool code (`postgres-driver.test.ts`, `crud.test.ts`, `crud-sql.test.ts` already cover the Postgres path with mocks), not a code change. Full suite 809/809 passing, typecheck clean — zero application source touched by this item.
  - **New capability, added 2026-09-19 — allowlisted runtime install.** User's own framing: it should be possible to add support for a new DB type "when the app is running," not only via a future release. Real constraint confirmed unchanged from above: the app can't safely fetch/install *arbitrary* driver code at runtime (RCE-shaped risk). Resolved design: a small, code-curated allowlist of pure-JS driver packages *not yet bundled* — the allowlist itself is only ever extended by us via an app update (never user- or agent-editable), but once a package is on the allowlist, the running app can `npm install` just that package on demand, behind an explicit confirmation dialog, the first time a user actually needs it — no bundle-size cost paid for engines nobody uses, no waiting for the next release once something is allowlisted. Doesn't help for native-binary drivers (Oracle's `oracledb`) — those still require the app-bundled path. Exact install location (must be outside the read-only `app.asar`, likely a `userData` subfolder) and integrity verification before `require()`-ing an installed package are implementation-time details, not decided here.

- [x] 77. Orchestrator capability: **isolated delegation** — a delegate call that gets a scoped, minimal context and its own independent round budget, instead of today's behavior. General mechanism, not tied to any one role; item #78 (presentation/page creation) is its first real consumer. **Implemented 2026-09-21.**
  - **The gap, confirmed in the current code, not assumed**: `dispatchDelegate` (`orchestrator.ts`) builds every nested call's conversation as `[...conversation, taskMessage]` — the *entire* parent conversation, unconditionally — and threads the *same* `RunState` object into every nested `runLoop`, so `state.roundsUsed` against `MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS = 12` is one shared pool across the whole run, delegates included. Fine for today's roles (a handful of delegate calls per turn, each needing the parent's context to do its job), wrong for a role whose task is bounded and self-contained and gets invoked *repeatedly* in one turn (e.g. once per slide) — every call would drag in the full accumulated conversation, and N such calls would collectively exhaust the same 12-round ceiling the rest of the turn still needs.
  - **Per-role opt-in, not a blanket behavior change**: added `isolated?: boolean` and `maxRounds?: number` to the `Agent` interface (`orchestrator.ts`). Every existing role (`planning`/`research`/`coding`/`terminal-devops`/`data`/`distribution`) leaves both unset in `roles.ts` — zero behavior change confirmed by the full existing suite passing unchanged, not just by inspection.
  - **What `isolated: true` changes in `dispatchDelegate`**: the nested conversation becomes just `[taskMessage]` — the task/brief the calling role hands it, nothing else — and `runLoop` gets a **fresh `RunState`** (its own `roundsUsed` starting at 0) plus a new `maxRounds` parameter threaded through (`agent.maxRounds ?? MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS`), replacing every hardcoded `MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS` reference inside `runLoop` itself (the loop condition, the live `roundStatusMessage` progress string, the round-cap wrap-up prompt, and the final stub message) — so an isolated delegate's own status line correctly reads e.g. `(round 1/6)`, never leaking the parent's `/12`.
  - **Real correction to this design note's own stated mechanism, found while implementing it — the actual code doesn't work the way the note assumed**: "token usage rolls up... into `session_usage`" implied `RunState.inputTokens`/`outputTokens` feed the persisted total. They don't — grepped every reference and found these two fields are **write-only, never read anywhere in the codebase**; the real persisted total comes entirely from the per-round `usage` *stream events* (forwarded live via `yield*` regardless of isolation, so this was never actually broken). Implemented the rollup as designed anyway (`state.inputTokens += nestedState.inputTokens` after an isolated call returns) for consistency with the field's existing write pattern and in case a future reader appears, but the real, functioning cost-accounting mechanism was the streamed events all along, not `RunState`'s own fields.
  - **2 new orchestrator integration tests, both confirmed to have real teeth** (temporarily reverted the isolation branching, watched both fail with the exact expected signature — leaked `EARLIER-CONTEXT-MARKER` and a `5/12` status instead of `1/5` — then restored the fix and confirmed they pass again): one asserts the exact `CompletionRequest.messages` sent for an isolated delegate's own round contains only its task, not the parent conversation; the other burns 3 outer rounds first, then confirms the isolated delegate's live status messages start at `(round 1/5)`/`(round 2/5)` — its own maxRounds — never leaking the shared pool's `/12`, proving genuine decoupling rather than a coincidental fit.
  - **No live app verification** — this is a pure orchestrator-internals capability with zero observable surface today (opt-in, no real role uses it yet; #78 is its first real consumer). The mocked, teeth-confirmed integration test is the right and sufficient verification method here, matching how this exact class of orchestrator-internals work (the original context-bloat fixes) was verified previously in this project.
  - **Why this matters beyond presentations**: any future role with the same shape (bounded, self-contained, invoked repeatedly per turn on a narrow brief) can opt into this without another orchestrator change — this is the general primitive, not a one-off special case wired only for page creation.

- [x] 78. Presentation/deck creation, built on #77's isolated delegation — the top-level orchestrator plans a deck's structure/flow and delegates each planned slide to a new, isolated `page-creator` role that picks a fitting visual/infographic idea for that slide's content and builds it via `pptxgenjs`. **Implemented 2026-09-21, with a real architectural correction to the original design and a real live-found-and-fixed reliability bug — see below.**
  - **Two-tier split, confirmed as the right call over a template-menu approach**: `pptxgenjs` genuinely supports infographic-grade output (170+ shape types, 9 chart types, images/SVGs, tables, precise positioning/layering — checked directly against the package's real type definitions, not assumed), so a fixed menu of pre-built layouts would under-use it. One layer decides *what* slides exist and in what order; `page-creator` decides *how* one slide's content becomes a visual, with real creative latitude over shape/color/icon/chart choices — not a rigid template picker.
  - **Real architectural conflict found before writing any code, resolved with the user**: the original 2026-09-17 note said `coding` (a delegate itself) would delegate each slide to `page-creator`. Checked the actual code first and found this structurally impossible today — `dispatchDelegate` always passes `extraToolDefs: []` to a nested specialist's own `runLoop` call, meaning **a delegated `coding` call has zero `delegate_to_X` tools available at all** (the deliberate "recursion capped at exactly one level" policy from step 7 — #77 changed what context/budget a delegate gets, not who's allowed to delegate). Presented two real fixes to the user (widen recursion to 2 levels for this one case, vs. have the top level own deck structure directly) — **chose the latter**: `pptx_start_deck`/`pptx_add_slide`/`pptx_save_deck` are top-level tools (in `defaultTools`, reachable from Auto mode directly, same as every other tool), and the top-level model itself plans slide structure and delegates each slide to isolated `page-creator` — zero orchestrator recursion-depth change, fully preserves the step-7 policy.
  - **`page-creator` is isolated** (#77), `maxRounds: 6` — each `delegate_to_page-creator` call gets only that slide's brief, not the whole deck-planning conversation, and its own round budget decoupled from the shared 12-round pool.
  - **`AgentRole` widened to add `'page-creator'`, deliberately excluded from `AGENT_ROLES`** (the user-selectable/plannable list that powers the renderer's Ctrl+Tab picker and the classify-and-plan step's valid targets) — it's a delegate-only, isolated specialist expecting a specific one-slide brief; forcing a user conversation onto it directly, or letting the classifier target it as a whole plan step, would both be nonsensical. Still needs an `AgentRole` value (for `Agent.role`/`dispatchDelegate`'s map key) and an `AGENT_ROLE_LABELS` entry ("Page Creator").
  - **Round economy via tool shape, not via limiting creativity**: `page-creator` gets a single tool, `pptx_compose_slide`, taking a *whole slide's* visual spec in one call — an array of elements (`text`/`image`/`shape`/`chart`/`table`), each with its own type, position/size (inches, on a 10x7.5in slide), and content/style. Confirmed live: a real 2-element and a real 2-element (chart+text) slide each composed correctly in exactly one call.
  - **New `ToolContext.activeDecks` map** (`Map<string, {pres, slides}>`, fresh per turn, same lifetime/pattern as `registerSavedQuery`) — the only way state can survive across the top-level's own `pptx_add_slide` calls and the several separate, fully-isolated `delegate_to_page-creator` calls that build up one presentation together. `pptxgenjs` doesn't expose its own slide list publicly (`addSlide()` returns the new `Slide` directly, nothing else to index into later), so this module tracks the returned `Slide` objects itself.
