  - **A real, live-found reliability bug, fixed via a structural change, not just prompting**: the first live run (real chat, real local Ollama model, Auto mode) showed the model correctly getting a real `deckId` from `pptx_start_deck`, then **failing to carry that opaque id correctly through the delegate boundary** — `pptx_compose_slide` failed twice with invented placeholder ids ("deck1", "mydeck") instead of the real one, and the isolated `page-creator` even tried calling `pptx_start_deck` itself (correctly rejected — not in its own tool list) rather than recovering. Root cause: an opaque id has to survive a round-trip through natural language (the top-level's free-text `task` argument to `delegate_to_page-creator`, then the isolated specialist's own tool call) — this is genuinely fragile for a small local model, confirmed twice. **Fix**: `deckId` (on `pptx_add_slide`/`pptx_compose_slide`/`pptx_save_deck`) and `slideIndex` (on `pptx_compose_slide`) are now optional, defaulting to "the sole active deck" and "the most recently added slide" respectively — correct for the normal one-deck-at-a-time, one-slide-per-call flow, and sidesteps needing the id to round-trip through natural language at all in that common case; an explicit id is only required when genuinely ambiguous (more than one deck in progress at once), which throws a clear, actionable error rather than silently guessing. **Re-verified live after the fix**: the identical 2-slide request that failed before now completes correctly end-to-end (`done: true`, both slides composed, real file saved) — independently confirmed by unzipping the real saved `.pptx` and reading its actual slide XML, not just trusting the tool's own success message.
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — failed/ugly slide handling**: implemented as prompted behavior (no special orchestrator code needed) — the top-level's system prompt instructs it to retry a poor `delegate_to_page-creator` result once itself with a corrective brief (e.g. "simplify, fewer elements"), and only ask the user for their own direction if that retry also doesn't work, rather than retrying indefinitely.
  - **`pptx_compose_slide`'s input schema, settled during implementation**: a flat, simplified element schema (not pptxgenjs's own more verbose option shapes) — text/image/shape/chart/table, each a plain object with `type` + `x`/`y`/`w`/`h` plus type-specific fields — matching #73's precedent of not exposing library-internal complexity directly to the model.
  - **18 new tests** (`deck/index.test.ts`'s full real lifecycle — start, add slides, compose every element type including a real chart and table, save, and independently read the result back via `office_read_document`; the deckId/slideIndex-omission convenience and its ambiguous-multi-deck error case; workspace-boundary checks — plus a real mocked-orchestrator integration test in `orchestrator.test.ts` proving the whole pipeline: top-level plans, the isolated delegate's own request genuinely excludes the deck-planning conversation, and a real `.pptx` lands on disk). 614/614 full suite passing, typecheck clean.
  - **2026-09-22 — real live failure: the rich pipeline above got bypassed entirely, three compounding root causes found and fixed, user-reported ("not thrilled with it," thin per-slide content, weak research).** A real "build a presentation" session never touched `pptx_start_deck`/`page-creator` at all — traced via the actual session transcript, not guessed: (1) **classify-and-plan mis-routed it**: the request got planned as a `"coding"` step, but Coding has zero deck-building tools, so it fell back to `office_create_document`'s plain title+bullets .pptx path (no charts/tables/visual design at all) — matches exactly what the user saw. Root cause: `buildClassifySystemPrompt` listed every role in `this.agents`, including `page-creator` (deliberately excluded from `AGENT_ROLES`/`VALID_STEP_ROLES`, see shared/agents.ts) — an invalid option was visibly "available," and nothing told the classifier that deck-building isn't a `coding`-shaped task at all. **Fixed**: the classify prompt's role list is now filtered to `AGENT_ROLES` (matching what `parsePlan` actually validates against), plus an explicit line that deck-building is never a plan step for any role — it only happens in the top-level's own final round. (2) **`office_create_document`'s own tool description never disambiguated** from the real pipeline for an actual presentation request — a model reaching for "a tool that makes .pptx files" had no signal that this one is deliberately primitive (flat bullets, no visuals) and a much better tool exists for a real deck. Fixed: its description now says so directly, live-failure example included. (3) **Research was shallow**: two broad `web_search` calls, snippet text only, zero `parse_html` follow-through on the actual source pages — every downstream slide inherited that thinness regardless of `page-creator`'s own visual-design quality, since it can't invent facts it was never given. Fixed: Research's prompt now sets an explicit depth bar for report/presentation-shaped requests — search results are candidates, not the research itself; read the real content of the several best sources via `parse_html` before calling it done. All three are prompt/description-level fixes, no new mechanism — a real orchestrator-level fix (item #3 from the user's follow-up ask, page-creator flagging when it needs more info and the caller fulfilling that) and template support (items #4/#5) are deliberately deferred, user's own explicit priority call. 1 new orchestrator test (classify prompt excludes page-creator, mentions deck-building explicitly) plus the 3 description/prompt changes. 661/661 full suite passing, typecheck clean (main + web). Not yet re-verified live against a fresh real session — the fix is confirmed via the exact mechanism that broke, not yet by regenerating the same request end-to-end.
  - **2026-09-22 — full codebase/prompt review requested by the user ("it's crap... fails in between, takes huge amount of time, no control on how tools are performing... no feedback to the user... crapy messages"), three more real bugs found and fixed.** (1) **Duplicate content on retry, confirmed by unzipping a real saved `.pptx`**: `pptx_compose_slide` applied each element to the real `pptxgen.Slide` object in a loop with no rollback — a batch that failed partway (e.g. an invalid element) left the earlier elements permanently applied, so a corrected retry duplicated them onto the slide (found live: "Analytics Landscape" and a whole chart appearing twice in the actual slide XML, traced to a failed-then-retried compose call in the session transcript). **Fixed**: every element in a `pptx_compose_slide` call is now validated in a first pass before anything is applied in a second — a bad element rejects the whole call cleanly, nothing partial ever lands on the slide. (2) **Missing image files failed at the wrong tool, far from the mistake**: confirmed directly (a standalone `pptxgenjs` script) that `addImage()` never reads the file — only `writeFile()` does, at save time — so a hallucinated icon path silently "succeeded" while composing a slide and only surfaced as a bare `ENOENT` much later, at `pptx_save_deck`. **Fixed**: an image element's path is now checked to actually exist as part of the same validation pass, with a message naming the bad path directly. (3) **The page-creator prompt was actively misleading**: it told the model it could use "icons" as a design tool, but there's no icon/image generator — the only way to place an image is to reference a file that already exists, which the prompt never said, and likely explains both the invented icon paths and the model inventing non-existent element types (e.g. `"map"`) to get an icon-like effect some other way. **Fixed**: the prompt now enumerates the exact 5 valid element types, says explicitly not to invent image paths, and explains that a rejected call means nothing was applied so a retry should resend the whole corrected array. (4) **A real design flaw in this session's own earlier round-timeout fix — full writeup and fix under #80**, likely a major contributor on its own: a timed-out round's retry resent the identical prompt with the identical short timeout, so a round that genuinely needed close to or over 5 minutes could never converge, burning the whole turn deadline on repeated wasted restarts while surfacing a warning each time that read as a failure but was actually the mechanism sabotaging itself. Now adaptive — each retry gets more room than the attempt that wasn't enough. 5 new tests (3 in `deck/index.test.ts` — existing-image compose, missing-image rejection, no-duplication-on-retry verified against real generated XML; the existing round-timeout/deadline suite in `orchestrator.test.ts` rewritten to simulate the new adaptive formula exactly rather than assuming a flat 5 minutes per round — worked out via a standalone script first, not guessed, since the deadline is now crossed by 4 escalating-then-floored rounds, not a flat 3). 668/668 full suite passing, typecheck clean (main + web). Not yet re-verified live against a real running session.

- [ ] 79. Email-sending sub-agent — an isolated `email` role (per #77) that any specialist (or the Orchestrator) hands a loose brief to ("email the team about this") along with recipient address(es) and optional attachment file paths (workspace-relative, same convention as `read_file`/`write_file`); the role drafts the actual subject/body itself, sends it, and confirms back with the result. **Gmail phase implemented 2026-09-21 — item stays open until the Outlook/Microsoft Graph phase below also lands.**
  - **Resolved 2026-09-19 — full reasoning role, not a plain tool**: unlike a fully-specified send, the actual ask is often "email X about Y" rather than caller-supplied subject/body — composing the message *is* the task, so this is a genuine `page-creator`-shaped candidate for #77's isolated delegation (bounded, own round budget), not a plain mutating tool like `write_file`. **Implemented**: `email` role, `isolated: true, maxRounds: 4`. Unlike `page-creator`, `email` **is** included in `AGENT_ROLES` (user-selectable via the Ctrl+Tab picker, and a valid classify-and-plan step target) — "send an email about X" is a perfectly normal standalone request, unlike a one-slide brief.
  - **Confirmation behavior**: `mutates: true`, a real confirmation dialog showing the recipients/subject/attachments before sending — matches this app's existing convention, and matters more here than most mutating actions, since an email is genuinely irreversible once sent, unlike most of this app's other actions which at least stay local. **Implemented as designed** (`send_email`'s `describe()`).

  ### Phase 1 (implemented 2026-09-21): Gmail
  - **Library**: `nodemailer` (MIT-0, confirmed real via `npm view`), `service: 'gmail'` transport preset — no host/port config needed. New `src/main/tools/email/transport.ts` (`createGmailTransport`, `verifyGmailConnection`), `src/main/tools/email/index.ts` (`list_email_connections`, `send_email`).
  - **Auth**: app password. Real and durable today — Google's own direction is OAuth2 for *new* integrations, but app passwords remain fully supported for personal Gmail accounts with 2-Step Verification enabled; not deprecated. Sources: [Nodemailer: Using Gmail](https://nodemailer.com/guides/using-gmail), [Google: Sign in with app passwords](https://support.google.com/mail/answer/185833?hl=en).
  - **Storage**: a 4th connection type alongside DB/API/MCP (`src/main/data/email-connections.ts`, mirrors the existing 3), secret (the app password) via the existing keychain plumbing keyed `email:<id>`, dual-written to `.env`'s `EMAIL_<id>` under #75's storage mode. **A real gap found and fixed while implementing this**: `keychain.ts`'s `envKeyFor` prefix list (`db`/`api`/`mcp`) had been written before #79 existed and didn't include `email` — an email secret in file mode would have silently produced an invalid, colon-containing env key (`PROVIDER_EMAIL:<id>`) instead of `EMAIL_<id>`. Fixed, with a regression test confirmed to fail without the fix first.
  - **Settings UI**: new "Email accounts" section (mirrors External APIs' layout) — display name, Gmail address, app password, with a real help link to Google's app-password page. Validated the same way #74 validates a new connection: a real test SMTP login (`transporter.verify()`, no message sent) before the connection is saved, error shown inline with retry/abort — implemented directly in the form's own save handler (not the `ToolValidateInput`/confirmation-dialog machinery, since this is a plain Settings form with no agent tool call involved, unlike #74's agent-created connections).
  - **23 new tests** (`email-connections.test.ts`, `tools/email/index.test.ts` — mocked `nodemailer`, covering send/list/attachment-resolution/workspace-boundary/unknown-connection paths) plus the `keychain.ts` regression above. 629/629 full suite passing, typecheck clean.
  - **Live-verified against Google's real infrastructure, not just mocks**: the Settings UI form and help link render correctly; a real `validateEmailConnection` IPC call against a genuinely nonexistent Gmail address made a real network connection to Google's actual SMTP servers and got back Google's real, exact rejection (`535-5.7.8 Username and Password not accepted... gsmtp`) — confirming the full pipeline (Settings UI → IPC → nodemailer → real Gmail server) works end-to-end for the failure path. **Honest gap**: the success path (a real valid app password actually sending) was not live-verified — no real test credentials were available this session; the user can confirm that path themselves with a real account whenever convenient.
  - **Not added**: a dedicated orchestrator-level mocked-integration test (unlike #77/#78's pattern) — `email`'s `isolated: true` flag exercises the exact same `dispatchDelegate` code path already proven generically by #77's own tests (and again by #78's), so the marginal value didn't justify introducing `electron`/`nodemailer` mocking into `orchestrator.test.ts`'s currently-mock-free setup. The tool-level tests plus the already-proven generic isolation mechanism cover this.

  ### 2026-09-22: tried a Microsoft app-password provider, reverted — confirms Phase 2 below was the right call all along
  A later session, not having (re-)read the Phase 2 section below first, added `office365` and `outlook` app-password providers (separate nodemailer `service` presets: `Outlook365` for work/school, `Hotmail` for personal) directly parallel to Gmail's. **Reverted the same session, at the user's request, after confirming live** (a real SMTP login attempt against a real personal Outlook.com/Hotmail account with a real app password, not just docs) that Microsoft has in fact now fully retired SMTP Basic Auth for **both** account types — the exact `535 5.7.3 Authentication unsuccessful` a user hit, for a personal account, resolves this session's Phase 2 date ambiguity: the April 30, 2026 full-retirement date (not the more lenient December 2026 one) was the real one, and it's already passed as of this note. No per-account or admin toggle exists to bring SMTP Basic Auth back for either account type. **In its place**: a `custom` provider — host/port/secure given directly in Settings, `nodemailer.createTransport({host, port, secure, auth})` — for any *other* SMTP server (Yahoo, iCloud, a company's own mail server, Zoho, ...) that still supports password auth; genuinely useful on its own merits, not a Microsoft workaround. `EmailProvider` is now `'gmail' | 'custom'`. The real, first-class Outlook/Microsoft 365 option remains exactly the Phase 2 OAuth2/Graph design below — untouched, still the plan, just not built yet.

  ### Phase 2 (documented now, build later): Outlook / Microsoft 365 / Hotmail
  **Real constraint that shaped this whole design, confirmed via research 2026-09-19/21, sources throughout, and re-confirmed live 2026-09-22 (see note above)**: plain email+password (with or without 2FA) cannot work here at all — this isn't an app-password-specific gap, Basic Authentication itself (the underlying SMTP username/password login mechanism) is being retired industry-wide for Microsoft's mail services. Personal Outlook.com/Hotmail accounts have *already* dropped it entirely (app passwords for those accounts stopped working and can't be regenerated) — live-confirmed 2026-09-22 with a real account/app password, `535 5.7.3 Authentication unsuccessful`. For Microsoft 365 business accounts, secondary sources disagreed on the exact date at the time this was first written (one said Basic Auth for SMTP AUTH is disabled by default for existing tenants end of December 2026, admin-reversible until then; another said Microsoft already permanently disabled it March 1, 2026 with full retirement, no exceptions, by April 30, 2026) — the 2026-09-22 live confirmation (above, for a personal account) plus that date having already passed resolves this in favor of the earlier, harder April 30, 2026 deadline; still worth checking [Microsoft Learn's own deprecation page](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/exchange/clients-and-mobile-in-exchange-online/deprecation-of-basic-authentication-exchange-online) directly when this is actually built, in case anything's shifted further. The actionable fact is date-independent: OAuth2 is mandatory, no exceptions.
  - **Why the user's own mail client never showed an Azure/Entra screen — explains the whole design below, not a loophole to route around**: Microsoft's own first-party apps (Outlook for Mac/iOS) get to accept a real password directly because the app itself already carries Microsoft's own trusted identity. Non-Microsoft apps (Apple Mail, Thunderbird) do a *real* OAuth 2.0 flow behind an embedded, native-feeling sign-in view — each has its own pre-registered app identity (Apple's, Mozilla's) — that's what makes the OAuth screen invisible-feeling. Sachivji needs the exact same shape: its own pre-registered app identity, so the *end user* only ever sees a plain Microsoft sign-in popup, never Azure configuration. Sources: [Apple Community: Apple Mail MFA](https://discussions.apple.com/thread/254336647), [Microsoft Support: Modern Auth for non-Microsoft email apps](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/office/modern-authentication-methods-now-needed-to-continue-syncing-outlook-email-in-non-microsoft-email-apps-c5d65390-9676-4763-b41f-d7986499a90d), [Bugzilla: Thunderbird's own O365 2FA support ticket](https://bugzilla.mozilla.org/show_bug.cgi?id=1528136).
  - **Resolved — use Microsoft Graph's `sendMail` API, not SMTP OAuth2.** The original plan (nodemailer's XOAUTH2 SMTP transport against `smtp.office365.com`) works, but Graph is Microsoft's actual current recommendation: it isn't exposed to the SMTP-AUTH retirement at all (a completely different API surface), supports personal Outlook.com/Hotmail accounts via the same delegated `Mail.Send` scope as work/school accounts, and automatically writes to Sent Items (SMTP doesn't). `nodemailer` has no first-class Graph transport, so the Outlook send path is a plain authenticated `POST https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/me/sendMail` call, not routed through `nodemailer` at all — Gmail keeps using `nodemailer`, Outlook uses a direct Graph call; the isolated `email` role's tool implementation branches by the connection's provider, both exposed as the same `send_email`-shaped action to the model. Sources: [Dartmouth: SMTP OAuth2 vs Graph FAQ](https://services.dartmouth.edu/TDClient/1806/Portal/KB/ArticleDet?ID=168187), [Unipile: Microsoft Graph Email integration guide](https://www.unipile.com/microsoft-graph-api-email-integration-guide/).
  - **App registration — Sachivji needs exactly one, shared by every user of the app (not one per user, not something an end user ever creates).** Step-by-step, to actually follow when this phase is picked up (verify each step against the live Entra admin center UI first, since portal UIs shift):
    1. Sign in to the [Microsoft Entra admin center](https://entra.microsoft.com) with a Microsoft account under this project's control (a personal Microsoft account is sufficient for creating an app registration — no paid Azure subscription required for this).
    2. **App registrations → New registration.**
    3. Name: `Sachivji` (or similar — user-visible only on the consent screen, e.g. "Sachivji wants to: Send mail as you").
    4. **Supported account types: "Accounts in any organizational directory (Any Microsoft Entra ID tenant – Multitenant) and personal Microsoft accounts (e.g. Skype, Xbox)."** This is the one that makes both personal Outlook.com/Hotmail and any organization's Microsoft 365 work — picking a narrower option (single-tenant, or org-only) would silently break one of the two account types this item targets.
    5. **Redirect URI**: platform type **"Mobile and desktop applications,"** value `http://localhost` (loopback — the modern MSAL pattern for a native app using the system browser; libraries like `msal-node` bind an actual free port at runtime and match against the registered `http://localhost` base). Do not use the legacy `https://login.microsoftonline.com/common/oauth2/nativeclient` redirect URI some older docs show — that's an ADAL-era pattern, not the current MSAL/loopback approach this design uses.
    6. After creation, copy the **Application (client) ID** from the Overview page — this is the one value the app's code needs; **no client secret is created or used**, since a public/native client authenticates via PKCE, not a secret (a desktop app can't keep a secret safe anyway).
    7. **Authentication** blade → under "Advanced settings," confirm **"Allow public client flows" = Yes**.
    8. **API permissions** → Add a permission → Microsoft Graph → **Delegated permissions** → add `Mail.Send`, `offline_access` (needed for a refresh token — without it, the user would have to re-sign-in every ~60-90 minutes), `openid`, `profile`, `email`. These are all user-consentable delegated scopes; **no admin consent grant is needed for personal accounts**, and for most Microsoft 365 tenants a regular user can consent to these specific low-risk scopes themselves the first time they sign in — flag honestly in the UI that a minority of locked-down organizations may require their own IT admin to approve it once, since that's outside Sachivji's control.
  - **Token flow at runtime**: `msal-node`'s `PublicClientApplication` with `acquireTokenInteractive` (handles the loopback listener itself in current versions) using the client ID from step 6 and authority `https://login.microsoftonline.com/common` (the multi-tenant + personal endpoint matching step 4). Store the resulting refresh token in the keychain (or `.env` per #75) keyed `email:outlook:<accountId>`, dual-written per #75's resolved design; access tokens are short-lived and re-derived on demand via `acquireTokenSilent`, never persisted. A silent-refresh failure (`invalid_grant`, revoked consent, tenant policy change, or the token simply going stale from disuse) must surface as "please sign in again," not a silent failure — re-run the same interactive flow.
  - **End-user-facing flow, addressing the "well-orchestrated flow, not just documentation" requirement directly**: Settings → Email → "Add email account" shows two provider buttons, Gmail and Outlook/Microsoft, each with a one-line explainer so the visibly different flows don't look like an inconsistent or broken UI:
    - *Gmail*: "Sign in with your email and an app password" → the form described in Phase 1 above.
    - *Outlook/Microsoft 365*: "Sign in with Microsoft" (single button, no form fields at all) → opens the user's real default system browser (never an embedded webview inside Sachivji — a native app popping up its own login-look-alike window is exactly the shape of a phishing attack, and Microsoft's own guidance for native apps is to use the system browser) to Microsoft's actual `login.microsoftonline.com` page → user signs in and completes their own MFA there, entirely on Microsoft's real domain → browser redirects to `http://localhost:<port>/callback`, where Sachivji's already-listening local server catches the code, exchanges it, and closes the browser tab automatically → Settings shows "Connected: `<email>`" for that account.
    - Both paths land in the same place afterward: Settings lists every configured send-from account (masked where relevant), each independently removable/re-connectable, mirroring the pattern already used for DB/API/MCP connections.
  - **Confirmed alignment with the user's explicit position** ("I am not in favor of the app password right now" for Outlook): this design has zero app-password path for Outlook — Gmail keeps app password (the one place it's still genuinely durable), Outlook is 100% the OAuth2/Graph flow above.

- [x] 80. Round-timeout + turn-deadline hard reliability bound — every provider round (top-level or any delegate, isolated or not) is now guaranteed to end, one way or another, well inside a bounded wall-clock window, instead of a single round being able to sit unresponsive indefinitely with the Stop button unable to help. **Implemented 2026-09-22, then substantially reworked the same session after a design flaw was found — see the second entry below.**
  - **Found live**: a real turn (a rich multi-slide presentation) sat completely unresponsive for over an hour with zero visible error — confirmed directly against the running process via Chrome DevTools Protocol (`Runtime.queryObjects`/`getProperties` on every live Promise, `process._getActiveHandles()`/`_getActiveRequests()`, `lsof`) that nothing was actually pending anywhere: no open network connection, no pending Promise in the heap. The round had died silently with no bound on how long the app would just sit there before anyone noticed, and Stop had nothing left to cancel.
  - **User then explicitly tightened the requirement twice, mid-investigation**: "It should not take more than an hour" → "It should create the presentation in less than 15 minutes" — required an actual hard time-bound mechanism, not just a diagnosis.
  - **Two-tier bound**: a per-round timeout (`ROUND_TIMEOUT_MS`, base 5 min) races a real `provider.stream()` call via a combined `AbortSignal` (the real user/turn signal plus a `setTimeout`-driven one) — on firing, it's handled through the exact same "clean abort" path a genuine Stop click already goes through, distinguished only by `timedOut()` so a real Stop still ends the whole turn immediately while a mere round-timeout is treated as one failed attempt and the loop continues. A turn-wide `TURN_DEADLINE_MS` (15 min, `RunState.deadlineAt`, shared and never reset across every nesting level — including an isolated delegate's own fresh round *count*, which is a separate budget from the wall-clock one) stops the whole turn from running long in aggregate even if every individual round stays under its own timeout; once past it, `runLoop` falls into the same round-cap wrap-up path `MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS` exhaustion already used (one final, tools-disabled call synthesizing a real answer from whatever's already been gathered, rather than a bare stub).
  - **A real mid-implementation design correction, caught by the test suite, not the user**: the first version of the timeout catch block always ended the whole turn on any abort (matching pre-existing Stop-click behavior) — its own new tests then failed against it, revealing that a *timeout* must not end the turn the way a genuine Stop does. Redesigned to `continue` the loop on a timeout instead of returning.
  - **2026-09-22, same day — a second, real design flaw found during the full presentation-pipeline review below, and fixed**: the flat 5-minute timeout applied identically to every retry, and a timed-out round's retry resent the *exact same* prompt/context with zero adaptation — so a round that genuinely needed close to or over 5 minutes (plausible on a slow local model; confirmed elsewhere in this project that a single real round can take several minutes) could never converge: every attempt got killed just before finishing and restarted from zero, burning the entire 15-minute turn deadline on repeated wasted restarts, while surfacing a "stopped automatically, trying again" warning each time that reads as a failure but was actually this mechanism sabotaging itself. This is very likely a major contributor to the user's "fails in between... takes huge amount of time... crapy messages" complaint that prompted the review. **Fixed**: the per-round budget is now adaptive, not flat. `RunState.consecutiveRoundTimeouts` counts how many times in a row the *same* round has just timed out (reset to 0 the moment a round actually completes) — each retry's budget roughly doubles (`ROUND_TIMEOUT_MS * (consecutiveRoundTimeouts + 1)`), bounded above by the real remaining turn budget minus a fixed safety margin (`ROUND_TIMEOUT_SAFETY_MARGIN_MS`, so ending a round never leaves literally zero time for whatever runs next) and below by a floor (`ROUND_TIMEOUT_FLOOR_MS`, so even a round started with almost no turn time left gets a workable window). A round's *first* attempt at anything is unchanged (still the original 5 minutes, so a genuinely hung round is still caught exactly as fast as before) — only a round that's already timed out once gets more room on its retry. The warning message wording also changed, from "This step was taking longer than 5 minutes and was stopped automatically" (reads like a tool failure) to explicitly attributing the wait to the model generating, not a tool being stuck.
  - **4 tests** in `orchestrator.test.ts` (single hung round recovers cleanly; a genuine Stop during a hung round still ends the turn immediately, unlike a mere timeout; a whole turn crosses the deadline via consecutive round-timeouts and falls into the wrap-up path with distinct wording from a round-count exhaustion; an isolated delegate inherits the parent's already-ticking deadline rather than getting a fresh one) — rewritten the same session to simulate the real adaptive-budget formula exactly (worked out first via a standalone Node script, not guessed) rather than assuming a flat 5 minutes per round; the deadline is now crossed by 4 escalating-then-floored rounds under today's constants, not 3, a direct and verified consequence of the adaptive design. 668/668 full suite passing, typecheck clean (main + web). Not yet re-verified live against a real running session.

- [x] 81. Deck auto-save-on-turn-end safety net — a presentation the user was told was "created" could fail to exist anywhere in the workspace, with no error shown. **Implemented 2026-09-22.**
  - **Found live, user-reported** ("the app says the presentation is created... however it is not there"): traced to `ToolContext.activeDecks` (the only place an in-progress deck's state lives — see #78) being a fresh, empty `Map` constructed on every single `runChat` IPC call. A deck genuinely built across several delegate calls within one turn was fine, but if that turn ended (for any reason — hit a round/deadline limit, an error, or simply never got around to calling `pptx_save_deck`) without an explicit save, the entire in-memory presentation was silently discarded the moment the call returned — no file, no error, nothing to recover. A subsequent investigation turn found the model had covered this specific failure up by renaming an unrelated pre-existing file to the expected name rather than admitting the deck was never actually saved.
  - **Fixed**: `ipc/register.ts`'s `runChat` now wraps the orchestrator's run in a `finally` block that walks whatever's left in `activeDecks` after the turn ends and, for any deck with at least one composed slide, saves it automatically under a clearly-labeled fallback name (`unsaved-presentation-<deckId>.pptx`) and posts a real warning message explaining what happened and that the file can be renamed/continued whenever convenient — turning a silent, total loss of work into a recoverable, clearly-communicated one.
  - **Confirmed live-working, not just unit-tested**: found 2 real firings of this exact warning in a live session transcript, and both auto-saved files independently verified to exist on disk with correct, non-zero sizes matching their slide counts.

- [x] 82. Show an image inline in the chat, given a workspace path — raised by the user 2026-09-23 after a real session where a generated chart (`northwind_orders_1998.png`) could only be viewed by leaving the app to open the file manually. Built to the recommended first-pass scope: **images only, display-only (Fork 1a — no model vision), inline base64 (Fork 2a)** — video and model-vision stay explicitly separate, later items. `src/main/tools/show-media.ts`'s `show_media` tool reads an existing workspace image (`.png`/`.jpg`/`.jpeg`/`.gif`/`.webp`/`.svg`, 5MB inline-display cap, same `WorkspaceGuard` every file tool uses) and returns a JSON marker payload rather than changing the `Tool.execute(): Promise<string>` contract every other tool implements. `orchestrator.ts`'s `dispatchTool` recognizes that marker and splits it into a new `image` `ContentBlock`/`StreamEvent` (`protocol.ts`) plus a short, honest tool_result text ("Displayed X to the user.") — the base64 bytes are never sent back to any provider: confirmed (not assumed) that all 3 adapters' wire-format mapping functions already drop any unrecognized block type by construction (the same mechanism that already excluded `warning`), so this needed zero provider code changes, just updated comments plus a dedicated test per adapter proving it with a real base64 marker string absent from what's sent. Registered on Coding and Terminal/DevOps (the roles that actually generate images), not every role. **A real bug caught only by live verification, not by any unit test**: the renderer's CSP (`index.html`) had no `img-src` directive, so it fell back to `default-src 'self'` — which does not cover the `data:` scheme — and a `data:` URI `<img>` silently failed to decode (`naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight` stayed 0) in a real Electron launch; fixed by adding `img-src 'self' data:;` specifically, without loosening `script-src`/`style-src`/`default-src`. Live-verified end-to-end against a real, fully isolated Electron instance (separate `--user-data-dir`, a seeded SQLite db with a real 1x1 PNG's actual bytes, zero risk to real data): confirmed via real DOM state that the `<img>` genuinely decodes (`naturalWidth: 1, naturalHeight: 1`, matching the seeded image's real dimensions) and that the short tool_result text shows while no base64/marker text ever leaks into the visible transcript. New tests: the tool itself (extension/size/path/existence validation), the orchestrator-level marker-splitting behavior (success and failure paths), a dedicated wire-format-exclusion test in each of the 3 provider adapters' test suites (none existed for `warning` either before this), and role-membership coverage.

- [x] 84. Template auto-creation from an existing document — given a document handed to the app (via `office_read_document`/#73's read path, or a workspace path directly), create a reusable template from it (matching #73/#75's `templates/` folder + `office_fill_template`'s `{placeholder}`/`{#loop}` marker convention) and save it there. **Implemented 2026-09-24.**
  - **Resolved design question**: the open question was whether to auto-detect variable-looking content or rely on explicit user guidance. User's answer (2026-09-24): auto-detect via heuristic, but always confirm with the user, and allow this to be iterative — the agent may produce intermediate template drafts for review, refining the replacement list from feedback until the user confirms it's right.
  - **New tool `office_extract_template`** (`src/main/tools/office-docs/template-extract.ts`), registered on the Coding role alongside the other office tools. `action: "detect"` (read-only) runs a mechanical heuristic (`placeholder-detect.ts`) over the document's real text — dates (ISO, slash/dash, and both month-name orders), currency amounts, percentages, emails, phone numbers, URLs — and lists each candidate with a suggested `{placeholderName}`. Deliberately does NOT attempt to detect names/addresses/other free-text fields — that needs real judgment a regex can't provide (is "Acme Corp" a company name to templatize, or fixed branding that belongs in every output?); the tool description explicitly tells the agent to propose those itself from its own reading of the document and put the *full* candidate list (detected + agent-proposed) in front of the user before applying anything, matching this project's established "mechanical tool for cheap/certain patterns, model reasoning for judgment calls" split (see #76's dialect-reasoning note). `action: "apply"` (mutates) takes a confirmed list of exact-text → placeholder replacements and writes `templates/<templateName>`, always flat under `templates/` regardless of any directory component given (same fixed-destination-folder precedent as `git_clone_repo`'s `.sachiv-clones`) — every `apply` call regenerates fresh from the original source document, never compounding on a previous template attempt, which is what makes the iterative refine-and-reapply workflow safe (verified directly: a test applies once, then applies again with a *different* replacement list against the same source, and confirms the first call's change wasn't silently lost or duplicated).
  - **The real mechanics, prototyped against real files before being written into the tool**: for .docx/.pptx, replacement happens by opening the file's real internal XML (`word/document.xml` via `pizzip`; each `ppt/slides/slideN.xml` via `jszip`, both already dependencies) and substring-replacing matched text only inside `<w:t>`/`<a:t>` text-run elements — never touching surrounding structure, so original formatting/branding/images are untouched. For .xlsx, `exceljs` gives direct structured cell access (no raw XML needed) — a matched cell's rendered text has the match replaced and the cell value is overwritten with the resulting string. Confirmed live, not assumed, via a real generated .docx/.pptx (through `docx`/`pptxgenjs`, the same libraries `office_create_document` already uses) run through this replacement logic and then through `office_fill_template`'s real `docxtemplater` engine end-to-end — the templated document correctly round-trips, including a value that had to be replaced in two separate places getting the same new value in both.
  - **A real design correction found while prototyping, before it ever became a bug**: the first version only matched a run's content if it was *exactly equal* to the target text (whole-run match). This works for text authored as one plain string per paragraph, but is NOT representative of realistically-authored Word documents, where a bold label and its plain value are commonly two separate runs sharing one paragraph *or* one combined run like "Amount Due: $1,250.00" — tested directly against a real generated .docx and confirmed the whole-run-only version silently matched nothing for the combined case. Fixed before ever shipping it by switching to substring replacement within each run's decoded text content (re-encoding XML entities correctly on write-back), which subsumes the whole-run case as well.
  - **Known, explicitly documented limitation, inherited rather than solved**: only text within a single text run can be detected/replaced — if Word/PowerPoint's own internal editing history split a phrase across multiple adjacent runs, that phrase won't be matched. This is the exact same constraint `office_fill_template`'s own `docxtemplater` engine already has and this codebase already relies on; stated plainly in the tool's description rather than attempting a full run-merge algorithm, which is a lot of real complexity for a feature meant to stay bounded.
  - **`extract.ts` widened** with `extractDocxRuns`/`extractPptxRuns` (real per-run text, not the flattened plain-text view `extractOfficeText` gives) — `extractPptxText` refactored to build on top of `extractPptxRuns` rather than duplicating the slide-walking logic, zero behavior change (existing `office_read_document`/`office_fill_template` tests still pass unmodified).
  - **27 new tests** across `placeholder-detect.test.ts` (heuristic correctness per category, occurrence counting, and the explicit "does not detect names" limitation), `extract.test.ts` (new run-extraction functions), and `template-extract.test.ts` (detect/apply for all 3 formats, the fixed-`templates/`-destination guarantee, unmatched-replacement warnings, occurrence-specific replacement, the fresh-regeneration-not-compounding guarantee, input validation, and the full real cross-tool round-trip through `office_fill_template`) — 847/847 full suite passing, typecheck clean, `npm audit` clean (no new dependencies — reused `pizzip`/`jszip`/`exceljs`/`fast-xml-parser`, already present for #73's tools).
  - **#85** (template selection during document creation) remains open and unstarted — a separate, later item, not needed for this one to be useful on its own.
- [ ] 85. Template selection during document creation — when the user asks to create a document (`office_create_document`/#73) and no default template is configured, list the templates available in `templates/` (see #84) for the user to pick from, applying via `office_fill_template`; if none exist yet, explain how to create one (author one directly, or use #84 once built) and get explicit confirmation before proceeding without a template. Needs a "default template" setting (per document type?) to be added — none exists today. Depends on #84 for the "none exist yet" guidance to be concretely actionable, though the picker/confirmation flow itself can land independently. Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): 0.5.0 (detail), templates family with #197 and #198.
- [x] 149. `capRepeatedToolResults` erases real tool-result content it shouldn't, Implemented 2026-09-29 (the sibling `collapseOldestToolResultsToFit` mechanism, flagged below as equally exposed, is NOT fixed here — split out as #170). causing both repeated identical tool calls and final answers that drop or fabricate data — found live 2026-09-28 investigating session `cd81e5f5` (#148's session: "can you list all image files and the code files separately from the workspace?"). Root-caused via round-by-round DB reconstruction, not assumed: `orchestrator.ts:263-292` (`MAX_KEPT_RESULTS_PER_TOOL = 3`, `:235`) groups tool_use ids **by tool name only** — never by arguments/path, so all 15 `list_directory` calls in this turn (different paths/depths) shared one bucket — and, despite its own doc comment saying "within a single turn," actually runs against **the entire session's conversation array** (`prepared.messages` from `prepareConversation`, not turn-scoped; turn-scoping only happens at the coarse whole-turn-drop level in `applySlidingWindow`, which never fired here). Once a tool-name bucket exceeds 3, every older result is replaced with a content-free placeholder ("Earlier result omitted... see the tool_use input above for what was tried") that preserves *what was attempted* but nothing about *what it found*. Reconstructed exact round timing: by round 4 of this turn, an earlier turn's root `list_directory` result (which genuinely contained `generate_chart.py`) was already collapsed; by round 6 — the precise round the model first repeated a call — its own `list_directory("charts")` finding (real: `top_customers_2020.png`, `top_customers_2021.png`) had just been collapsed too; by the final round 16, none of the 3 still-expanded `list_directory` results was a charts listing. **Consequences, all traced to this one mechanism**: (1) the model re-issued identical `list_directory` calls repeatedly — not aimlessly, but because losing the actual result content (while `tool_use` inputs stay visible, per the function's own doc comment) left it unable to tell whether an earlier call had found anything, so it redid the call to get real data back, which then got collapsed again; (2) the final answer omitted both real charts images, since neither survived to the synthesis round; (3) the final answer stated a plausible-sounding but unlisted filename (`generate_chart.py`) as fact — real stale data from an earlier turn, but already collapsed 12 rounds before the synthesis round, so best characterized as the model pattern-completing from adjacent still-visible real context (`.py` files in `code/`, chart PNGs in `charts/`), not invention from nothing — flags that no code-level guard today prevents a tool-using model from stating an unverified filename as fact once its source result is gone from context. Two sibling mechanisms have the identical content-free-placeholder flaw and did **not** trigger this specific incident but are equally exposed: `refitConversationToBudget` (`:992-1009`, fires once a cheap token estimate crosses ~70% of the context window, collapses oldest non-mutating results via `collapseOldestToolResultsToFit`, `:441-472`) and the `MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS=20` (`:121`) wrap-up synthesis path (`:1885-1987`, reuses the same collapse pipeline at `:1936-1942`) — neither engaged here (session used 16 of 20 rounds; total content was far under budget for any real cloud-model context window). **Separately confirmed** (classification, `classify_gate_log`): this request was classified `simple`, which skips planning (`orchestrator.ts:891-902`, any plan with ≤1 step goes straight to free-form `runLoop`) — so there was never a systematic "enumerate every directory" step, which is why `documents/` was never queried at all despite appearing in every root listing; a contributing but separate cause from the collapsing bug above. **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak), directly informed by #148's discussion:**
  - **Key the cap by (tool name + argument shape), not name alone** — unconditional fix; this is exactly what let 15 differently-pathed `list_directory` calls compete for one shared 3-slot bucket.
  - **Genuinely scope it to the current turn**, matching its own doc comment (today it runs over the whole session array). This needs a real, consistent turn-identification mechanism — the same one #158's turn-continuation marker needs; build one shared notion of "current turn," not two separate ones across these two fixes.
  - **The core fix: placeholders become compact-but-complete, not content-free with a short summary.** For a listing-shaped result specifically, a "collapsed" version keeps every filename it found — just terser formatting (names only, no per-file metadata) — rather than silently dropping them. This directly satisfies #148's hard requirement (a "do something for each file" task can still see every entry even from a collapsed result, because the essential enumerable data was never erased, only de-verbosed) without needing the harder problem of inferring whether the task "still needs" the data.
  - **#148 as a complementary mitigation, not a substitute**: fewer redundant calls (one `find_files` instead of 15 `list_directory` calls) means the eviction bucket is hit far less often, but this fix is still required regardless of call volume, since the underlying flaw (erasing real data) is independent of how often the cap triggers.
  - Ready to implement, alongside #148 and sharing #158's turn-identification work. See also [[project-sachivji-dangling-tool-use-bug]] for the project's established pattern of code-level (not prompt-level) fixes for this class of replay/context bug.
  - **Implemented**: `capRepeatedToolResults` (`orchestrator.ts`) now buckets by a `bucketKey(toolName, input)` — `list_directory`/`find_files` key by their path (+ `find_files`' pattern too) via a new `RESOURCE_KEY_ARGS` map, so different targets no longer share a bucket; every other tool (including `web_search`) deliberately keeps name-only bucketing (confirmed via the existing test suite: `read_file` is already used in two places as a generic stand-in for a fuzzy-repeated-call tool, so it was deliberately left OUT of `RESOURCE_KEY_ARGS` too, not just `list_directory`'s siblings — resource-keying it would have silently changed that established test behavior). **Genuinely turn-scoped now**: reuses `splitIntoTurns` (exported from `context/prepare-context.ts` — the one canonical turn-boundary definition in this codebase, now shared rather than re-invented, per this item's own requirement) and only counts/touches `tool_use` ids from the last turn; an earlier turn's results are never read or marked stale by this function at all. **Compact-but-complete collapse**: a new `compactListing()` strips `list_directory`'s `"dir "/"file "` prefix (or `find_files`' header line) and joins every real entry it found into one line, wrapped in a short "collapsed but still complete" note — never the old fully content-free placeholder for a listing tool; a listing with nothing to compact (empty dir / no matches) is left completely untouched rather than wrapped in a placeholder around nothing. New tests in `orchestrator.test.ts` (all real orchestrator-integration tests, matching this function's existing test convention, not unit tests of a private function): (1) 5 `list_directory` calls to 5 different real paths — direct regression test for the incident's actual root cause, confirms none collapse; (2) the same path called past the cap still collapses its oldest calls, and the collapsed content contains the real filename, not the generic marker; (3) an earlier, already-closed turn's 3 calls (already at the cap) are completely untouched by one new call in a fresh turn to the same path — direct proof of turn-scoping. Full suite 1330/1330 passing, typecheck clean.
- [ ] 170. Extend #149's "compact but complete, never content-free" fix to `collapseOldestToolResultsToFit` — split out 2026-09-29 from #149's own text, which flagged this as an equally-exposed sibling mechanism but was never explicitly decided as in-scope for that pass. Confirmed still using the fully content-free `CONTEXT_FIT_OMITTED_MARKER` (`orchestrator.ts:511-542`) for every non-mutating tool result it collapses, same flaw #149 just fixed for `capRepeatedToolResults`. Only one call site (`orchestrator.ts:1078`, inside `refitConversationToBudget`) — confirmed this single function is also what the `MAX_TOTAL_ROUNDS` wrap-up synthesis path reuses (per #149's original investigation), so fixing it here covers both, not two separate fixes. Straightforward reuse, not a new design question: apply the same `LISTING_TOOL_NAMES`/`compactListing`-shaped logic already built for #149 to this function's own collapse loop — the triggering CONDITION differs (budget pressure vs. per-tool repetition count) but the collapse OUTPUT shape should be identical. Ready to implement whenever picked up (no discussion needed — this is applying an already-decided pattern, not deciding a new one). **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.3.2.**
- [x] 148. Recursive directory listing + find-by-pattern tool — Implemented 2026-09-29 (grep-in-files split out as #169, not part of this pass). Originally scoped 2026-09-28 as prompted directly by a real session (`cd81e5f5`, "can you list all image files and the code files separately from the workspace?") where the agent made **15 separate `list_directory` calls** for what a single recursive pass should cover: `list_directory(".")` repeated 6 times (byte-identical results each time), `list_directory("code")` 3 times, `list_directory("charts")`/`list_directory("resources")` twice each — and still never queried `documents/` at all, despite it appearing in every root listing. **Confirmed root contributor, not just a bad-luck loop**: `list_directory`'s real schema (`file-ops.ts:73-92`) takes only `path` — there is no `depth`/recursive parameter at all — but the model called it with `{"path":"code","depth":2}` and `{"path":"","depth":3}` hoping for recursion; the extra field is silently ignored by `execute()` (only `input.path` is read), so it got back the identical flat listing with no error or hint that recursion isn't supported, and kept guessing. `shell_execute` already exists and could run `ls -R`/`find`/`grep` inside the workspace guard, but the model reached for repeated `list_directory` calls instead in this session. Wanted: (1) a real recursive/depth-aware option on `list_directory` (or a dedicated `find_files` tool) with glob-pattern matching (e.g. `**/*.png`) so "list every image file" is one call; (2) a `grep`/`find_in_files` tool for content search across the workspace, through the same `WorkspaceGuard` every file tool uses — mechanical/typed tools in `file-ops.ts`'s existing style, not just telling the model to prefer `shell_execute` (loses the structured, guard-consistent, confirmation-free read-only path the other file tools have). Related but separate from #149 (this same session's `capRepeatedToolResults` bug, which dropped the found charts images and produced a stale-but-uncited filename in the final answer) — that's why data got *lost*; this is why the agent had to grope one directory at a time to *find* it in the first place. **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak):** a separate `find_files` tool, not an extended `list_directory` — keeps `list_directory`'s simple one-level shape predictable, and a real typed schema (path, glob pattern, e.g. `**/*.png`) means an unsupported param is a validation error, not silently ignored the way #149's incident started (the model guessing at an undocumented `depth` param). Default-ignore the same things `.gitignore` conventions already cover (`node_modules/`, `.git/`, `venv/`/`.venv/`, this project's own `.sachiv-clones/`) — reuse `.gitignore` parsing if the workspace has one rather than a hardcoded list that drifts. **No artificial cap on what the tool itself returns** — the filesystem enumerates a large tree fine, that's not the bottleneck. Instead, shape what reaches the model: reuse/extend #50's existing trim-for-provider mechanism (already caps oversized tool results to ~4000 chars for the provider, full data always kept in SQLite/UI) but make it listing-aware — a truncated `find_files` result should say "N files found, showing first K, narrow the pattern or use `write_file` to persist the full list" rather than a blind character cut; no new "write to file" tool needed, `write_file`/`shell_execute` already cover that escape valve. **Hard requirement carried into #149's design**: whether an older listing gets collapsed to a one-line summary in context later must depend on whether the current task still needs the individual entries — a one-off "what's in this folder" can safely shrink once served, but a "do something for each file" task can't, since the model still needs every entry to act on it. This is the same judgment #149's incident needed and didn't have. **Implemented**: `tools/find-files.ts` (new), a real typed `find_files` tool (`path`, `pattern` — glob with `**`/`*`/`?`, no full glob-library dependency added, hand-rolled and bounded to those three operators), registered in `tools/setup.ts` and attached to the three roles that already had `list_directory` (`coding`/Developer, `data-processor`, `document-creator` — same reasoning: any role limited to one-level listing had the same gap). Never descends into `node_modules`/`.git`/`venv`/`.venv`/`__pycache__`/`dist`/`out`/`build`/`.cache` plus this project's own `EXCLUDED_FOLDERS` (now exported from `workspace-layout.ts` for reuse, so the two lists can't drift apart) — a real `.gitignore` at the workspace root is also read and merged in, via a **deliberately partial** reader (basename + basic glob patterns only, no `!` negation, documented as failing open rather than risking a real file being silently hidden) rather than a full gitignore-spec library, given the concrete driving need was simple directory excludes, not complex negation rules. Results are returned relative to the workspace root regardless of the search `path`, directly usable as `read_file`/`write_file`'s own `path` input. No cap on what the tool itself returns (a `MAX_ENTRIES_SCANNED = 20,000` safety-valve on the *walk*, not the result count, only trips on a pathologically large tree and says so honestly). **`trimToolResults` (#50, `context/prepare-context.ts`) made listing-aware** per this item's own decision: a new `toolNamesByUseId` lookup (a `tool_result` block carries no tool name itself, only `toolUseId`) lets the trim step recognize a `find_files`/`list_directory` result and cut it at a line boundary with an honest "N found, showing first K, narrow the pattern/path or use write_file to persist the full list" message, instead of the generic blind mid-string character cut every other tool still gets. New tests: `find-files.test.ts` (real tmpdir, no filesystem mocking, matching `file-ops.test.ts`'s own convention) covers `**` vs. bare `*` scoping, default-ignored directories never descended into, a real `.gitignore` respected, workspace-relative output from a subdirectory search, sorting, the no-match message, and the workspace-boundary refusal; `prepare-context.test.ts` gained two tests for the new listing-aware trim (line-boundary cut with every kept line verified as a real, complete original entry; an under-cap listing left untouched). `prompt-budget.test.ts`'s ceilings raised deliberately (not padded) for `routerBare`/`routerToolSchemas` (full unfiltered registry, #96) and the three roles `find_files` was added to, with the reason recorded in the test file itself. Full suite 1327/1327, typecheck clean. No live Electron verification — this is a pure backend agent tool with no UI surface, so the real-filesystem test suite is the appropriate verification here, same standard `file-ops.ts` itself is held to.
- [ ] 169. `grep`/`find_in_files` — content search across the workspace, through the same `WorkspaceGuard` every file tool uses — split out 2026-09-29 from #148's original scope (item 2 of its "Wanted" list), not implemented alongside `find_files`. Real design considerations distinct from #148, not yet decided: binary-file detection (searching a `.png`/`.docx` byte-for-byte would be both wasteful and produce garbage matches — needs the same kind of extension/content-sniffing guard `write_file`'s `assertTextWritable`/`show-media.ts` already use elsewhere, reused rather than reinvented); how much match context to return per hit (bare line vs. a few lines of surrounding context, mirroring `grep -C`); and — the sharper risk versus #148 — result-size explosion: a content search across a large text corpus can produce far more matches than a file listing ever would, so the same #148/#149 "compact but complete" principle (cap the *view*, never silently drop *data* the model still needs) needs its own answer here, likely a per-file match cap plus a total-file-count cap with the same honest "showing first N, narrow your pattern" framing `find_files`'s trim now uses. Fetch and show a picture from the web — found 2026-10-06 asking for a named person's bio and photo (session "Can you search on a person…"): no tool could save an image from a URL, `parse_html` listed text and links but never `<img>` addresses, and the model blamed "login walls" it never tested. The Trump failures were the model guessing Wikimedia thumbnail widths and file paths (400/404), one original over the 5 MB cap, then a 429 — fixed by listing the real thumbnail URLs from the file page. - [x] 185. Files outside the workspace, and a tidy workspace root — from the passport session (2026-10-04): the folder search worked only because `shell_execute` is unchecked (the file tools reject outside paths, so access depended on which tool the model picked), and one script run left 120 loose images in the workspace root. Done: **read** outside the workspace is allowed per folder after a prompt (just once / this session / always — "always" folders are listed and removable in Settings > Workspace); covers `read_file`, `list_directory`, `find_files`, `show_media`, `office_read_document`, `xlsx_read` and the `parse_*` tools' `filePath` (read only: `append` outside is refused); **creating a NEW file** outside (`write_file`) is asked every time, never remembered, never overwrites, never creates folders; editing or deleting outside files is not offered. Never reachable whatever is clicked: `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.azure`, `~/.kube`, `~/.docker`, `~/.config/gcloud`, `~/.sachivji`, the keychain, Sachivji's own app data, `/etc`, `/System`. The prompt is never auto-approved (not by the session blanket, not by a per-tool rule). Mechanism: `Tool.externalPaths` + `ExternalAccess` (security/external-access.ts), asked by the orchestrator before the tool's own confirmation. **Scratch files:** `python_run` sets `SACHIV_TMP` (workspace `tmp/`), moves a BULK (4 or more) of new loose root-level files to documents/, code/, resources/ by extension and anything else to `tmp/`, reports the new paths, and `tmp/` files older than 7 days are deleted at the start of a turn; the Developer prompt says scratch goes in `tmp/`. One or two new root files stay where the script wrote them (they are usually the requested output). **Not covered:** `shell_execute`/`python_run` are not sandboxed and can still read, edit or delete anywhere the user can (their confirmation is the only guard); sqlite connections can point anywhere; images from outside are not yet readable as text (see #183).
- [x] 186. Deck builder: a deck came out as a pile, and a failing call was repeated 34 times — manual test 6.11 (old prompt "Make a 3-slide deck about our roadmap", 2026-10-06). Causes found in the session: (1) a Gemini 503 ended the turn mid-deck and the next turn could not continue — deck state lives per turn, so `Unknown deck` (the first attempt was auto-saved as `unsaved-presentation-<id>.pptx`); (2) the Page Creator's `pptx_compose_slide` calls came out corrupted (the `type` field swallowed the fields after it) and it repeated them ~34 times until the user pressed Stop; (3) after "continue" its calls used `left/top/width/height` instead of `x/y/w/h`, which the tool silently ignored, so every element took pptxgenjs's default position and the whole deck piled up in the top-left corner; (4) the tool text and the Page Creator prompt said a slide is 10x7.5 in but the real layout is 10x5.625 (16:9), so content laid out for 7.5 ran off the bottom. Done: `pptx_compose_slide` now maps left/top/width/height and numeric strings to x/y/w/h, recovers a garbled or missing `type` from the leading word or the fields present (and says it did), REQUIRES numeric x/y/w/h, requires positive w/h (a line may be flat), and rejects anything that runs off the 10 x 5.625 in slide with a message stating the bounds; the layout is set explicitly to 16:9 and the prompt/tool text now say 10x5.625; a run now stops after 6 failed calls in a row to the same tool (orchestrator `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_TOOL_FAILURES`), with a warning saying why. **Second round, from the rerun with the concrete Project Aurora prompt:** it now ran in ~45 s with exactly the expected 11 calls and a clean layout, but (5) the Page Creator, which sees only its brief, added content it was never given (three invented milestone cards on the title slide that contradicted slide 2, filler subtitles and descriptions, a thank-you line) and the router's final summary described slides that did not match the file; (6) the text carried literal backslash-n characters (an escape escaped twice). Done: the Page Creator prompt now says the design is its own but the content is not — only the brief's words, nothing added, reworded or repeated — and its final message must quote the text now on the slide; `pptx_compose_slide` returns `Text on the slide: ...` so what is reported matches what is there; literal `\\n`/`\\r\\n` in text and table cells become real line breaks. Re-verified live in a throwaway copy with its own workspace: 11 calls, slide 1 only the title and subtitle, slide 2 the three milestones in the brief's words, slide 3 only "Questions?", nothing off-slide, no literal `\\n`, accurate final message (the one addition: a small "Milestones" heading on slide 2). Not done: continuing a deck across turns (needs deck state kept per session, not per turn); judging whether a composed slide LOOKS good (overlap, text overflow) — only bounds are checked.
