Verified live in a throwaway copy with a 5-section research file: the top-level read the file, downloaded a stock image, then for each of 5 slides `pptx_add_slide` + `delegate_to_page-creator` (+ its `pptx_compose_slide`), one `pptx_save_deck`; the saved deck has 5 slides, Calibri/Georgia, navy and off-white backgrounds, one picture, nothing off-slide, no literal `\\n`, and no script. Still open: a model that guesses image URLs is refused (correct) and ends with fewer pictures than slides unless it asks search for image results; `shell_execute` could still be used to run a python-pptx script (not guarded); the research text file is saved at the workspace root by the Developer step re-typing the Researcher's output (wasteful; a direct save would be better).
- [x] 188. Presentations: real stock pictures, a Page Creator that designs, and research with depth — user feedback 2026-10-06 ("stock images are a good idea; the page creator shows no creativity; the researcher returns too little"), plus a freeze found in the next session. **Pictures:** `find_stock_images` (Openverse, no key; commercial-use, non-mature photographs, wide by default, Wikimedia via its thumbnail, `queries` for a whole deck in one call, each result with a ready credit line, URLs registered with the link guard); `download_image` takes `images:[{url,name}]` (up to 12, quiet, failures reported per image); pictures crop to fill (`fit: cover`, default) instead of stretching, with natural size read from the file header (`deck/image-size.ts`); shapes take `transparency` for text-over-photo overlays; text takes `fontFace`/`italic`/`valign`, slides a `background`. **Page Creator:** a design playbook in its prompt (layouts by slide purpose, type scale, margins, colour, picture treatment); a brief's coordinates/sizes/element lists are non-binding — found live that the router wrote a coordinate-level layout into every brief, so slides 2-8 came out identical; the router now sends content, purpose, picture and design system only, and picks one design system per deck. **Deck rounds:** `pptx_start_deck` takes `slides: N` (all slides at once; an unindexed compose fills the first empty slide), the round cap went 20 -> 30 (a 12-slide deck hit the "Stop here / Continue" box). **Research depth:** the Researcher got `save_research_note`/`read_research_notes` (a run keeps only the 3 latest results per tool, so a long read would lose early pages), `write_file` (saves its own findings — no separate Developer step), `find_stock_images`, `web_search depth:advanced`, 40 rounds, a depth protocol in its prompt, and an Agent `completionGate` that sends a shallow run back to work (visible "[Completion check by Sachivji]" notes, up to 6): at least 8 pages read over 4 searches (naming unread URLs from its own searches), notes of at least 10,000 characters, and — when a file is asked for — a file of at least 12,000 characters with 8 source links. Measured on the same request: 6.4 KB / 0 sources / 0 pages read (flash-lite) -> 10.8 KB / 10 sources / 7 pages (flash-lite, with the gate) -> 26.7 KB with real NIST/OWASP/Stanford/PwC/IBM sources (GPT-5.4). **Freeze:** `parse_html` fed a large PDF to the HTML parser on the main thread — the window froze ~100 s and ended in "Maximum call stack size exceeded" (session "I want you to research for the material…", 23:01). `resolveSource` now refuses PDFs, images, video/audio, archives and office files by Content-Type (and a `%PDF-` first-bytes check), and caps a body at 5 MB while reading it; the PDF that froze it is refused in ~1 s. **Duplicate research:** an incomplete wiki step no longer adds a web follow-up when a Researcher step is already planned (that run researched the same topic three times). **Not done:** reading PDFs (research leans on PDF reports — NIST, OWASP, Stanford, McKinsey; needs a PDF text extractor run off the main thread with a page cap and timeout); judging whether a composed slide LOOKS good; the Researcher still misses depth on weak models without the gate's nudges; the router (not the Page Creator) writes the briefs, so a weak router can still dictate layout despite the prompt.
- [x] 171. An oversized `search_history` chunk can crash the local Ollama embedding server, and `runBackfill` retries it forever — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live during the §13.3 LLMWiki manual-test pass during the §13.3 LLMWiki manual-test pass (unrelated to LLMWiki itself; surfaced in the Sachivji terminal as a wall of repeating `OllamaProvider.embed`/`BadRequestError` stack traces the user flagged mid-session). Root-caused via direct evidence, not assumed: pulled the actual message from `sachiv.db` — `09ce3807-01d2-4e7b-a0f1-d6958b1b0c9f`, a 30,886-character raw JSON `tool_result` (234 rows from an earlier Northwind "Top 10 customers" test session) with no blank lines anywhere in it. `chunkText()` (`src/main/search-history/chunk-text.ts`) only splits on blank-line paragraph breaks (`MIN_CHUNK_CHARS = 20`, split on `/\n\s*\n/`), so this message stays one single ~31KB chunk, passed whole to `provider.embed()` in `embedAndStoreChunks` (`src/main/search-history/indexer.ts`) — far past `nomic-embed-text`'s 2048-token context. Confirmed in `journalctl -u ollama` at the matching timestamp: `llama-server` loads the model, the prompt fills the full `n_ctx_slot = 2048`, returns one `400` on `/v1/embeddings`, then **segfaults** (`signal: segmentation fault (core dumped)`) — running on the `Vulkan0 (llvmpipe...)` software backend, consistent with this dev machine's pinned-GLIBC/no-real-GPU-accel setup (see `user-env-dev-machine` memory). Ollama restarts `llama-server` on a new random port each time it dies, which is why the error log shows a different `127.0.0.1:PORT` in every line (`33361`, `45253`, `40947`, `46205`, `46037`, `36803`, ... all within ~3 minutes). **The amplifying bug**: `runBackfill` (`indexer.ts`) has no permanent-failure tracking — on any embed error it just leaves the message unindexed and retries the same batch after `BACKFILL_BATCH_DELAY_MS` (500ms), forever. Since this message can never successfully embed (deterministically oversized), it keeps crashing the local embedding backend in an infinite loop rather than failing once and giving up. **Not yet decided/fixed** — two independent gaps, either or both worth closing: (1) cap chunk size before calling `embed()` (hard character/token ceiling in `chunkText`, or a fallback split when no blank-line boundary exists within a chunk) so an unbroken blob like a raw tool-result JSON dump can't reach the embedding call whole; (2) give `runBackfill`/`embedAndStoreChunks` a per-message failure counter (e.g. in `message_embeddings` or a new column) so a message that fails N consecutive times is marked permanently skipped instead of retried every pass — matches this codebase's existing "artifacts are derived, not claimed" distrust-of-unbounded-retry pattern elsewhere. Separately, this exposes a real crash-safety gap in the local Ollama/llama.cpp Vulkan backend itself (a 400 response would be the correct behavior for an over-length input, not a segfault) — outside this app's control, but worth guarding against defensively given it's independently reachable (e.g. any other oversized message, not just this one). **Both gaps implemented**: (1) `chunkText()` (`chunk-text.ts`) now applies a hard `MAX_CHUNK_CHARS = 4000` ceiling (same constant used elsewhere in this codebase for tool-result trimming — a familiar, already-proven-safe size, not a newly-invented number) after paragraph-splitting, via a new `splitOversized()` that hard-slices any paragraph still over the cap — no unbroken blob can reach `embed()` whole regardless of model/provider. (2) `runBackfill` (`indexer.ts`) now tracks consecutive embed failures per message in an in-memory `Map` (`embedFailureCounts`, reset on app restart — a per-process bound, not a permanent record); after `MAX_EMBED_FAILURES_PER_MESSAGE = 3` failures it gives up on that message using the exact same "indexed with no vector" bookkeeping the pre-existing no-searchable-text branch already uses, so it stops being selected by the backfill query instead of retrying forever. New tests: `chunk-text.test.ts` (a 9500-char unbroken paragraph splits into 3 bounded chunks with no content lost; a short real paragraph stays untouched while only an oversized sibling gets split), `indexer.test.ts` (a message whose embed always throws triggers exactly 3 attempts then gets marked given-up; a later backfill pass does not retry it). Full suite 1386/1386, typecheck clean. The underlying Ollama/llama.cpp segfault-on-oversized-input itself is unverified as actually unreachable now (would need a live oversized-input retest against the real local server to fully confirm) — this fix prevents this app from ever sending that input, which is the part within its control regardless.

- [x] 172. Large query-result handling needs a design revisit: honest reporting, intermediate storage, and steering away from raw fetches — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live, same session as #171, investigating a real turbine power-curve/yaw-misalignment question (`turbine_performance`, 52,704 rows for T001/2024) against local `KagentData` (sqlite). **Root cause confirmed by reading the code, not assumed**: all four DB drivers (`sqlite-driver.ts:65`, `mssql-driver.ts:82`, `mysql-driver.ts:71`, `postgres-driver.ts:67`) already hard-cap every `db_execute_sql`/saved-query result to `MAX_RETURNED_ROWS = 200` via `rows.slice(0, MAX_RETURNED_ROWS)` — but `rowCount` reports the real, uncapped total (`rows.length`), and `index.ts:90`'s formatting (`` `${result.rowCount} row(s):\n${JSON.stringify(result.rows,...)}` ``) prints the true count immediately before the truncated body, with no truncation notice anywhere. So the model (and every later consumer) sees e.g. "52704 row(s):" followed by only the first 200 — a confident, wrong claim of completeness, not an absence of a cap. Traced downstream: `handoff.ts`'s `describeCapture()` (`:124`) regex-parses that same misleading header into the handoff file's `summary` field, so a later Developer step reading `.sachiv-handoff/<turn>/NN-db_execute_sql.json` for real computation is told "52704 rows" while the file on disk genuinely has 200 (confirmed directly: the captured file for this session's first query had exactly 200 rows, covering only 2024-01-01 00:00 through 2024-01-02 09:10 — 1.4 days of a requested full year). A real power-curve/yaw-misalignment analysis built on this would silently analyze 1.4 days and report it as the full year. Same mechanism, smaller scale, is the root cause of #171's oversized chunk (that incident's "234 row(s)" message is this same capped-driver shape, just small enough to stay under the fold). **Live-observed mid-session**: once the model had the schema, it spontaneously wrote a binned aggregate query (`ROUND(wind_speed_ms,1) AS wind_bin, AVG(active_power_kw), AVG(theoretical_power_kw), AVG(yaw_error)...`) instead of a raw pull — the right instinct — but even that came back as 268 rows and got silently truncated by the same cap, cutting it off above roughly the top few wind-speed bins, with the same misleading uncapped header. **Decided 2026-10-02 (Deepak): needs a real design revisit, not a quick patch, covering three threads together:**
  - **First pass (threads 2 & 3 only)**: a shared `formatQueryResult()` made the truncation honest ("N of TOTAL row(s) shown — TRUNCATED...") and `db_execute_sql`'s description nudged toward aggregation — but still returned the large (now up to a 100,000-row driver-level safety backstop, `MAX_SAFETY_ROWS`) result inline, relying on downstream layers (the provider-context trim, a new `search_history` size-skip) to stop it from actually reaching the model/index in full.
  - **User correction, same day: "we should not put the large data in context."** Right call — producing a large result and relying on other code to contain it is exactly backwards; large data should never become message/conversation/UI content at all. **Redesigned and implemented**: `formatQueryResult` (`tools/db/types.ts`) now takes a `workspaceRoot` and is async. A result at or under `DISPLAY_ROW_LIMIT = 200` rows is returned exactly as before (byte-for-byte unchanged for every normal-sized query). A larger one is written straight to `resources/db_export_<id>.json` in the workspace, and the tool returns only a small, honest summary — the real row count, a 5-row sample, and the file's path — which is all that ever becomes message/context/stored content; the real data lives only in that file. All four call sites (`db_execute_sql`, `db_list_rows`, `db_find_rows`, saved queries) updated to pass `ctx.workspaceGuard.root` and await the new async call — `db_list_rows`/`db_find_rows` already self-limit to ≤200 via their own SQL `LIMIT`/`TOP`, so this branch is reachable in practice mainly through `db_execute_sql` and saved queries. The driver-level cap (`MAX_SAFETY_ROWS = 100_000`, all four `*-driver.ts`, raised from the old `MAX_RETURNED_ROWS = 200`) is now purely a memory safety backstop against a truly pathological query, not a UX cap — a real analytical pull (a full year of 10-minute-interval telemetry, the motivating case) comfortably fits under it and gets its complete, real data into the export file, closing thread 1. `handoff.ts`'s `describeCapture()` fix (counting the real parsed JSON array length instead of regex-guessing from header text) and the `search_history` indexing size-skip (`MAX_INDEXABLE_CHARS`, `indexer.ts`) both stay in place as independent, still-valuable defense-in-depth for any *other* capturable tool (`web_search`, `parse_csv`, `api_call`, `xlsx_read`) that might also occasionally produce an oversized single result — only the DB path itself no longer depends on them. `handoff.ts`'s `MAX_CAPTURE_BYTES` was raised to 20MB then reverted back to its original 2MB once large DB results stopped flowing through that generic capture path at all. New/updated tests: `sqlite-driver.test.ts` (a 5000+-row query returns the real full count, not capped at 200 — confirms the driver-level change), `index.test.ts` (a 300-row `db_execute_sql` result writes the complete data to a real file and returns only a small summary — reads the file back and confirms every row, including the last one, is genuinely there and never appears in the returned text), `indexer.test.ts` (an oversized message is skipped from embedding entirely, provider never called), existing `crud.test.ts`/`saved-query-tools.test.ts`/`handoff.test.ts` all still pass unchanged (small-result behavior is identical to before). Full suite 1389/1389, typecheck clean (`npm run typecheck`, node + svelte-check). No live re-verification yet against the real app.
  1. **Intermediate/full-data storage** — a real path for a later step (Developer/`python_run`) to get genuine row-level data when a task actually needs it (this one does — a correct yaw-misalignment analysis needs the whole year, not 200 rows or a lossy bin). Likely a dedicated full-export mechanism (query result written straight to a workspace file, e.g. CSV/Parquet, bypassing the 200-row conversational cap entirely) rather than ever routing bulk row-level data back through chat-shaped `db_execute_sql` output. A temp-SQLite-table alternative was considered and set aside for now — no current consumer needs SQL-shaped follow-up queries on handoff data; a plain file is simpler and matches how `python_run` already consumes handoff JSON today.
  2. **Proper/honest reporting** — stop the header lying about completeness: the row-count line and `HandoffDataFile.summary` must reflect what's actually present ("200 of 52704 rows, truncated — ...") wherever a cap is applied, not the pre-cap total. Applies equally to the 268-row aggregate case above, which is silently losing distinct values too, not just raw rows.
  3. **Steering toward aggregation** — `db_execute_sql`'s tool description and/or the Data Access role prompt should nudge toward `GROUP BY`/binned SQL for trend-and-pattern questions (power curves, time-series analysis) so a meaningful summary fits the cap by design, rather than relying on an arbitrary first-N-by-row-order slice of raw data. The live session already showed a local model reaching for this on its own once it had the schema — worth reinforcing, not inventing from nothing.
  All three now implemented — see the two bullets above for the actual final design (thread 1's "dedicated full-export mechanism" above was the right instinct; what shipped is exactly that, a file write from the DB tool itself, not the temp-SQLite-table alternative that was set aside).

- [x] 173. Compound "domain knowledge + data" question never dispatched an MCP Operator step — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live, same session as #171/#172 (turbine power-curve/yaw-misalignment question against `KagentData`, with `llmwiki` connected over the fresh §13.3 RefDocs workspace). **Confirmed via `tool_usage_log`, not assumed**: every tool call across the whole session (8 calls over ~35 minutes) is tagged `agent_role = 'data'` — zero `mcp-operator` calls at any point. The connected `llmwiki` MCP server was never touched once, despite the request asking to compare against a "reference power curve" and look for "evidence of yaw misalignment," both domain-interpretation questions. **Confirmed the wiki genuinely has relevant content** (queried the live FTS index directly): the ingested `Lee_Fields_2021_loss_waterfall_taxonomy.md` has direct hits for both "yaw misalignment" ("effect of wake position and yaw misalignment on power loss...") and "power curve" ("generic power curve adjustment loss...") — this isn't a case of the wiki having nothing on-topic. **Confirmed the planning instruction already demands this shape** (`orchestrator.ts:1503-1505`): "Domain knowledge (rules/constraints, or how to interpret something — from a wiki/MCP server...) together with the data those rules apply to (database/API) is always compound, one step per source, even in a single sentence." The rule exists, is strongly worded, and was not followed. **Plausible contributing factor, not confirmed as root cause**: `db_introspect_schema` surfaced a literal `theoretical_power_kw` column on `turbine_performance`, which may have led the model to treat "reference power curve" as fully satisfiable from data alone — a defensible call for that half only; it doesn't explain skipping domain knowledge for interpreting "evidence of yaw misalignment," which has no definition anywhere in the data and is exactly what the wiki's loss-taxonomy document covers. **Caveat from the initial finding, now resolved**: the first observation ran on local `gpt-oss-custom` (confirmed via `/api/ps`), raising the question of whether this was a local-model/resource-starvation compliance gap rather than a universal one. **Retested same-day against cloud `gpt-4o-mini`** (OpenAI) with the identical prompt and `llmwiki` connection: the plan again omitted any `mcp-operator` step (confirmed via `tool_usage_log` — every call tagged `agent_role='data'`), ruling out "local model only" — this is a real planning-prompt-compliance gap on a capable cloud model too, not a local-model artifact. (That same retest also separately surfaced a genuine tool-call infinite loop — 18+ identical `db_list_rows` calls with no new information — and a UI hang on a `warning`-only turn, tracked separately as #174.)

**Implemented**: moved the judgment from prose-only to a structured, provider-enforced field plus code-level insertion, rather than further sharpening the prose (which the planning instruction already stated strongly and which still wasn't followed on two different models). `buildPlanSchema` (`orchestrator.ts`) gained two new required boolean fields alongside `complexity`: `needsDomainKnowledge` and `needsUserData`, each with a schema `description` guiding the model's judgment (the `needsDomainKnowledge` one explicitly covers the "is there evidence of X" interpretation-question shape that triggered this incident). `Plan`/`parsePlan` carry both through, defaulting to `false` (not rejecting the whole plan) when a model's reply omits them — same "fails open" convention every other field here already uses, so the plain-prompt fallback path (a model that rejected structured output) degrades safely. New `shouldInsertDomainKnowledgeStep`/`ensureDomainKnowledgeStep` (`orchestrator.ts`, next to the existing `substituteUnavailableMcpOperator`/`applyMcpAvailabilityFallback` #145/#146 pair, same file, same pattern): when `needsDomainKnowledge` is true, no step already targets `mcp-operator`, and a server is actually connected (`isRoleAvailable`, the same live-fact check #145/#146 use — never inserts a step that would just report "no connected server fits"), a domain-knowledge step is inserted at the front of the plan in code. The 1-step-plan shortcut (`run()`, skips `executePlan` entirely for a plan with ≤1 step) had to be widened to also check `shouldInsertDomainKnowledgeStep`, since the common failure shape is a model planning exactly one (non-mcp) step — without this, the insertion logic inside `executePlan` would never even be reached. The planning prompt's own prose rule was trimmed (not deleted) to point at the new field rather than repeat the full explanation, keeping token cost roughly flat despite the new mechanism. `needsUserData` is captured and parsed but **deliberately not enforced** — no demonstrated incident of a plan omitting a needed data step the way this one omitted the domain-knowledge step, so enforcement there would be speculative; revisit if a real case turns up. New tests (`orchestrator.test.ts`): inserts the missing step when needed+missing+available; does not insert when no server is connected (leaves the no-op single-step path untouched); does not insert a duplicate when the model's own plan already included one. `prompt-budget.test.ts`'s `classifyPrompt` ceiling needed no increase (the prose trim offset the new schema field-description cost); `db_execute_sql`'s own small description addition (part of #172) needed `routerToolSchemas`/`routerBare` raised by 10 tokens each, reason recorded in that test file. Full suite 1381/1381, typecheck clean. No live Electron re-verification yet — next manual-test pass should re-run this exact scenario live to confirm the inserted step actually produces a correct, wiki-grounded answer end-to-end, not just that the plan now contains the step.

- [x] 174. A `warning`-only turn leaves the chat UI permanently stuck (composer frozen, Stop does nothing) — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live, same test pass, while retrying #173's question against OpenAI. Repro: switch active provider to OpenAI before a model is set, send a message — `runChat` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts:696`) throws `"No model configured for openai. Set one in Settings."` before any stream/model call ever starts. User then set the model and tried to continue — no effect; Stop also had no effect. **Confirmed directly from `sachiv.db`**: the turn's only persisted message is `[{"type":"warning","message":"This request failed before completing — No model configured for openai...","failureCategory":"communication_failure"}]` — no further message was ever added after the user set the model and tried to continue, confirming the app genuinely stopped accepting/advancing the turn rather than just being slow. **Root cause confirmed by reading the code**: `App.svelte`'s stream-event handler has three relevant branches — `error` (~405-409) and `message_stop` (~410-417) both correctly do `msg.pending = false`, `runtime.sending = false`, `unsubscribe()`; the `warning` branch (~388-394) does none of that, only pushes the warning segment. Once a turn's terminal signal is a bare `warning`, `runtime.sending` never resets, which: (1) permanently locks the composer into `ChatView.svelte:579`'s "steering note" mode instead of a normal send box (matches "did not continue the work"); (2) makes `stopGeneration()` (`App.svelte:423`) a no-op, since it only calls `cancelChat` on a stream that's already finished server-side — nothing comes back to reset `sending` (matches "not responding to Stop"); (3) leaves the last live `statusText` ("Data Access agent is reviewing tool results...") on screen forever, since nothing ever updates it again. **Open question, not yet resolved**: `register.ts:539-556`'s catch handler emits *both* an `error` event and a `warning` event for this exact failure (in that order) — the `error` handler alone should have reset `sending` correctly, so there may be a second contributing issue (event ordering/delivery, or something swallowing the `error` event) beyond the `warning` handler's missing cleanup; not confirmed, would need live renderer debugging (devtools) to pin down, not just static reading. **Not yet fixed.** Minimum fix: give the `warning` branch the same `msg.pending = false; runtime.sending = false; unsubscribe()` cleanup `error`/`message_stop` already have (a `warning` is informational content, not necessarily turn-ending, so this may need a signal from the backend distinguishing "this warning ends the turn" vs "this warning is mid-turn" — e.g. #171/#9.x's informational no_information-style warnings are likely NOT meant to end the turn — rather than a blanket change). Separately worth a recovery-path hardening regardless of root cause: `stopGeneration()` should probably force-reset local `sending`/`pending` state after some timeout even if no backend acknowledgement ever arrives, so a stuck turn is always recoverable from the UI without a full app restart. **Implemented**: the open question above resolved itself into the actual fix — rather than a blanket "every warning ends the turn" change, found that the backend already has exactly the distinguishing signal needed and already uses it elsewhere: `failureCategory` (`shared/protocol.ts`'s `warning` event/`ContentBlock` variant) is set only on a genuinely turn-ending warning (`communication_failure`, `stopped_by_user`, `no_answer`) and left `undefined` on an informational mid-turn one (the pinned-role-fallback notice at `orchestrator.ts:994`, the dropped-context-turns FYI at `register.ts:757`) — confirmed by reading every `type: 'warning'` emit site in `src/main`, not assumed; `prepare-context.ts`'s own `failedTurnWarning()` already uses this exact same presence check for an unrelated purpose (deciding whether a past turn counts as failed for context-trimming), so this is an established convention, not a new one invented for this fix. `App.svelte`'s `warning` branch now checks `event.failureCategory`: when present, it does the identical `msg.pending = false; runtime.sending = false; unsubscribe()` cleanup `error`/`message_stop` already have; when absent, behavior is unchanged (the turn continues normally). This resolves the originally-reported repro (misconfigured model) and, found during the same investigation, a more common one: the user manually clicking Stop mid-run also ends the turn on a bare `failureCategory: 'stopped_by_user'` warning with no paired `error`/`message_stop`, which hit the identical hang — now also fixed by the same change. The `stopGeneration()` timeout-based hardening (defense in depth for a future, still-undiscovered terminal-warning shape) was not added this pass — the root cause is now addressed directly via the real distinguishing signal, so a blind timeout fallback would be solving a problem that (as far as currently known) no longer exists; revisit only if a new stuck-turn shape turns up. No automated test added — this codebase has no existing test harness for `App.svelte`-level behavior (only plain `.ts` utility modules are unit-tested anywhere in `src/renderer`), so introducing one was out of scope for this fix; verified via a clean `npm run typecheck` (`svelte-check`, 0 errors) and full backend suite (1386/1386) instead. Live re-verification in the real running app (both the original misconfigured-model repro and a mid-run Stop click) is still needed to confirm the fix actually unsticks the composer, not just that it compiles.

- [x] 175. A round truncated mid-tool-call by the output-token limit failed the whole step outright, with no recovery — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live retesting #173's fix against cloud `gpt-4o-mini`: Developer's response hit the 16,384-token per-round output limit while writing a `python_run` call, leaving a garbage/empty call (`python_run {}`, no `code` at all) — confirmed via `orchestrator.ts`'s own existing `stopReason === 'max_tokens'` handling (`:2013-2029`) that this was never actually executed (the function returned immediately with `isError: true` before ever reaching tool dispatch), it just ended the step with "(no response)" and no chance to recover. Likely triggered by the exact scenario #172's python_run/role-prompt fix targets (embedding ~250 real rows as literal data in the generated script instead of reading the handoff file), though the truncated text itself was never persisted (the JSON never finished), so this can't be proven with certainty — only inferred from the dataset size and timing. **Implemented**: `runLoop` (`orchestrator.ts`) now checks, right after a round's dangling `tool_use` blocks are pushed to `conversation`: if `stopReason === 'max_tokens'` and at least one tool call was in progress (the clearest signal the cut-off was mid-call, not a finished thought) and a bounded retry budget remains (`MAX_TRUNCATED_TOOL_CALL_RETRIES = 1`, same pattern as the existing `MAX_EMPTY_RESPONSE_RETRIES`), each dangling tool call gets a real, informative `tool_result` ("cut off by the output-length limit... produce a SHORTER response... read it from its file instead of embedding it") rather than being executed with whatever garbage survived truncation — every real provider requires a matching result for every `tool_use`, so this also keeps the conversation well-formed instead of risking a 400 on the next request. The round then retries once with that explanation already in context; a second truncation falls through to the prior behavior (fail the round, `isError: true`) rather than retrying forever. New tests (`orchestrator.test.ts`): a truncated tool call recovers into a real final answer within one retry (confirms the garbage call was never executed — a real `read_file` error on a missing path would read differently — and that exactly 2 requests were made, not more); a second consecutive truncation still fails cleanly after exactly one retry, not an unbounded loop. Full suite 1383/1383, typecheck clean. No live re-verification yet against the real app — next pass should re-run the exact #172/#173 scenario again to confirm this actually recovers the Developer step live, not just in the mocked-provider test.

Continued. Year Est/ Category Subcategory Avg M..."), the other a bibliography citation ("Kassebaum, J.: Power Curve Testing with Remote Sensing..."). The rule existed; the model never judged that it applied, because having *some* search hits (even irrelevant ones) read as "found it" rather than triggering a relevance check against the actual content. **Implemented**: `mcpOperatorPrompt()` (`roles.ts`) strengthened with this exact found-live story — explicitly says to judge relevance from the real returned content, not from the call having "succeeded," names the table-fragment/citation case as a concrete example of what "not relevant" looks like, and instructs retrying with a different query or reading further *before* falling back to `no_information`. `prompt-budget.test.ts`'s `role:mcp-operator` ceiling raised (390 -> 520, reason recorded in-file) to cover it. - [x] 177. Data Access never recognized a real `theoretical_power_kw` reference column already in the schema, so a "reference power curve" comparison ended up comparing the turbine's real data against a second pull of its own real data — and the resulting chart was a meaningless zigzag because that mis-identified "reference" data got plotted as an unsorted line — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live in the same end-to-end retest as #176. **Confirmed directly from the real SQL**: step 2 queried `turbine_performance` for T001/2024 (the real data, correct); step 3, labeled "Retrieve the reference power curve for comparison," ran `SELECT wind_speed_ms, active_power_kw FROM turbine_performance WHERE turbine_id = 'T001' AND timestamp BETWEEN '2024-01-01' AND '2024-12-31' ORDER BY timestamp` — the same table, same turbine, same year, never touching `theoretical_power_kw` (confirmed present in `db_introspect_schema`'s own earlier output in this exact session). The Data role prompt already had a closely-related rule from a past incident ("match a named quantity to the column whose name/semantics actually mean that... rather than substituting a similarly-shaped column") — but it didn't trigger recognition that "reference" is a synonym for a column literally named `theoretical_*`. Downstream consequence, traced to the Developer step's own generated code: since the "reference" dataset was just more real, noisy, non-monotonic data (not a genuine reference curve), and the chart code plotted it as a connected line in its original timestamp order (not sorted by wind speed) rather than a scatter, the result was the chaotic red zigzag crossing the whole plot — confirmed by viewing the actual generated chart image, not assumed. **Implemented**: two separate, independently-justified prompt fixes, both reinforcing already-present role rules rather than inventing new mechanisms. (1) Data role prompt (`roles.ts`): the existing column-matching rule now explicitly calls out that "reference"/"expected"/"theoretical"/"rated" name the same kind of column, with this exact incident as the found-live example, and says to check the schema for a matching column before assuming a second query is even needed. (2) Developer/coding role prompt (`roles.ts`): new short rule next to the existing matplotlib guidance — sort each series by its own x-axis value before drawing it as a line; real/noisy data left in timestamp order and plotted as a line makes a meaningless zigzag, use a scatter for noisy raw data instead. `prompt-budget.test.ts`'s `role:data`/`role:coding` ceilings raised (2640 -> 2737, 2760 -> 2801; reasons recorded in-file) to cover both. Full suite 1389/1389, typecheck clean. Not yet re-verified live.

- [x] 178. Domain-information acquisition had no way to say "found something, but not enough" — a partial result silently passed as `done`, and even `no_information`'s existing web fallback was only ever a hopeful note, not a guarantee — Implemented 2026-10-02. User-proposed design (verbatim intent, 2026-10-02): (1) get domain info from available sources (MCP first); (2) fall back to web if no source; (3) analyze what was gathered for completeness; (4) if still short, make further MCP/web calls; (5) if still short after that, ask the user or propose how to proceed; all coordinated so "nothing falls through the crack." **Grounded against the real architecture before building**: `HandoffStatus` already had `done`/`needs_input`/`out_of_scope`/`no_information`/`failed`, each either decided in code or self-reported via a `[handoff: ...]` tag (`handoff.ts`). `no_information` (#143/#144) already covers step 2 reasonably (a step that found *nothing* gets a fallback note suggesting a later round try `web_search`/Research) — but it's a passive note the top-level model might or might not act on, not an enforced action, and it only fires for *zero* results — a *partial* result (exactly #176/#177's live incident: real hits from the right document, but useless snippets) had no distinct status at all and just reported `done`. There is no separately-dispatched "Planner" role in the running system — `classifyAndPlan`/`executePlan` (`orchestrator.ts`) already *is* that coordination layer, so this extends it rather than building a new one. **Implemented, three pieces:**
  1. **New `incomplete` handoff status** (`handoff.ts`): `HandoffStatus`/`ParsedTag`/the `TAG` regex all extended to recognize `[handoff: incomplete]`, parsed identically to the existing tags (same tail-anchored regex, same code path) — distinct from `no_information` (found nothing) and `done` (fully answered). Taught to the two roles that gather information for a later step: MCP Operator's prompt (`roles.ts`) now says to fall back to `incomplete` (with what's still missing) when refining/reading further still leaves only a thin result, instead of forcing a `done`/`no_information` binary that has no room for "found something, but it's thin." Research's prompt got the same addition for when it's filling a gap and the web only partly closes it too.
  2. **Code-enforced follow-up, not a hopeful note** (`orchestrator.ts`): new `ensureFollowUpOnIncomplete`, placed next to the existing `substituteUnavailableMcpOperator`/`ensureDomainKnowledgeStep` reactive-step mechanisms (#145/#146/#173) — when a step reports `incomplete`, it's `mcp-operator`, a Research agent exists, and the user hasn't restricted sourcing, a real Research step is **spliced into the live `steps` array** right after the incomplete one (JS array iterators read the array fresh each `.next()`, so a mid-loop splice is picked up on the very next iteration — confirmed, not assumed, via the new tests below) — not just a note hoping a later round picks it up. Bounded by a new `state.handoff.incompleteFollowUps` counter, capped at 1 per turn, same `redirects`-style cap this file already uses for `out_of_scope` ping-pong — once exhausted (or the reporting role isn't `mcp-operator`, e.g. the inserted Research follow-up itself comes back incomplete), it falls through to piece 3 instead of retrying forever. The step's own marker/fallback-note text (same place `no_information`'s already lives) reports `(PARTIAL INFORMATION — found something, but not enough)` and says explicitly whether a follow-up was actually inserted or whether none is available, so a later round never has to guess.
  3. **Final synthesis must disclose, not paper over**: `buildOrchestratorSystemPrompt()` (the system prompt for the post-plan synthesis round) now requires that if any step ended PARTIAL INFORMATION, the final answer says plainly what could and couldn't be confirmed and proposes how to close the gap or asks the user — directly targets the live incident where the final answer was a shallow "you can examine the plot" despite the domain-knowledge step having openly admitted its search came up thin.
  **New tests** (`orchestrator.test.ts`): an `incomplete` MCP step inserts a real Research step (verified via `provider.requests` count — 5 calls, not the original 4 — and the step's own description references the real gap, not a generic placeholder); the cap holds across two separate `incomplete` steps in one plan (only one Research step ever gets inserted, confirmed via a text-based count, and the second incomplete step's own note correctly says no further source is available rather than falsely promising a follow-up). Full suite 1391/1391, typecheck clean (node + svelte-check). `prompt-budget.test.ts` ceilings raised for `routerPrompt`/`routerBare`/`role:research`/`role:mcp-operator` (reasons recorded in-file).
  **Known scope boundary, not yet closed**: this mechanism only fires inside `executePlan` (a plan with 2+ steps or a 1-step plan whose `needsDomainKnowledge` flag already forces it through, per #173's own gating fix) — the single-step/direct-`delegate_to_mcp-operator` path (the #146 case: a plain "look this up in the wiki" request that never builds a compound plan at all) has no equivalent reactive mechanism today, matching `no_information`'s own existing asymmetry (its fallback note is also `executePlan`-only) rather than introducing a new one. Closing that gap, if a real incident ever shows it matters, is separate follow-up work.
  **Also not yet verified live** — built and tested against the mocked-provider test suite only; the next real run should confirm an `incomplete`-tagged MCP step actually triggers a real inserted Research step against a live model and connected wiki, not just in the test harness. The live checklist UI (the plan step list shown while a turn is running) was not updated to reflect a step spliced in after the initial `plan` stream event — the backend mechanism (the step genuinely runs, its result genuinely reaches the final synthesis) works regardless, but the live UI may not visually show the inserted step in the running checklist; a cosmetic follow-up, not a functional gap, if it turns out to matter in practice.

- [x] 179. Planner proactively planned a redundant web-search step alongside an MCP Operator step for a single, pure domain-knowledge question — web should be a reactive fallback (or user-requested first choice), never a parallel step planned "just in case" — Implemented 2026-10-02. Found live: asked "What is the CERC convention for calculating PLF, and what does the 8,766-hour figure represent?" (no database/data component at all — a pure definitional question) with `llmwiki` connected. **Confirmed directly from `sachiv.db`**: the plan had 2 steps from the start — `[Plan step 1 — MCP Operator]` and `[Plan step 2 — Researcher]` — not a reactive insertion (`tool_usage_log` shows 3 real MCP calls: 2 searches + a `read` on the matched document, producing a genuine, detailed, substantive answer about the CERC convention with no `[handoff: no_information]`/`[handoff: incomplete]` tag). The web step then ran immediately after anyway, found nothing useful (DuckDuckGo's free API returned no instant answer for either query), and the final synthesis had to reconcile a real wiki answer with two failed web searches that never needed to happen. Distinct from #178 (which handles the *reactive* case — a step that turns out insufficient after running); this is the *plan itself* proactively scheduling two sources for one need before either had run. **Implemented**: the planning prompt's existing compound-step rule (`orchestrator.ts`, the same rule #173 added `needsDomainKnowledge` next to) now explicitly distinguishes "domain knowledge + the data it applies to" (genuinely two different kinds of source — correctly compound) from "domain knowledge from two sources for the same question" (not compound, redundant) — never plan MCP Operator and Research together for one domain-knowledge need up front; add web up front only if the user asked for it or for outside sources explicitly; an insufficient wiki result is handled after the fact via the existing `no_information`/`incomplete` tags (#143/#144/#178), not by hedging with both from the start. `prompt-budget.test.ts`'s `classifyPrompt` ceiling raised (1150 -> 1255, reason recorded in-file) to cover it. Full suite 1391/1391, typecheck clean (node + svelte-check). **No new automated test** — same as #176/#177 (also prompt-only advisory fixes), this is a planning *judgment* a scripted mock can't meaningfully exercise (it would just replay whatever plan JSON is scripted, regardless of the prompt); verified only by confirming the instruction text is present and measured (the budget test). Not yet re-verified against a real model — the next live retest of a pure domain-knowledge question (with no explicit "also check the web" phrasing) should confirm the plan now has one step, not two. **Scoped out, not addressed this pass**: the user's broader suggestion that even a genuinely reactive web fallback could *offer* rather than silently auto-run ("suggest the use of web search as additional information source if user agrees") — the existing #143/#144/#178 fallbacks stay automatic-with-disclosure, since that's already built, tested, and not what broke in this incident; changing that to a confirm-gated flow is a separate, bigger design question if it turns out to matter.

Third "Tool output" display state (Hidden), uniform across every tool — not per-tool, by explicit user decision — and Save Session (Markdown/PDF) must reflect it — Implemented 2026-10-02, user request. **Design decisions, both explicit and user-confirmed rather than assumed**: (1) rejected a per-tool classification (e.g. defaulting `find_files`/`read_file`/`python_run` to hidden while other tools behave differently) in favor of one uniform global state covering every tool identically — "I do not want per tool change in the behavior." (2) The third state reuses the *existing* Cmd/Ctrl+O mechanism (cycling through it) rather than adding a Settings-page control — "the third state can just simulate the same (instead of going through the settings page)." (3) An `image` block (e.g. a chart) is the one deliberate exception to "uniform" — a content-type rule, not a per-tool one: "Hidden should show the image as it is important part of the output. That's the main point." (4) Save Session must reflect the same display state — confirmed live by reading the actual export code first, not assumed: neither `sessionToMarkdown` nor the PDF path (`PrintView.svelte`, an entirely separate rendering component from the live chat view) respected any display state before this change; both rendered full tool detail unconditionally. **Implemented**: `App.svelte`'s old `toolDetailsExpanded: boolean` replaced throughout (App.svelte, ChatView.svelte) with `toolDisplayMode: 'hidden' | 'partial' | 'shown'`, persisted under a new `toolDisplayMode` setting key (the old boolean key is simply abandoned — a UI preference resetting to default on upgrade is harmless, no migration needed). Cmd/Ctrl+O and the status-bar button now cycle hidden → partial → shown → hidden instead of toggling two states; 'partial' is the default, preserving today's exact original collapsed-by-default behavior unchanged. `ChatView.svelte`'s tool-call rendering: 'shown' is pixel-identical to the old "expanded" behavior (last-call-auto-expanded, per-block click-to-toggle, unchanged); 'partial' is pixel-identical to the old "collapsed" behavior (name + ✓/✗ status row, non-interactive); 'hidden' is new — suppresses the entire tool-call row (head, input, result) — except an `image` block, which still renders exactly as before in every state, matching the explicit exception above. Thinking blocks (which already shared the same toggle) follow the same shown/not-shown split; a 'hidden' or 'partial' state doesn't suppress the thinking row entirely (scope was "Tool output" specifically, not thinking) — it stays at the collapsed presentation either way, only 'shown' unlocks the full text. Export: `sessionToMarkdown` (`sessions/export.ts`) gained a `mode` parameter, defaulting to `currentToolDisplayMode()` (reads the same persisted `toolDisplayMode` setting directly via `getSetting`, since export runs in the main process regardless of whether a chat window is even open) — 'hidden' omits tool_use/tool_result blocks entirely, 'partial' shows name/status only, 'shown' is the original unfiltered behavior; an `image` block is emitted in every mode, unconditionally. `PrintView.svelte` (the separate PDF-rendering component) got the identical mode-aware treatment for consistency between the two human-readable export formats — and, found while doing this, a real **pre-existing gap unrelated to this feature**: it never rendered `image` blocks *at all*, in any state, before this change (a chart was silently absent from every PDF export) — fixed as part of the same pass since it's squarely the same "does an image show up" question. `sessionToJson` deliberately stays exempt from `toolDisplayMode` — its own doc comment already called it "Full-fidelity dump of exactly what's in the DB," and filtering that would contradict its stated purpose; flagged explicitly rather than silently applied, in case the user disagrees. New/updated tests (`export.test.ts`): the two pre-existing tests that checked full tool_use/tool_result content now pass `'shown'` explicitly (preserving their original intent — they test Markdown *formatting*, not the new filtering); new tests cover 'hidden' (omits tool content, keeps the image), 'partial' (name/status only, never the real input/result), and that `exportSession` genuinely reads the persisted setting rather than a hardcoded default. Full suite 1398/1398, typecheck clean (node + svelte-check). Not yet verified live — next pass should confirm cycling Cmd/Ctrl+O through all three states looks right in the real app, and that a real Markdown/PDF export in each state matches.

Traced to a genuine gap in `McpManager`/`registerIpcHandlers` (`src/main/mcp/manager.ts`, `src/main/ipc/register.ts`): every `stdio` MCP server connection spawns a real child process (`StdioClientTransport`), but there was no `app.on('before-quit', ...)` (or any other) shutdown hook that ever closed those clients — `McpManager.disconnect()` existed but was only ever called for one server at a time (Settings' "Remove"/"Reconnect" buttons), never for all of them on app quit. Confirmed live via `ps`: every quit or dev-mode main-process restart left the previous `stdio` child process running as an orphan (reparented off the dead main process, not to it), and gbrain's own strict single-writer PGLite lock then correctly refused the *next* launch's fresh connection outright — the exact error the user saw. Non-locking servers (llm-wiki-mcp, llmwiki) have the identical underlying leak, just silently: confirmed via `ps` a genuinely orphaned `llmwiki` `local_server.py` process left over from this same session's own earlier verification testing (cleaned up manually; not caused by the app, by an unclosed throwaway script, but the identical shape of bug). **Fixed**: new `McpManager.disconnectAll(registry)` closes every connected server's client (the same real cleanup `disconnect()` already does, just for all of them), wired to a new `app.on('before-quit', () => mcpManager.disconnectAll(toolRegistry))` in `registerIpcHandlers()` — covers the well-behaved quit path (menu quit, window-all-closed, Cmd/Ctrl+Q); a hard kill/crash still can't be caught by any in-process hook, the same residual gap any child-process cleanup has, not specific to MCP. 2 new tests in `manager.test.ts` (`disconnectAll` closes every client and unregisters every tool; a no-op when nothing's connected) — 849/849 full suite passing, typecheck clean. Not yet live-verified against a full quit/relaunch cycle of the user's real running dev session (deliberately not restarted out from under them mid-session); takes effect on the app's next natural restart.

Confirmed false by the same session's own next exchange: the identical tool, called correctly seconds later once the user supplied `energy_wiki` themselves, worked immediately and returned the real page list. Checked the rest of this session's history and 3 other real sessions for the same shape of failure (a failed tool call immediately followed by a plain-text give-up response) before concluding this was isolated: one other match was an unrelated delegate's real, successful answer coincidentally adjacent to an unrelated error in the transcript ordering; the other two were the already-fixed `shell_execute`/coding-role-boundary bug from earlier in this file, both predating that fix — no other live, current instance of this exact pattern found. **Fixed**: `SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`src/main/ipc/register.ts`) gained an explicit instruction, with this exact incident as the named example (matching this file's own established "cite the real incident" convention) — a failed tool call is not the same as the capability not existing; read what the error actually says, and a validation/missing-argument/bad-value error means retry with a corrected argument (via the tool's own `inputSchema`, or by first calling a listing/lookup tool to find the right value) before ever concluding a capability is missing. Deliberately a prompt-level fix, not a code-level one: unlike a mechanically-decidable pattern (e.g. capping repeated identical failures), "what's the right corrected argument" is a judgment call an arbitrary MCP server's own error shape can't be mechanically parsed into a fix for. No existing test file covers `SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s content (it isn't exported, and no `register.test.ts` exists) so none was added just for this string — typecheck clean, full suite still 849/849 passing (unaffected). Not yet re-verified live against a real running session with the fix in place.

## Context management & token economy
- [x] 46. Exact token counting per provider (tiktoken for OpenAI, provider-native counts elsewhere) — tiktoken (js-tiktoken, o200k_base) for OpenAI and, as a documented approximation-of-an-approximation, Ollama; real API-based counts already existed for Anthropic/Google
- [x] 47. Real-time cost estimation per session — approximate, hand-maintained pricing table (`src/shared/model-metadata.ts`); unknown models show "cost unknown" rather than a fabricated number, Ollama models are correctly free
- [x] 48. Context-usage meter in UI (current usage vs. model limit, low-context warning) — tracks cumulative session usage (not live post-trim context size, which #51 now keeps bounded by design) against the model's context window, with a warning state past ~80%
- [ ] 49. Rolling summarization of earlier turns — deferred, needs its own LLM-call/trigger-policy design Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): 0.5.0 (detail); revisit after 0.4.0.
- [x] 97. Account for system-prompt/tool-schema overhead in output-token budgeting — flagged by an external code review (`review.md`), confirmed: `resolveMaxOutputTokens()` computed remaining context purely from `provider.countTokens(conversation)`, never adding the system prompt or tool-definition JSON sent in the same request. Now reuses `overheadTokens` (`prepare-context.ts`, exported — already built for #51's input-side trim) with the top-level orchestrator's own prompt + full default tool registry as a deliberate worst-case estimate, since `state.maxOutputTokens` is computed once and shared across every round/nested delegate call in the run (varying per-round system prompts/tool sets aren't tracked individually). Costs one extra real `countTokens` call per turn for Anthropic/Google (now 2, not 1) — still only at `run()` startup, not per round. New test proves the subtraction is real, not just computed: a scenario picked so the old (conversation-only) math would've hit the model's output ceiling, but the new overhead-aware math lands measurably below it (32768 → 25536).
- [x] 98. Re-fit context against the window before every provider request, not just once per turn — flagged by `review.md`, confirmed: `prepareConversation` (`ipc/register.ts`) ran exactly once before a turn starts; the only within-turn trimming was `capRepeatedToolResults`/`collapseDuplicateToolResults`, which dedupe by tool name and URL overlap, not total conversation size — `executePlan`'s appended step results and an ordinary long multi-round tool-call sequence were never re-checked against the model's context window mid-turn. Deliberately did *not* reuse `applySlidingWindow`'s whole-turn-dropping logic directly mid-turn — `splitIntoTurns` treats each plan-step "user" message as its own turn boundary, and naively re-running it inside `runLoop` risked dropping the turn's own *originating* user message (the oldest "turn") before any plan-step content, which would be actively harmful. Instead added `refitConversationToBudget` (`orchestrator.ts`), called before every `provider.stream()` in both the main round loop and the exhausted-rounds/time wrap-up call: a cheap local char-count estimate (`roughTokenEstimate`) gates whether a real `countTokens` check even runs at all — zero added cost/latency for an ordinary small conversation — and only once genuinely over budget does it collapse the *oldest* still-expanded, non-mutating tool results (never a mutating one) to a placeholder, re-measured with the same cheap local estimate between collapses so it's at most one real `countTokens` call per round even when several results need collapsing. New tests: an old large read-only result gets collapsed while a same-sized mutating one doesn't, and an ordinary small conversation triggers zero extra `countTokens` calls.
- [x] 102. Whole-turn failures no longer silently resent as unanswered "real" context, forever — found live tracing a real Groq session (4 identical retries of the same question): a thrown provider error left literally nothing persisted (the user's message had already been inserted before `orchestrator.run()` even started), a Stop click before any content produced also left nothing, and an empty/no-output round only ever left a UI-only `warning` block — every provider adapter strips `warning` from the wire format entirely, so even that was invisible to the model. Each retry then resent the whole growing, still-unanswered history in full. Iterated through explicit direction to the final design: (1) qualifier comments, categorized (`ContentBlock`/`StreamEvent`'s `warning` variant gained an optional `failureCategory: 'communication_failure' | 'stopped_by_user' | 'no_answer'`); (2) don't just silently strip failures from context — repeated ones specifically should be removed, but (3) don't discard failure information entirely either, since it can help the model choose a different approach. Landed as `collapseFailedTurns` (`prepare-context.ts`, wired into `prepareConversation`): an **isolated** single failed turn is kept but converted into real `text` content (a plain `warning` is normally stripped from the wire format, which would make even a deliberately-kept failure invisible); a **run of 2+ consecutive** failed turns collapses into one synthetic turn — the most recent attempt's own question plus one consolidated note ("attempted N times in a row, failed each time — most recent reason: ...") — never N near-identical resends. Three new failure-producing sites, all top-level-turn-scoped (`isTopLevel`, so a nested specialist's own internal empty round is never mistaken for the whole turn failing): `ipc/register.ts`'s `ChatSend` catch (`communication_failure`, previously persisted nothing at all), `orchestrator.ts`'s three user-Stop-with-nothing-produced branches (`stopped_by_user`, same previous gap), and the existing max_tokens/no-visible-output warnings (`no_answer`, only when there's no partial text either — a genuinely partial-but-useful cut-off response is never treated as a failure). Tool-call denial's existing "The user denied this tool call." — already reaching the model fine, since `tool_result` content was never filtered the way `warning` is — got a `[Rejected by user]` prefix for wording consistency, per explicit direction, with no functional change needed. New tests: `classify-gate`-style isolated unit tests for `collapseFailedTurns` (isolated vs. repeated vs. non-failed cases, never collapsing across a real answer in between), an integration test reproducing the exact live bug shape, and updated orchestrator abort-branch tests to expect the new warning. The `ipc/register.ts` catch-block change has no dedicated unit test (this file has no existing test harness of any kind — Electron IPC glue, tested live elsewhere in this codebase); worth triggering a real failure (e.g. an invalid API key) to live-verify the persisted note actually appears and gets collapsed correctly on a repeated retry.
- [x] 103. A `tool_use` with no matching `tool_result` no longer permanently wedges a session — Implemented 2026-09-25
  - **Found live, from the user's own real chat session, not assumed**: session "Can you tell me what documents we stored in llmwiki?" failed on *every* send with the provider's own raw `400 Not the same number of function calls and responses` (Mistral). Root cause: the app was closed/the stream abandoned while `mcp__llmwiki__list_knowledge_bases` was in flight, so the assistant's `tool_use` was persisted with no `tool_result`. Every later send replays full history, so `toOpenAIMessages` kept emitting that unanswered `tool_calls` entry — invalid wire format for any OpenAI-compatible provider (Anthropic's and Google's formats reject it too). `collapseFailedTurns` (#102) did not catch it: it only detects a turn whose assistant content is *just* a failure `warning`, not one ending in a real `tool_use`.
  - **Audited the real DB for the same shape before concluding it was isolated**: 3 of 5 sessions had it, and not just MCP tools — `delegate_to_terminal_devops` + `delegate_to_coding` (session "This github repo…") and `delegate_to_research` (session "Tell me about Electron…") too.
  - **Fixed** (a code-level wire-format invariant, not a prompt fix): new `repairDanglingToolUses` (`src/main/context/prepare-context.ts`), run first in `prepareConversation`, synthesizes an explicit `isError` `tool_result` ("no result was recorded … outcome is unknown; do not assume it succeeded or failed") for every unanswered `tool_use` — kept rather than stripping the call, so the model still sees that it tried something. Handles a fully dangling call (inserted as its own message directly after the assistant message, so it stays in the same turn for `splitIntoTurns` and the sliding window can't split the pair) and a partially answered parallel batch (only the missing ids are merged into the existing result message). Only affects what's sent; SQLite and the UI transcript are untouched.
  - **Verified**: 6 new tests in `prepare-context.test.ts` (untouched when fully answered, dangling before a later user message, dangling at end of history, partial parallel answer, no input mutation, pair kept in one turn) — 855/855 full suite, typecheck clean. Also run against the real DB via a disposable test (deleted afterward): all 3 wedged sessions had exactly the dangling calls listed above before repair and none after; the 2 healthy sessions were unchanged.
  - **Not covered**: prevention at the source (a hard kill mid-tool-call can't be caught by any in-process hook, so a dangling block can still be *persisted* — it is now just harmless on replay). Not yet re-verified by sending a message in one of the real wedged sessions from the running app (takes effect on next app restart).
  - **Correction, 2026-09-25 (see #105): this fix was incomplete and its verification was flawed.** Only one of the "dangling" calls it flagged (`mcp__llmwiki__list_knowledge_bases`) was truly dangling; the `delegate_to_*` ones were a different shape (a delegate's real result persisted non-adjacent, after the specialist's own messages). The verification checked only for unanswered calls, assuming a result must be in the very next message, so it never looked for orphan results and passed vacuously for that shape. #105 replaces `repairDanglingToolUses` with `repairToolPairs`, which covers both.
- [x] 50. Tool-output trimming after the agent extracts the essential answer — oversized tool_result content capped (~4000 chars) only in what's sent to the provider; full result always stays in SQLite and the UI transcript
- [x] 51. Sliding-window retention (system prompt + recent turns) — drops the oldest complete turns once `provider.countTokens()` exceeds ~70% of the model's context window, never splitting a tool_use/tool_result pair
- [x] 52. Agent-driven relevance selection (decide what enters context, not everything) — a `search_history` tool (Research role), not a silent automatic mechanism: searches the user's own past conversations across all sessions via vector search (`sqlite-vec`), returns tagged excerpts with session/date. Embedding storage/generation is a separate, independently-configured (provider, model) pair from the chat provider — defaults to Ollama (`nomic-embed-text`), reconfigurable to OpenAI/Google (Anthropic has no embeddings API). Degrades cleanly with a clear message when no working embedding model is available, rather than failing silently.

- [x] 62. Mermaid diagram rendering in chat Markdown — real, current version checked via `npm view` before installing (`mermaid@12.0.0`); its transitive `chevrotain`→`lodash-es` dependency chain had 5 real high-severity npm audit findings (code injection / prototype pollution in `lodash-es`'s `_.template`/`_.unset`), fixed via a `package.json` override to `lodash-es@^4.18.1` rather than downgrading mermaid a full major version — `npm audit` clean afterward, same precedent as #73's own dependency-vulnerability handling. `markdown.ts`'s `extractMermaid` reuses the exact "extract before parsing, placeholder, splice back after sanitization" shape `extractMath` already established for KaTeX, rather than a new pattern: a ```mermaid fence (confirmed directly against `marked`'s real output, not assumed — it becomes `<pre><code class="language-mermaid">...`) is pulled out into a `data-mermaid-id`-tagged placeholder div before `DOMPurify.sanitize`, since `mermaid.render()` is async and `renderMarkdown` must stay synchronous for the overwhelming majority of messages with no diagram at all. `mermaid-render.ts` (new, dynamically `import()`-ed — mermaid is large, and most conversations never trigger it) fills each placeholder in with real SVG asynchronously and imperatively, cached by exact diagram source text (not by placeholder id, which isn't stable across a streaming message's repeated full-HTML `{@html}` re-renders), with a real DOMPurify SVG-safe profile (`USE_PROFILES: { svg: true, svgFilters: true }`, per this task's own original note) and graceful per-diagram error handling (a model can produce genuinely invalid diagram syntax) that never breaks the rest of the message. **Two real bugs found only by live verification, neither would have been caught by the jsdom-based unit tests alone (jsdom has no real SVG layout engine — `mermaid.render()` throws `getBBox is not a function` under it, confirmed directly, so the actual rendered-diagram-looks-right behavior is only checked live, the same precedent as #82/#87/#88):** (1) Mermaid's default HTML-label rendering (`<foreignObject>` containing namespaced XHTML `<div>`/`<span>`) silently lost every label's text after DOMPurify's `svg` profile sanitized it — even after explicitly widening `ADD_TAGS`/`ADD_ATTR` to allow `foreignObject`/`div`/`span`/`style`, the sanitized output still left an entirely empty `<foreignObject>` (confirmed by direct inspection of the raw vs. sanitized SVG string, not assumed). Fixed properly, not worked around: `mermaid.initialize({ htmlLabels: false })` forces plain SVG `<text>`/`<tspan>` labels instead, which DOMPurify's stock `svg` profile already handles correctly with zero widening — simpler and tighter than fighting SVG/XHTML namespace sanitization. (2) `mermaid.render()` creates its own hidden `#d<renderId>` sandbox element directly under `<body>` (entirely outside this app's own container) for off-screen layout, and does not reliably clean it up on a genuine parse error — confirmed live as a real, visible stray artifact (Mermaid's own large built-in "bomb icon" error graphic) appearing directly on the page, unrelated to this app's own caught-and-handled error state. Fixed with an explicit `document.getElementById(...)?.remove()` in a `finally` block around every render attempt. New tests: `markdown.test.ts` (extraction, multi-diagram, non-mermaid fences unaffected, placeholder survives sanitization, the — corrected after live testing proved the original assumption wrong — real behavior that an *unclosed* fence still gets extracted since `marked` treats an unterminated GFM fence as running to end-of-input); `mermaid-render.test.ts` (mocking `mermaid` itself, since its real render can't run under jsdom: caching, stale-DOM-node guarding, error handling, real SVG sanitization against an actual script-bearing payload). Live-verified end-to-end against a real, fully isolated Electron instance: a real flowchart and sequence diagram both render with fully legible labels and correct arrow styles, a genuinely invalid third diagram degrades to a clear, styled error message, and zero stray elements exist anywhere under `<body>` outside the app's own root.

- [x] 150. Settings import/export via a new account menu, for enterprise/restricted-network deployment — user request 2026-09-28. Sachivji's primary user base will often be enterprise users in restricted environments that don't allow arbitrary outbound connections; want a mechanism for a system administrator to pre-configure common settings (API/provider keys, MCP servers, database connections) in a file that a user can then import, instead of each user configuring everything from scratch. **UI**: the account row in the sidebar (`App.svelte`'s `.user-row` button, ~line 699) currently does one thing only — `onclick={toggleSettings}` opens the Settings panel directly. Wanted: clicking it instead opens a menu with three items — **Settings** (today's behavior), **Export Settings** (a save-file dialog with a default filename/location — user suggested the workspace root as the default location), **Import Settings** (a file-open dialog to pick a previously exported file, then validate and display what will be imported before applying). **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak):** curated subset only — providers, MCP servers, DB connections; personal UI prefs (theme, `toolDetailsExpanded`, etc.) are excluded, since the goal is admin-provisioned org-level connections, not overriding each user's own UI choices. **No live secrets ever included in an export** — connection *shape* only (host, port, MCP command/args, DB connection string minus credentials), the same "shape without the secret" precedent #155's edit-by-id already establishes (blank-means-unchanged). **Import merges, doesn't overwrite by default**: a colliding entry (same name/id as something already configured) is skipped and flagged as a conflict rather than silently clobbered; one upfront preview/approval for the whole batch (matching the "validate and display before applying" framing already in this item), not per-item confirmation — but conflicts are always called out explicitly. Ready to implement.
  - **Implemented.** New `src/main/settings/import-export.ts`: `buildSettingsExport()` reads `activeProviderId` plus each provider's `model:<id>` setting (only when actually set — an unconfigured provider contributes nothing), every MCP server's shape (`name`/`transport`/`command`/`args`/`url`/`description` — deliberately never `env` or the bearer token, since `env` is a plausible secret-carrier this app has no way to distinguish from ordinary config), and every DB connection's shape (`name`/`engine`/`filePath`/`host`/`port`/`database`/`username` — `SaveDataConnectionInput`'s own `password` field is never read at all, so there's nothing to accidentally leak). `previewSettingsImport()` opens a native file-open dialog, parses and validates the JSON (`formatVersion === 1`), then diffs every entry against current local state (`listServerConfigs()`/`listConnections()`/`getSetting()`) to compute `willImport` per item — an existing same-name server/connection or an already-set provider/model setting is flagged to skip, never silently overwritten. `applySettingsImport()` re-derives the exact same skip/import decisions (never trusts a stale decision handed back from the renderer) and actually writes only the non-conflicting ones via the same `saveServerConfig`/`saveConnection`/`setSetting` every other Settings write already goes through — no new persistence path. New `IpcChannel.SettingsExport`/`SettingsImportPreview`/`SettingsImportApply`, exposed via `preload/index.ts`. New `AccountMenu.svelte` replaces `App.svelte`'s old single-purpose `.user-row` button (which called `toggleSettings` directly) with a small dropdown (Settings / Export Settings… / Import Settings…, closing on an outside click via `<svelte:window onclick>`); Import opens a centered preview overlay listing every entry with an explicit "Import" or "Already configured — skip" verdict before a single batch-apply confirmation, matching the decided "one upfront preview/approval for the whole batch" shape — a genuine overlay/modal here, unlike `ToolConfirmDialog`'s deliberately-inline pattern, since nothing about this flow needs to stay reachable during an in-flight generation the way tool confirmation does.
  - New tests (`src/main/settings/import-export.test.ts`, 8 tests, real in-memory DB + mocked `dialog`/`writeFile`/`readFile`, same pattern as `sessions/export.test.ts`/`mcp/config.test.ts`): export includes only providers with a model actually selected and never leaks a connection's password (asserted by string-searching the whole serialized payload, not just field-by-field); export writes the built payload to the chosen path, and a cancelled save dialog writes nothing; import preview correctly flags a same-name existing server as skip and a new one as import; a non-export JSON file and a genuinely malformed JSON file are both rejected with a plain error, not a throw; a cancelled open dialog returns cleanly; apply imports only the non-conflicting entries and never overwrites an already-set provider/model. Full suite 1367/1367 passing, typecheck clean (main + renderer).
  - **Live-verified** against a real running app (isolated Electron instance, CDP): the account menu renders exactly the 3 decided items. The native save/open file dialogs themselves can't be driven over CDP (they're OS-level, outside the page) — that surface is covered by the mocked-dialog unit tests above instead, the same honestly-flagged limitation already used elsewhere in this project for untestable-from-Linux-CI paths (e.g. `shell.ts`'s Windows branch). Everything reachable live was: calling `applySettingsImport` directly against the real running app's real SQLite-backed settings/MCP/connection stores created a real MCP server and a real DB connection, set `activeProviderId`/`model:ollama` for real, and returned `{imported: 4, skipped: 0}`; re-applying the identical payload a second time correctly returned `{imported: 0, skipped: 4}` with no duplicate server or connection created; the resulting connection DTO was confirmed to carry no password field at all.

Scrollbar visual inconsistency across the app — Implemented 2026-09-29. user request 2026-09-28: the vertical scrollbars in different panes (session list, chat window, user input area) look different from each other. **Correction 2026-09-29**: the original premise here (that `SettingsPanel.svelte` has custom scrollbar CSS other panes lack) was checked and is wrong — a repo-wide search for `webkit-scrollbar`/`scrollbar-width`/`scrollbar-color` in `src/renderer/src` returns zero real hits (the earlier "`.settings-body`, ~line 1372" citation pointed at a code comment that merely mentions the word "scrollbar" in passing, not actual styling); `theme.css` has none either. **There is no existing custom scrollbar styling anywhere in this app today** — every pane is on the unstyled OS/browser default, so this is a fresh styling decision, not a "match Settings" consistency fix. **Decided 2026-09-29 (Deepak):** thin/overlay style (`scrollbar-width: thin` for Firefox, a matching thin `::-webkit-scrollbar` for Chromium/Electron), colors pulled from `theme.css`'s existing tokens rather than hardcoded. Defined **once, globally**, as a base rule in `theme.css` rather than per-component, so every scrollable pane inherits it automatically and this can't re-drift the next time a new pane is added. **Implemented**: a universal `*` selector rule added to `theme.css` (`scrollbar-width: thin`/`scrollbar-color` for Firefox, matching `*::-webkit-scrollbar`/`-track`/`-thumb`/`-thumb:hover` rules for Chromium/Electron), thumb from `var(--border)` (hover: `var(--panel-hover)`), transparent track — applies identically in both light and dark themes since it references the existing theme tokens rather than hardcoded colors. Typecheck clean. Live-verified against a real isolated Electron instance: `getComputedStyle(document.body)` returned `scrollbarWidth: "thin"`, `scrollbarColor: "rgb(51, 51, 49) rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)"` — confirming the rule is active and resolves to the real `--border` token value (`#333331`), not a guess.

Confirmed: this is a **UI-only gap, not a backend one**. Every "Add ..." form (DB: 816-849/`saveConnection()` 86-106; API: 1013-1046/`saveApiConnection()` 263-281; MCP: 962-988/`saveMcpServer()` 195-217) is create-only — none ever passes `id`. The IPC layer already supports full edit-by-id with blank-secret-means-unchanged semantics for all three (`shared/ipc-contract.ts`: `SaveDataConnectionInput` :278-289, `SaveApiConnectionInput` :337-347, `SaveMcpServerInput` :424-435 — each doc comment states omitting `id` creates new, omitting the secret field on an edit leaves the stored value unchanged), so this is mostly a Settings-UI task: an edit-mode toggle per row that prefills the existing "Add ..." form with the entry's current (non-secret) values and calls the same save function with `id` set. **Masking**: every sensitive field today is a bare native `<input type="password">` (`connPassword` :838, `apiSecret` :1025/1027/1030, `mcpToken` :976, etc.) — no custom asterisk rendering and no reveal/show-password toggle exists anywhere in the renderer (checked).
