A reveal-password toggle is explicitly deferred, out of scope for #155 — it's a cross-cutting gap across every password field in the app, not specific to Edit, and belongs as its own TODO if wanted. **Implemented**: each of the three lists (`SettingsPanel.svelte`) now conditionally renders a `<li class="edit-row">` in place of the normal read-only row when that entry's id matches a new per-list `editingConnectionId`/`editingApiConnectionId`/`editingMcpId` state — reusing the exact same form fields (and `save*`/`reset*` functions, now id-aware) the "Add ..." forms already use, rather than a second set of components. The old narrow MCP description-only inline edit (`editingMcpDescriptionId`/`saveMcpDescription`) was removed and fully subsumed by the new full-field edit, which also edits the description. All three secret fields (`connPassword`/`apiSecret`/`mcpToken`) render blank on edit and are only sent if the user actually types something, matching the IPC layer's existing blank-means-unchanged contract. The "+ Add ..." buttons and each list's own reset function now also clear the other's edit/add state, so only one form (an edit-in-place or a fresh "Add") can be open at a time per list. `pendingChanges()` (the "leave Settings" unsaved-entry prompt) updated to also detect an in-progress edit, not just an in-progress add. Typecheck clean (0 errors), full suite 1313/1313 passing (no dedicated `SettingsPanel.svelte` test file exists, matching this component's established pattern of live-only verification). **Live-verified end-to-end against a real, fully isolated Electron instance** (separate `--user-data-dir`, CDP-driven): for all three connection types — prefill shows the entry's real current values with the secret field genuinely blank; changing one field and clicking the edit row's own scoped Save button persists exactly that change while leaving other fields (including the stored secret, confirmed via `hasSecret: true` surviving a blank-secret save) untouched; Cancel discards the edit and returns to the read-only row; re-opening Edit afterward shows the freshly-saved values.
- [x] 182. KaTeX inline math can't wrap internally, pushing the chat panel wider — user report 2026-10-04, in the "smallest prime number of n digits" session: a reply contained a single inline `$...$` expression listing every divisor of 1000000001 (`$1, 7, 11, ..., 5257900001 / \text{etc.}$`), rendered as one unbroken KaTeX span wider than the chat panel. **Root cause confirmed in KaTeX's own CSS** (`node_modules/katex/dist/katex.min.css`), not an app styling gap: `.katex .katex-base{white-space:nowrap;width:min-content}` applies to every formula, inline or display-mode — KaTeX positions fractions/radicals/sub-superscripts relative to each other assuming the whole expression stays one unbroken unit, so force-wrapping it would misplace that layout for any real equation. **Fixed for now**: `MarkdownText.svelte` wraps `.katex`/`.katex-display` in `overflow-x: auto` (the same established pattern `pre`/`table` already use), so it scrolls horizontally in place instead of stretching the panel — matching how GitHub/Jupyter and most other KaTeX-in-markdown renderers handle this; there is no general CSS way to force a formula to wrap. **The deferred alternative, implemented 2026-10-13** (part of a quick-wins pass): `extractMath`'s shared `renderToPlaceholder` (`src/renderer/src/lib/markdown.ts`) now takes an opt-in `plainFallback` flag, passed only by the single-`$` call site (never by an explicit `\(...\)`/`\[...\]`/`$$...$$` delimiter, which is the model deliberately asking for real math) — past 60 characters it skips KaTeX and returns the literal, HTML-escaped `$source$` text instead, which wraps normally like any other text. Trade-off accepted as before: a rare genuinely-long real equation written with bare `$` now shows as raw LaTeX source rather than scrolling, but an explicit delimiter is unaffected regardless of length. 3 new tests (long → plain text, short → still real KaTeX, explicit delimiter never falls back even past the threshold). Full suite 1,625/1,625, typecheck clean. **Not yet live-tested in a running app.**

The user should be able to find a previous session by text they remember: search the stored messages of all sessions for a user-entered string and open the matching session. **Not started; design open, to be decided when we pick it up.** Sketch of what was asked: a magnifier (lens) icon that is always easy to reach in the left pane (next to the session list / "new session" control) and starts the search; some good way to take the query and show results as a list (session title, a snippet with the match highlighted, date), from which the user picks one and that session opens in the chat view, ideally scrolled to the matching message. Questions to settle then: where the input lives (inline filter box at the top of the session list vs. a modal / command-palette style overlay vs. a dedicated search pane); matching engine (SQLite FTS5 over `messages` for ranked, fast prefix/phrase search vs. plain `LIKE`, and whether to index tool output or only user/assistant text — tool output can be huge and noisy); how to handle sessions whose content is stored as structured blocks rather than plain text; result limits and incremental/debounced search; keyboard shortcut (e.g. Cmd/Ctrl+F in the left pane or Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+F); jump-to-message and in-session highlighting; and whether exported/archived or deleted sessions are included. **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.4.0, built together with #190.**

Questions to settle then: a find bar over the chat view (match count, next/previous with Enter / Shift+Enter, Esc to close, case sensitivity option) vs. Electron's built-in `webContents.findInPage`; the transcript is rendered Markdown with collapsed tool output (#72/#88), so decide whether collapsed tool blocks are searched and auto-expanded on a match; long sessions that render lazily or virtualised; highlighting inside KaTeX / Mermaid / code blocks; and making sure Cmd/Ctrl+F doesn't hijack the shortcut when focus is in the message input or Settings. **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.4.0, built together with #189.**
- [x] 199. Remember the window's position and size and restore them on the next start — user request 2026-10-10. Today `src/main/index.ts` always opens a fixed 1200x800 window (`new BrowserWindow({ width: 1200, height: 800, … })`) wherever the OS puts it. **Implemented 2026-10-13**, part of a quick-wins pass: new `src/main/window/bounds.ts` (same settings-persistence pattern as the existing `window/zoom.ts`) persists `getBounds()` on `resize`/`move` (debounced 500ms) and `close`; `resolveInitialBounds` validates a stored position actually overlaps a currently-connected display before using it — a disconnected second monitor, or a resolution change, would otherwise strand the window permanently off-screen with no way back short of editing the database by hand — and falls back to the default 1200x800 size with no forced position (letting Electron/the OS place it, same as today's first-launch behavior) rather than defaulting to a hard-coded corner. 8 new tests (parsing, the on-screen check including a partial-overlap case, the full resolve path with a mocked `screen.getAllDisplays`). Full suite 1,622/1,622, typecheck clean. **Live-confirmed by the user 2026-10-13** on the local build — resizing/moving the window and restarting restores the same position and size.

Store in `app_settings` or a small JSON file in the user-data folder, whichever the app already does for scalar settings.

Release build for the sponsor on Windows 11 AMD64 — noted 2026-10-10, to start after manual-test section 12 is finished. **Status as of 2026-10-14: built, live-verified end to end on a real Windows 11 AMD64 VM, and handed off to the sponsor (Vinodji) via `docs/windows_install.md`; all four original open decisions below are now resolved or deliberately deferred — see the dated notes further down this entry.** **Closed 2026-10-14 — Vinodji confirmed the handoff worked on his own machine**, closing the one item left open above. **Update 2026-10-19: release 0.3.1 is installed and running well on the sponsor's Windows 11 AMD64 machine (installed 2026-10-18) with Anthropic, OpenAI and Tavily keys; he will read the User Guide and explore the app before giving feedback. His feedback feeds the release triage (`docs/release_triage.md`).** The sponsor is a non-developer, so the installer and first launch must work with no manual configuration. Notes (not decisions) are in `docs/manual_testing.md` section 12, "Notes for the sponsor release": Python (bundle / fetch at install or first launch / ship without), code signing and SmartScreen (certificate, Azure Trusted Signing, or instructions), first-run provider key (screen and/or pre-filled settings file), and Windows testing on a real x64 machine, since the dev machine is Apple Silicon. Existing pieces: NSIS target in `electron-builder.yml`, `release.yml` windows-latest job, `electron-updater`, `scripts/install-prerequisites.ps1`.
  - **Update 2026-10-10: a build-from-source Windows test flow was added** (`scripts/windows-test-bootstrap.cmd`, `scripts/windows-test-run.cmd`, `scripts/windows-test-cleanup.cmd`, plus the existing `scripts/install-prerequisites.ps1`/`.cmd`; walkthrough in `docs/Window_test_deploy.md`) — this is a **QA/dev path to run the app from source on a Windows box**, not the sponsor's signed installer, so it does not by itself answer the 4 open decisions above. What it does give: a repeatable way to actually exercise the native-module / shell-path / managed-Python-runtime code on real Windows once a machine is available, which is the "Windows testing on a real x64 machine" need from the notes. **Works on Windows 11 x64 or ARM64** — on ARM64 the scripts deliberately install the **x64** Python and Node (via nvm-windows) and refuse a non-x64 Node, because the app's pinned numpy and the `sqlite-vec` native module **have no Windows ARM64 build**; the whole stack then runs under Windows' x64 emulation. That is a useful smoke test (e.g. a VMware ARM64 VM on an Apple Silicon Mac) but **is not proof for a real AMD64 PC**, and it raises a question worth settling before #202 proceeds: **what CPU architecture is the sponsor's actual PC?** If it's a Windows-on-ARM machine (e.g. a Surface), the shipped installer would hit the same no-ARM64-native-build problem and need the same x64-under-emulation approach, which changes the install story. **Caveat: the scripts themselves have not yet been run end-to-end on Windows** — written and documented but unverified live; the doc says to expect small fixes on the first real pass. Next concrete step: run `windows-test-bootstrap.cmd` + `windows-test-run.cmd` on an actual Windows 11 box (x64 or ARM64 VM) and fix whatever the first live run finds, before relying on them for #202/#131's Windows testing need.
  - **Decision (Deepak, 2026-10-11) — Python and Ollama for the sponsor build:** bundle a standalone Python interpreter plus the pinned base packages pre-baked into the shipped venv (overrides the general-release "no bundled Python" decision in `design-discussion.md` §4.3 item 9, specifically for this non-developer sponsor install); bundle the Ollama CPU-only binary plus the `nomic-embed-text` embedding model only (274MB, Apache-2.0) so local search-history works with no manual Ollama install — **no local chat model is bundled** (ruled out: `gpt-oss:20b`/`qwen3.8` class models are 5–13GB+, plus GPU-backend bloat and per-model license review, for a feature a cloud key already covers). Chat itself stays cloud-provider-only: first run asks the sponsor to add a provider API key in Settings, no pre-filled key. **(a) and (b) done 2026-10-11** (bundled Python — see the Progress note below). **(c) done 2026-10-11** (bundled Ollama lifecycle manager — see the Progress note further below); **changed from the original plan: the embedding model itself is pulled over the network on first run, not bundled into the installer** (user call, 2026-10-11 — keeps the installer smaller; the CPU-only Ollama runtime alone is bundled, ~66MB). **(d) a first-run screen for the provider key is still not started** — today's onboarding gate covers Python and (new) the Ollama/embedding-model step, but not a provider API key screen; the sponsor must find Settings themselves to add one (#111 doesn't cover this). Code signing/SmartScreen and the sponsor's actual CPU architecture (noted above) are still separately open.
  - **Progress 2026-10-11 — bundled Python, (a)+(b) above, built and unit-tested:** `scripts/fetch-bundled-python-win.mjs` downloads a pinned standalone interpreter (astral-sh/python-build-standalone, CPython 3.12.15, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc-install_only_stripped`, sha256-verified against the release's own `SHA256SUMS`) into `resources/python-win-x64/`, plus the 5 pinned `BASE_PACKAGES` and their transitive deps (16 wheels total) as prebuilt win_amd64/cp312 wheels into `resources/python-win-x64-wheels/` via `pip download --platform win_amd64 --python-version 3.12 --implementation cp --abi cp312 --only-binary=:all:` — runs on any host OS (only downloads/extracts files, never executes the Windows binaries), idempotent, `--force` to redo. Verified live on this (Linux) dev machine: 70MB interpreter + 35MB wheels, ~105MB total, checksum matched, all 16 wheels resolved with no sdist fallback needed. Both folders gitignored (fetched assets, not source) and wired into `electron-builder.yml`'s new `win.extraResources` (`python-win-x64` → `python-win-x64`, `python-win-x64-wheels` → `python-win-x64-wheels`); `npm run dist:win` now runs the fetch first via a new `predist:win` script.
    `workspace-runtime.ts`: `RuntimeDeps` gained optional `bundledPython()`/`bundledWheelhouse()`; `defaultRuntimeDeps()` resolves them from `process.resourcesPath` (win32-only, `undefined` everywhere else including dev/Vitest, where `resourcesPath` doesn't exist — correct, no bundled interpreter in dev). `detectPython` now tries the bundled interpreter first (prepended to the existing `py`/`python`/`python3` candidate list, through the same probe — not special-cased), so on the sponsor build detection never touches `PATH` at all. `installPackages` passes `--no-index --find-links <wheelhouse>` to pip when a bundled wheelhouse is reported, so the venv's own `-m pip install` needs no network; the failure message drops "check your network connection" in that case since it would be misleading. 4 new tests (bundled interpreter wins over every system candidate and system probing never runs; falls back to the system probe cleanly when nothing is bundled; `ensureWorkspace` passes the offline pip flags end to end; the offline failure message blames the bundled packages, not the network) — 25/25 in the file, 1,592/1,592 full suite, `typecheck:node` clean.
    **Not done:** `python-run.ts` wasn't touched — it already resolves the interpreter via `workspaceVenvBinDir`/`withWorkspaceVenv` (the *workspace's* venv, not the base interpreter directly), so it needs no change; this was confirmed by reading it, not assumed. **Not yet verified live**: nothing above has run inside a real packaged Windows build yet — only unit-tested with mocked `run`/`exists` on this Linux machine. Ollama+embedding-model bundling ((c) above) and the first-run provider-key screen ((d) above) are still not started.
    **Corrected 2026-10-11: the test VM is clean (no git/Node/Python/npm), matching the actual sponsor scenario** — building from source on it (what `docs/Window_test_deploy.md`'s flow or a local `npm run dist:win` need) is not the right test; only the packaged installer `.exe` itself should ever touch that machine. Since this Linux dev machine cannot cross-build electron-builder's Windows NSIS target (no Wine) and `release.yml` only fires on a `v*` tag (which publishes a real GitHub Release — not wanted for a test build), added `.github/workflows/windows-dist.yml`: a `workflow_dispatch`-only job on `windows-latest` that runs `npm run dist:win` (the `predist:win` fetch included) and uploads `release/*.exe` as a downloadable Actions artifact — no tag, no release, nothing published. **To use:** push this branch, then on GitHub: Actions tab → "Windows installer (manual)" → Run workflow → pick `design_change` → wait for it to finish → download the `sachivji-windows-installer` artifact from the run page → copy the `.exe` into the clean VM and run it. That installer run is also the first real test of #111's onboarding gate with a bundled (not system) Python, and of SmartScreen on this still-unsigned build (expected; see the 4 open sponsor-release decisions above).

  **Fix 2026-10-11 — first live CI run failed:** pushed, registered `windows-dist.yml` on `main` too (GitHub only lists `workflow_dispatch` workflows that exist on the default branch — not just on the branch being run), triggered it on `design_change`. It failed in `predist:win`: `fetch-bundled-python-win.mjs` extracted the interpreter under the OS tmpdir (`C:\Users\RUNNER~1\...\Temp`) then used `fs.rename()` to move it into `resources/python-win-x64`, which on GitHub's Windows runners sits on a different drive (`D:\a\...`, the checkout) — `rename()` across drives throws `EXDEV: cross-device link not permitted`, same restriction as a cross-filesystem rename on Linux. Root cause, not a flake. Fixed by extracting into a tmp dir created *under* `resources/` itself (`resources/.pbs-*`, cleaned up immediately after) instead of the OS tmpdir, so the final move is same-drive; removed the now-unused `node:os` `tmpdir` import. Re-ran the script locally end to end after the fix (70MB interpreter + 16 wheels, same as before) to confirm, not just syntax-checked.

  **Fix 2026-10-11 (second CI run) — electron-builder tried to publish:** the fetch/build/package steps all succeeded this time, but the job still failed: `Error: GitHub Personal Access Token is not set, neither programmatically, nor using env "GH_TOKEN"`. Cause: `electron-builder.yml` has a `publish:` block (GitHub provider, `releaseType: draft`), and electron-builder auto-publishes by default whenever it detects a CI environment (GitHub Actions always sets `CI=true`) unless told `--publish never`; `npm run dist:win` (and `dist:mac`/`dist:linux`) never passed that flag because they'd previously only ever been run locally, not in CI. `release.yml` was unaffected — it calls `electron-builder` directly with `--publish always` and its own `GH_TOKEN`, bypassing the `dist:*` scripts entirely. Fixed by adding `--publish never` to all three `dist:*` scripts in `package.json` (a plain `dist:*` build should never auto-publish just because it happens to run in a CI environment). Re-triggered the workflow.

  **Fix 2026-10-11 (live VM feedback) — installer gave no "it's done" confirmation:** installed and ran successfully on the clean VM (SmartScreen bypass as expected, app launched), but the user reported no message after the install finished — had to search the Start Menu manually. Cause: no `nsis:` block in `electron-builder.yml`, so electron-builder used its default **oneClick** NSIS mode — a bare progress bar with no wizard pages, no Finish screen, no shortcut. Fine for a developer, wrong for the sponsor (a non-technical user per the #202 note). Added a top-level `nsis:` block: `oneClick: false`, `perMachine: false`, `allowToChangeInstallationDirectory: true`, `createDesktopShortcut: always`, `createStartMenuShortcut: true`, `runAfterFinish: true` — the standard wizard with an explicit Finish page and both shortcuts. Not yet re-tested live as of this note (re-triggered the workflow after committing).

  **Root-caused and fixed 2026-10-11 (live VM, reported a third time) — Settings showed no providers (and no session) after first-run onboarding, fine after a full restart:** not a race at all. `App.svelte`'s startup effect (`checkFirstRun().then(...)`) only calls `initProvider()`/`loadSessions()` on the branch taken when `showFirstRunSetup` is false; when onboarding runs (any genuinely fresh install/app-data), that branch is skipped entirely — it just dismisses the splash and returns. `finishOnboarding()` (the `FirstRunSetup` `onFinish` callback) then only set `onboardingCompleted`/`showFirstRunSetup = false`; it never called `initProvider()` or `loadSessions()` either. So `providers` and `sessions` stayed at their empty `$state([])` initial values for the rest of that app lifetime post-onboarding — not just the provider dropdown, no session was auto-created or selected either. Only a full quit+restart re-ran `checkFirstRun`, which now short-circuits on `onboardingCompleted === 'true'` and takes the normal init path. Reproduced live end to end this session: fresh app-data VM install → onboarding (including the new Local Embeddings step) → configured OpenAI/Anthropic keys → provider-model picker opened Settings with zero provider rows → quit/restart → all 7 providers present. **Fixed**: `finishOnboarding()` now also `await Promise.all([initProvider(), loadSessions()])` after flipping `showFirstRunSetup = false`, so the first-run path ends in the same initialized state the normal-launch path does. `typecheck` (node+web) clean, full `src/renderer` suite 51/51 passing. **Not yet re-tested live** — fix made and unit-verified during the same paused live-VM session; next step on resume is reinstalling fresh and confirming the provider list/session appear immediately after onboarding, no restart needed.

  **Fix 2026-10-11 (live VM) — default workspace landed inside OneDrive:** the user's clean VM resolved the default workspace to `C:\Users\<user>\OneDrive\Documents\Sachivji Workspace`, not the plain `C:\Users\<user>\Documents\Sachivji Workspace` they expected. Cause: `defaultWorkspaceRoot()` (`src/main/tools/setup.ts`) built the path from `app.getPath('documents')`, which follows Windows' "Known Folder Move" redirection when OneDrive has taken over the Documents known folder (common on OEM/managed machines, exactly this VM) — a real problem, not just a path a user wouldn't expect: the workspace holds the Python `venv`, sqlite databases and model files, none of which should be swept into cloud sync (slow, wastes OneDrive quota on files never meant to leave the machine, and sqlite + background cloud sync risks file-lock conflicts mid-write). Fixed, Windows-only: build the Documents path from `app.getPath('home')` (the plain profile dir, unaffected by known-folder redirection) joined with `'Documents'`, instead of `app.getPath('documents')`. macOS/Linux unchanged — no equivalent report there, and iCloud Drive Desktop/Documents sync (the Mac analogue) is a separate, unconfirmed concern, not fixed here. New test stubs `process.platform` to `'win32'` and checks the path is built from `home`, not `documents`; all 5 tests in `setup.test.ts` pass, full suite 1,593/1,593, `typecheck:node` clean. **Not yet re-tested live** — existing installs on the VM already have their workspace created at the old OneDrive path; this only changes the default for a workspace not yet created (a fresh profile, or after clearing the setting) — migrating an already-created workspace is a separate, not-yet-needed step.

  **Progress 2026-10-11 — bundled Ollama, (c) above, built and unit-tested (user request: "We want Ollama in the build. Also, we want the post install step of downloading the embedding model"):** `scripts/fetch-bundled-ollama-win.mjs` downloads the official `ollama-windows-amd64.zip` (ollama/ollama GitHub release, sha256-verified against the release's own `sha256sum.txt`) and extracts only the CPU-relevant subset into `resources/ollama-win-x64/` — **not** the whole zip: the official Windows build bundles CUDA v12/v13 and Vulkan GPU backends alongside the CPU one, about 1.9GB uncompressed, of which this CPU-only/embedding-only bundle needs none (no local chat model is bundled — see the Decision note above). Extracting only `ollama.exe` + `lib/ollama/*` minus `cuda_v12/`, `cuda_v13/`, `vulkan/` brings it down to **44 files, 66MB**. Extraction uses `jszip` (already a project dependency) instead of a shell `unzip`/`tar`, since Windows has no `unzip` on PATH by default and GNU `tar` (confirmed on this dev machine) can't read zip at all — jszip loads the archive and writes only the wanted entries, so the 1.8GB of GPU DLLs is never even decompressed. Verified live on this machine: real download + checksum + extraction, idempotent re-run, correct `ollama.exe` + `lib/ollama/...` layout preserved (same relative structure as the official unzip, since `ollama.exe` resolves its own DLLs relative to itself). `fetch-bundled-python-win.mjs`'s `download()`/`sha256OfFile()` were factored out into a new shared `scripts/fetch-utils.mjs` so both fetch scripts use one implementation — re-verified the Python script still works end to end after the extraction, not just by re-reading the diff. Wired into `electron-builder.yml`'s `win.extraResources` (`ollama-win-x64` → `ollama-win-x64`), `package.json`'s `predist:win` (now fetches Python then Ollama), and `.gitignore`.

  **The embedding model itself is deliberately not bundled** — per the user's explicit instruction this round, overriding the original 2026-10-11 decision's "bundle Ollama... plus the nomic-embed-text embedding model" wording: `nomic-embed-text` (274MB) is pulled over the network on first run instead, via a new `OllamaRuntime` class (`src/main/runtime/ollama-runtime.ts`, same dependency-injected, testable shape as `WorkspaceRuntime`/`RuntimeDeps`): `bundledExe()`/`modelfilePath()` resolve `process.resourcesPath` on win32 only (`undefined` everywhere else, including dev/Vitest — a strict no-op on every build except this one, same pattern as the bundled Python); `ensure()` picks a free port (tries 11434 first, then 11435–11438, in case the sponsor already runs their own Ollama for something else), spawns `ollama.exe serve` with `OLLAMA_HOST`/`OLLAMA_MODELS` pointed at a port and a writable `userData`-relative folder (not the read-only install dir), waits for a health check, then `POST /api/pull` with `stream:true`, parsing the NDJSON response for `{completed,total}` progress — and finally runs `ollama create nomic-embed-text -f <bundled ModelFileNomicEmbed>` (also added to `electron-builder.yml`'s `extraResources`, a single small text file) to **recreate the model in place with its context-size fix**: `models/ModelFileNomicEmbed` already documented that the model's published default (`num_ctx 8192`) exceeds its real trained context (2048) and crashes `llama-server` on an embed call — this step was previously a manual instruction for a human tester, now automated. State flows through a new `OllamaRuntimeState` (`shared/runtime.ts`: `'unavailable' | 'starting' | 'pulling-model' | 'ready' | 'failed'`, plus `baseUrl`/`modelPullProgress`), broadcast the same way as the existing Python `RuntimeStateChanged` (new `OllamaGetState`/`OllamaEnsure`/`OllamaStateChanged` IPC channels, `register.ts` wiring, `app.on('before-quit', () => ollamaRuntime.stop())`). `providers/setup.ts` gained `setBundledOllamaBaseUrl()`, called whenever the runtime's `baseUrl` changes, so **both** the registry's `'ollama'` provider and the separate `getEmbeddingProvider('ollama')` path (the one actually used for real embed calls — easy to miss, since it builds its own `OllamaProvider` independently of the registry) point at the bundled server's real port instead of assuming `localhost:11434` — everyone else (dev, mac, linux, or a sponsor with their own separately-running Ollama) is unaffected, since `setBundledOllamaBaseUrl` is only ever called with a defined value on this one build. `FirstRunSetup.svelte` gained a third onboarding step, `'ollama'`, shown only when the Python step's Continue is clicked **and** the tracked Ollama state isn't `'unavailable'` — on every other build this check short-circuits straight to `finish()`, so the onboarding flow is visually unchanged there; on the bundled build it shows live pull progress and a Retry/"Continue anyway" pair on failure (local embeddings degrade gracefully to "pick a cloud provider in Settings instead", same framing as the Python step's "continue without"). New tests: 12 in `ollama-runtime.test.ts` (unavailable short-circuit, the full happy path with progress events and the modelfile step, port fallback, no-free-port failure, health-check-timeout failure + kill, pull failure, missing-modelfile skip, apply-modelfile failure, `ensure()` idempotency, `stop()`, `getState()`) — all passing; full suite 1,605/1,605; `typecheck:node` and `typecheck:web` both clean; `npm run build` (electron-vite) succeeds. **Not yet verified live** — nothing above has run inside a real packaged Windows build yet, same caveat as the bundled-Python work before its own first live test.

  **Fix 2026-10-11 — Ollama status was only ever visible during onboarding:** caught before the live test, while explaining to the user why their already-onboarded VM wouldn't show the new onboarding step on an in-place upgrade (`onboardingCompleted` is already `'true'` from the earlier install, so `FirstRunSetup` never shows again — though `ollamaRuntime.ensure()` itself still runs automatically on every launch regardless, onboarding or not). That left no way to see its status, progress, or retry a failure once onboarding was done — the Python runtime has a whole "Python runtime" section in Settings for exactly this; Ollama had nothing. Added a matching "Local embeddings (bundled Ollama)" section to `SettingsPanel.svelte` (status, message, live pull percentage, a Retry button on failure) — hidden entirely when `status === 'unavailable'`, so Settings is unchanged on every build/platform except this one. `typecheck:web`/`typecheck:node` clean, full suite still 1,605/1,605, `npm run build` succeeds.

  **Found + fixed 2026-10-11 (live VM, manual test 13.4) — no `npx` on the sponsor VM, so MCP servers like Context7 can't connect:** resuming the live test after the providers fix above, `npx` was confirmed missing from `PATH` in a plain command prompt on the VM — expected, since this is deliberately a genuinely clean Windows machine with no git/Node/Python/npm (the same starting state the bundled-Python/Ollama work above already addressed for those two). Test 13.4.1 configures Context7 with `command: npx, args: -y @upstash/context7-mcp`; `StdioClientTransport` (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`) spawns that via `cross-spawn`, which resolves a bare command against `env.PATH` (confirmed by reading `cross-spawn/lib/util/resolveCommand.js` — it's `parsed.options.env || process.env`, so an explicitly-passed `env` really is what gets searched, not silently ignored) — so any MCP server configured to launch through `npx`/`npm`/`node` fails the same way inside the app as on the command line, not just in a user's own shell. User's call: bundle Node too, same reasoning as Python/Ollama — without it they can't run MCP servers over npx at all. **Implemented**: `scripts/fetch-bundled-node-win.mjs` (same pattern as `fetch-bundled-ollama-win.mjs`: downloads the official `node-v24.21.0-win-x64.zip`, sha256-verified against that release's own SHASUMS256.txt, extracted via `jszip` with the zip's single top-level folder stripped — no GPU-style exclusion needed here, the whole ~102MB/1994-file tree is wanted since `npx`/`npm` depend on npm's own `node_modules`). Verified live on this dev machine: real download, checksum match, `node.exe`/`npm.cmd`/`npx.cmd` all present at the extracted root. Wired into `electron-builder.yml`'s `win.extraResources` (`node-win-x64` → `node-win-x64`), `package.json`'s new `fetch:bundled-node:win` + `predist:win` chain, and `.gitignore`. **Runtime wiring** (`src/main/mcp/client.ts`): rather than rewriting `config.command` to guess at absolute paths for `npx`/`npm`/`node` specifically, `resolveStdioEnv()` just prepends the bundled Node directory to `PATH` in the env passed to `StdioClientTransport` — more general (covers any MCP server whose own process shells out to `node`/`npm` too, not just the configured launch command) and lets the SDK's existing `cross-spawn`/`which` resolution do the rest via Windows `PATHEXT` (`.CMD` included by default). `defaultBundledNodeDir()` gates on `process.platform === 'win32'` + `process.resourcesPath` + the extracted `npx.cmd` actually existing — `undefined` on every build/platform but this one (dev, mac, linux, and Vitest all unaffected), same DI shape as `bundledPython()`/`bundledExe()` above. New tests in `client.test.ts` (`resolveStdioEnv` prepends correctly with a given dir, a custom `env` still merges on top, and the pre-existing no-bundle behavior is unchanged when `bundledNodeDir` is `undefined`) — full suite 1,608/1,608, `typecheck` (node+web) clean, `npm run build` succeeds. **Not yet verified live** — needs a new Windows build (the running sponsor-VM install predates this fix) and a retry of manual test 13.4 end to end.

A build including the Settings-visibility fix is running at the time of pausing: https://github.com/dpurandare/AryaAI/actions/runs/38138024622 — check its result first on resume (`gh run view 38138024622`); if it succeeded, the next step is downloading `sachivji-windows-installer` from that run and testing on the sponsor VM. **Still open for this session:** (1) live-verify the bundled Python + Ollama + OneDrive-avoiding workspace path + NSIS wizard + Settings Ollama section all together in one real install (nothing has been tested together yet — each fix so far was reasoned through and unit-tested, not confirmed live after the most recent few commits); (2) confirm the "Local embeddings" onboarding step actually appears and completes on a genuinely fresh onboarding (the user's current VM already has `onboardingCompleted=true`, so a true first-run test needs either a fresh Windows user profile or clearing that app setting); (3) item (d) from the 2026-10-11 decision — a first-run provider-key screen — is still not started; (4) code signing/SmartScreen and the sponsor's real CPU architecture are still separately open.

  **Live VM retest 2026-10-12, build `38153023176` (tip `e89d068`) — user ran a full clean-slate uninstall/reinstall and worked through the resume checklist.**
  - **Found live, not yet fixed — first-launch false negative on bundled-resource detection, same shape in two places.** On first launch, Settings reported Python "not available at the location" it should be bundled at; clicking Retry found it immediately with no other change. The embedding-model setup then showed the identical symptom for Ollama/the model file; Retry resolved that too. Both `bundledPython()` (`workspace-runtime.ts`) and `bundledExe()`/`modelfilePath()` (`ollama-runtime.ts`) use the same `bundledResourcePath()` + `existsSync()` check at startup — two independent hits of the same pattern in one session points at a systemic first-launch race (extracted installer files/NSIS not fully settled — antivirus scan or disk flush — by the time the first `existsSync()` call runs), not two separate bugs. Retry already recovers cleanly both times, so this isn't blocking, but it fired twice in a row for the same user — worth deciding whether to make the check retry internally with a short delay before ever surfacing "not found," rather than relying on the user noticing and clicking Retry.
  - **Provider list confirmed live** — fix `9deb0ab` holds: opening the OpenAI API key setting showed the provider immediately, no restart needed.
  - **Local Embeddings onboarding step confirmed live** — real network pull of `nomic-embed-text` observed completing (not a no-op).
  - **Manual test 13.4 (Context7/MCP) — still open, this run doesn't verify the bundled-Node fix.** Asked Sachivji to search for and configure the Context7 MCP server; it installed Context7 as a **remote HTTP MCP server** (`https://mcp.context7.com/mcp`), not the `npx @upstash/context7-mcp` stdio launch that commit `e89d068`'s bundled Node / `resolveStdioEnv()` targets. HTTP transport never touches the bundled-Node PATH-prepending code at all, so this success doesn't exercise that fix — it happened to land on a transport that was already going to work without it. **Still need**: explicitly configure an MCP server via the `npx`/stdio path on this VM (e.g. force Context7's stdio config, or use another `npx`-based server) to actually close 13.4.
  - **Found + root-caused + fixed 2026-10-12 — the user did go on to force the npx/stdio path, and it failed with `MCP error -32000: Connection closed`.** No app log file exists at all today (checked: no `electron-log` or equivalent wired in; `console.error` calls just go to a GUI app's unattached stdout/stderr and vanish) — a real gap on its own, logged separately below. Relaunched the installed app from `cmd.exe` with Electron's own `--enable-logging --log-file=...` flag to capture it, which surfaced the real error: `node_modules/npm/bin/npx-cli.js` (and `npm-prefix.js`) — the scripts `npx.cmd` itself shells out to — threw `MODULE_NOT_FOUND`, crashing the child before any MCP handshake, which the SDK reports as "Connection closed". Checked the installed app's `resources\node-win-x64` directly on the VM (`dir` listing): only 14 top-level files (`node.exe`, `npm.cmd`, `npx.cmd`, docs, etc., 93.8MB) — **no `node_modules` folder at all**, versus the 1994 files/102MB the fetch script (`fetch-bundled-node-win.mjs`) actually extracted at build time (confirmed by downloading the real official zip and counting: 1994 non-directory entries, exact match with the CI log's own "1994 files" line — so the fetch step was never the problem). Root cause confirmed directly in `app-builder-lib`'s own installed source (`node_modules/app-builder-lib/out/util/filter.js`): `createFilter()`'s copy filter — shared by every electron-builder copy operation, not just `files` — has a hardcoded, no-opt-out rule, `if (relative === "node_modules") return false`, meant to stop an app's own root `node_modules` from double-bundling (references `electron-builder#867`). Our `extraResources` entry (`from: resources/node-win-x64`) has `node_modules` sitting directly at that root, so its relative path is the exact string `"node_modules"` and the whole subtree — including `npm/bin/npx-cli.js` — got silently dropped, while every sibling file copied fine. Verified by calling the real installed `FileMatcher`/`createFilter` code directly against a scratch directory mirroring the structure: the single-entry config reproduces exactly this (`node.exe` kept, `node_modules` dropped); a second `extraResources` entry rooted one level inside — `from: resources/node-win-x64/node_modules, to: node-win-x64/node_modules` — sidesteps the exact-string check entirely (confirmed `npm/bin/npx-cli.js` now kept). Fixed in `electron-builder.yml` by adding that second entry alongside the existing one. **Not yet verified live** — needs a new build and a real `npx`-based MCP connection test (not HTTP) to close 13.4 for real this time.
  - **Found, not yet fixed — no log file exists for this app at all.** Found while diagnosing the above: there's no persistent log anywhere (no `electron-log`, no file-based logger wired into the main process), so a double-clicked packaged build has nowhere for `console.error`/crash output to go — the only way to see it this session was relaunching from `cmd.exe` with Electron's `--enable-logging --log-file=...` flag, which a non-technical sponsor would never do. Worth adding a real log file (e.g. under `userData`) before the sponsor build ships, so a failure can be diagnosed from what the user already has instead of asking them to relaunch from a terminal.
  - **DB-connection test closed out** (pending since the 2026-10-11 pause note). Copied `generate_sample_db`/`validate_db` scripts into the VM workspace, had Sachivji run them, create the database, add the connection in-app, and explain the schema — all worked end to end; a few intermediate script failures (missing dependencies) came up and were handled gracefully by the agent itself without the user needing to intervene.

  **Live VM retest 2026-10-12 part 2, build `38217344070` (tip `c338540`, includes the `node_modules` extraResources fix above) — partial reinstall (uninstall + reinstall, `%APPDATA%\Sachivji` and the workspace deliberately preserved, no fresh onboarding needed since nothing about it changed in this build).**
  - **Manual test 13.4 (Context7/MCP) closed — confirmed on first try.** The `npx`/stdio Context7 configuration connected successfully; the `node_modules` extraResources fix holds.
  - **Found a third instance of the same first-launch-after-process-start transient race, this time for provider API keys — confirmed transient, not real data loss.** After the reinstall, Settings showed no saved keys for Anthropic/OpenAI/Tavily, asking to re-enter them — alarming at first since it looked like real data loss. Root mechanism (confirmed via Electron/Chromium's own documented `os_crypt_win.cc` behavior, not guesswork): `safeStorage` on Windows DPAPI-wraps one random AES key and stores it in a `Local State` file beside `credentials.enc.json` under `userData`; if that wrapped key is ever unreadable, Chromium's documented behavior is to silently mint a new one, orphaning everything encrypted under the old key. This looked at first like exactly that — except the same user's VM is **not** reverted/reset between sessions (ruled out live with the user), which would normally make DPAPI's key stable. Re-entering the three keys and then **merely relaunching the app (no reinstall)** confirmed they persisted fine the second time — so this is the same shape as the Python/Ollama first-launch false negatives already logged above (an early post-launch call failing once, clean on the next attempt), not permanent key loss. Three independent hits of "first call after a fresh process start intermittently fails, clean on retry" in one build now (Python detection, Ollama/model detection, safeStorage decrypt) makes this very likely one shared environment-level cause (something not fully ready — antivirus real-time scanning, a slow-to-initialize OS subsystem — in the first second or two after this app's process starts on this VM specifically), not three unrelated bugs. **Worth deciding before the sponsor build ships**: unlike the Python/Ollama cases (which show a clear Settings error with a Retry button), this one is silent and confusing — it just looks like the keys were never saved, with no indication to the user that it's transient and a relaunch would fix it. If this pattern recurs, consider either an internal retry-with-short-delay on first-launch-sensitive reads generally, or at least a clearer message/relaunch prompt for this one specifically.

Still open: (a) the shared first-launch transient-race pattern above (not blocking — every instance so far has a working Retry/relaunch path, but three in one session is enough to treat as one real thing to fix, not noise); (b) first-run provider-key screen — not started; (c) code signing/SmartScreen; (d) confirming the sponsor's real CPU architecture; (e) the no-log-file gap noted just above.

  **Found + root-caused + fixed 2026-10-12, on the Linux dev machine (not the Windows VM) — "list the tools available with the MCP Context7" sent the model into a 20-call loop against the wrong tool, then a vague non-answer.** The user asked to try the now-closed Context7 config on this machine directly; ran the app via a CDP driver (no monitor available) and found the real session: the model called `mcp_list_connections` 20 times in a row — byte-identical, non-error results every time — before giving up with "2 tools, named `mcp__Context7__*`", never naming them. Root cause: `mcp_list_connections` (`src/main/tools/config-gen/mcp-list.ts`) only ever reported a tool *count* per server (`McpServerStatusInfo.toolCount`, `src/main/mcp/manager.ts`), never the actual tool names — `McpManager` already tracks `managed.toolNames: string[]` internally per connected server, it just wasn't exposed. With no way to get the real names, the model kept re-calling the only tool it had, hoping for more detail that was never coming; there's also no loop-guard for a *successful*-but-unhelpful repeated call (the existing `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_TOOL_FAILURES` guard only catches failures). **Fixed**: added `toolNames: string[]` to `McpServerStatusInfo` and `McpManager.getStatus()`, and `mcp_list_connections` now lists them (`connected, 2 tools: mcp__Context7__resolve-library-id, mcp__Context7__query-docs` instead of `connected, 2 tools (named mcp__Context7__*)`). Bumped two "deliberate growth" prompt-budget ceilings for the larger tool description/output (`role:mcp-operator` 770→772, the whole-registry total 10000→10009) with dated comments per the existing convention. Full suite 1,608/1,608, `typecheck:node` clean. **Live-verified, not just unit-tested**: restarted the dev app and re-sent the exact same message via the CDP driver — before the fix, 20 calls/22s/no real answer; after, 1 call/5s, and the final answer correctly names both real tools (`mcp__Context7__resolve-library-id`, `mcp__Context7__query-docs`). Not yet rebuilt into a Windows installer — this is pure app logic, not platform-specific, so it'll land in whatever Windows build comes next; no urgency to rebuild just for this alone.

  **Live-verified on the Windows VM 2026-10-12, build `38235090674` (tip `1ecb3ef`) — the `mcp_list_connections` fix above confirmed on the actual sponsor platform, not just the Linux dev machine.** Triggered a fresh build off `1ecb3ef` (previous Windows installer, `38217344070`, only had the `node_modules` fix, not this one). User did a partial reinstall (uninstall + reinstall, `%APPDATA%\Sachivji` preserved — keys/workspace untouched, confirmed not needed since this build changes no onboarding/provider-storage behavior) and re-sent the same "List the tools available with the mcp Context7" prompt: no 20x loop this time. Closes out the last open thread from this fix.

  **Found + root-caused + fixed 2026-10-12 (two more, both via real VM session dumps the user exported and shared) — a FOLLOW-UP question in the same session ("Answer the question referring the Context7") sent the router into a second, different loop, worse on gpt-4o than on gpt-4o-mini.** Dump 1 (`gpt-4o-mini`): the model called `mcp_create_connection` for the already-configured Context7 server twice (failed "already exists" both times), then `mcp_list_connections` four more times (identical, no error), then gave up and answered the actual question (an axios timeout query) from memory, never calling the real `mcp__Context7__*` tools. Dump 2 (`gpt-4o`, same prompt): skipped straight to `mcp_list_connections`, called it **26 times in a row**, byte-identical every time, and only stopped because the user pressed the Stop button — confirmed directly with the user this was a genuine Stop, not the model finishing on its own (the "stopped before producing a response" message is reserved for that case, `orchestrator.ts`'s `emitStoppedNoAnswer`).

  **Root cause 1 (the wrong tool was reachable at all):** `fullRouterTools` (`router-tools.ts`) — the tool list the top-level router actually gets, used whenever the "slim router" setting is off, which is the default (`register.ts`: unset setting → full router) — only ever filtered out `mcp__*` server tools and the two specialist-only research-note tools. It did **not** exclude `mcp_create_connection`/`db_create_connection`/`api_create_connection`, even though these are owned exclusively by the Configurator role ("you only set connections up — using them afterwards is another specialist's job", `roles.ts`). The slim router (`selectRouterTools`) already excluded these by ownership; full router leaked them straight through, so the top-level model had a plausible-looking (wrong) tool sitting right there and used it instead of delegating. **Fixed**: added a small named `CONNECTION_SETUP_TOOLS` set (`mcp_create_connection`, `db_create_connection`, `api_create_connection`) that `fullRouterTools` now excludes too — deliberately a named set, not a blanket "exclude everything any specialist owns": that blanket version was tried first and broke `find_stock_images`/`download_image` (Researcher-owned, but needed directly by the full router for the deck-building flow, which is router-only since no specialist can delegate to Page Creator) — confirmed by running the test suite and catching the regression before committing.

  **Root cause 2 (nothing stopped a *successful*-but-unhelpful repeat):** `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_TOOL_FAILURES` only ever counts repeated calls that keep *failing* — every `mcp_list_connections` call here succeeded with identical output, so that guard never applied, on either model. Also traced why the MCP Operator never got pulled in on its own: the only code-level nudge toward it (`knowledge-first.ts`'s `KNOWLEDGE_FIRST_REFUSAL`) is narrowly keyed to the model's *first* `web_search` call of the turn, and the one auto-insertion mechanism that forces an `mcp-operator` step (`ensureDomainKnowledgeStep`) only runs on the compound-*plan* path — this request was classified as simple/direct, so neither ever fired. Reaching `delegate_to_mcp_operator` was left entirely to the model's own judgement, and neither model made that call. **Fixed**: a new generic guard (`MAX_IDENTICAL_CALLS_BEFORE_NUDGE` / `MAX_IDENTICAL_CALLS_AFTER_NUDGE`, `orchestrator.ts`) tracks a run calling the exact same tool with the exact same input (key-order-independent JSON comparison), success or failure alike. After 2 identical calls in a row it injects a one-time `[Repetition check by Sachivji]` note — naming `delegate_to_mcp_operator` by name when the repeated tool is one of the mcp admin tools and an MCP Operator is actually connected/available, since that's the concrete missing instruction found above; after 2 more identical calls despite the nudge, it ends the turn outright with a clear message instead of burning the round budget (would have cut the `gpt-4o` case off at round 4, not 26, with no Stop click needed). Deliberately **opt-in to a small named set** (`GUARDED_IDENTICAL_CALL_TOOLS`: the `mcp_list_connections`/`mcp_create_connection`/`db_list_connections`/`db_create_connection`/`api_list_connections`/`api_create_connection` admin/status tools only) rather than a blanket rule over every tool with exceptions carved out — a blanket-with-exceptions version was tried first and broke two already-correct, already-tested behaviors: a test where the user explicitly asks to list the same directory 4 times (expected to really run all 4), and the existing research completion-gate's own more specific nudge for repeated `web_search` calls. Everything outside the named set (reads, writes, web_search, `api_check_status` polling a job) is untouched, left to the mechanisms that already govern it.

  5 new tests in `orchestrator.test.ts` (nudges then stops with the right message and tool-call count; does not trip on changing input; does not trip a non-guarded tool like `api_check_status` even repeated many times; omits the `delegate_to_mcp_operator` mention when no server is connected; a differently-named call in between resets the streak) plus 2 new tests in `router-tools.test.ts` for the `fullRouterTools` exclusion (including the explicit regression check that picture tools survive). Full suite 1,614/1,614, `typecheck` (node+web) clean, `npm run build` succeeds.

  **Live-verified 2026-10-12, commit `f97952b`.** First pass on the Linux dev machine (driven headlessly via CDP, no monitor on this box), running the exact 4-prompt sequence ("List the tools available with the mcp Context7" / "What MCP servers are connected?" / "How do I set a default request timeout in axios?" / "Answer the question referring the Context7") against all three providers with real API keys:
  - `gpt-4o-mini`: nudge fired at 2 identical `mcp_list_connections` calls on message 4 (as designed); model chose to answer from memory rather than delegate — stopped at 2 calls instead of the original bug report's 4-6.
  - `gpt-4o`: looped on message 3 this time (one message earlier than the original report) — 2 calls, nudge, 2 more calls, then the hard stop fired with the warning message; same pattern repeated on message 4. Bounded at exactly 4 calls every time, with a clear message, vs. the original **26** calls that needed a manual Stop click.
  - `claude-sonnet-5-5`: never looped at all — correctly called `delegate_to_mcp_operator` on both message 3 and message 4, which drove the real `mcp__Context7__resolve-library-id` → `mcp__Context7__query-docs` calls and returned a real, well-sourced axios-timeout answer citing the actual library ID/version. Confirms the loop is a router-model-judgement gap this fix bounds, not something every model hits.

Design revisit: MCP Operator tool schemas are not lazily loaded — decided 2026-10-10 (manual-test section 12.11). **Not for this release; design revisit only.** Today every tool of a connected MCP server is put into the MCP Operator's context with its full schema, so a server with many tools (the earlier "gbrain" measurement: 144 tools dominating the prompt) makes that one role heavy on every request; no other role is affected. The design (`docs/design-discussion.md`, the MCP Operator paragraph near line 335) intended the opposite: tools **deferred**, name plus a one-line description until first use, the full schema loaded on demand. That part was not built. Questions for the revisit: how a deferred tool is loaded (a search/describe call, or a two-step list-then-load), how this interacts with prompt caching (a stable tool list is what makes caching pay; loading tools mid-turn changes the prefix), whether provider-side tool search exists on the providers we support, how many tools make it matter (a threshold), and how a user-written server description in Settings can steer which tools load. Measure the prompt cost with the largest real server before designing. Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): 0.5.0 (detail); revisit after 0.4.0. Design merged into #123.

- [x] 207. Self-contained Linux release (general, not sponsor-specific) — user request 2026-10-13, extending the Windows sponsor build (#202) to Linux and promoting it to the general release policy for that platform (decided with the user: updates the "no bundled Python" call in `docs/design-discussion.md` §4.3 item 9 for Linux the same way #202 already overrode it for Windows). **Implemented and live-verified on this (Linux x64) dev machine 2026-10-13** — researched feasibility first (checked real GitHub/PyPI/nodejs.org release assets before writing any code), then built and packaged both Linux targets for real, since this dev machine can run them natively (unlike Windows, which needed a CI runner).

  **Feasibility research, with direct evidence, not assumed:**
  - **Python**: `python-build-standalone` publishes both a glibc (`x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`) and a musl (`x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`) standalone build. Tried musl first on the theory that a fully static binary would be maximally portable — **wrong**: downloaded and ran it, and it's dynamically linked against musl libc, hardcoding `/lib/ld-musl-x86_64.so.1` as its ELF interpreter, which mainstream desktop distros (Ubuntu, Fedora, Debian) don't ship — only Alpine does. Musl would have been *less* portable, not more. Switched to the glibc build, confirmed it runs on this machine (GLIBC 2.31) and needs only GLIBC 2.17+ (`objdump -T` on the real binary). All 5 pinned packages (matplotlib, numpy, pillow, python-docx, openpyxl) resolve as prebuilt wheels for cp312: numpy/matplotlib/pillow via `pip download` with 3 stacked `--platform` flags (`manylinux2014_x86_64`, `manylinux_2_17_x86_64`, `manylinux_2_28_x86_64` — pillow 12.3.0 only publishes the newest of the three, numpy/matplotlib the oldest; pip's `--platform` matches wheel filenames literally so passing one tag isn't enough), python-docx/openpyxl as universal `py3-none-any` wheels. Verified live: 16 wheels resolve, matching the Windows wheelhouse's wheel count exactly.
  - **Ollama**: the official `ollama-linux-amd64.tar.zst` (CPU base build) embeds `lib/ollama/cuda_v12/` and `cuda_v13/` subfolders inside one archive — same shape as the Windows zip's embedded CUDA/Vulkan backends. Downloaded and listed it directly: excluding those two folders cuts 2148MB down to 103MB/55 files (bigger than Windows' 66MB because Linux ships one CPU shared library per microarchitecture — haswell, skylakex, zen4, etc — chosen at runtime, instead of Windows' one generic DLL). Extracted and ran the filtered binary directly on this machine: works, needs GLIBC 2.28 at most.
  - **Node**: `nodejs.org` publishes `node-vX.Y.Z-linux-x64.tar.gz` directly (no filtering needed, unlike Ollama).
  - **Extraction approach**: reused the existing Windows Python script's own precedent of shelling out to the system `tar` for `.tar.gz` (it already does this, contrary to its own comment's "pure JS" framing — only the Ollama/Node *Windows* scripts use `jszip`, because Windows has no `unzip`/`tar` on PATH by default). Linux's `.tar.zst` (Ollama) needed one more capability check: confirmed `tar --zstd` works on this machine's GNU tar 1.30 (and is standard on GitHub's `ubuntu-latest` runners) before relying on it, rather than assuming.

  **Built, three new scripts mirroring the Windows ones** (`scripts/fetch-bundled-{python,ollama,node}-linux.mjs`, using `scripts/fetch-utils.mjs` unchanged): same pinned-release-plus-sha256 pattern, writing to `resources/{python,ollama,node}-linux-x64` (gitignored). Wired into `package.json` as `fetch:bundled-*:linux` + a `predist:linux` chain, same shape as `predist:win`. All three ran live on this machine end to end: Python (104MB interpreter + 44MB/16-wheel wheelhouse), Ollama (103MB, CUDA stripped), Node (208MB) — every extracted binary (`python3`, `ollama`, `node`, `npx`) executes directly.

  **`electron-builder.yml`**: added a `linux.extraResources` block for the four fetched directories, hoisted the shared `ModelFileNomicEmbed` entry (the `num_ctx` fix for `nomic-embed-text`, previously Windows-only) to a top-level `extraResources` so both platforms use the one entry. **Found and fixed a real bug before it could repeat #202's node_modules incident**: first build attempt copied the Windows pattern's two-entry node_modules split verbatim, pointing at `resources/node-linux-x64/node_modules` — electron-builder logged `file source doesn't exist` because Linux's official tarball actually nests npm two levels down, at `lib/node_modules`, not at the tarball root like Windows' zip. Checked directly with `find`: confirmed the real path, and confirmed electron-builder's copy filter (the one that silently dropped Windows' `node_modules` — TASKS #202) only matches a path that is *exactly* `"node_modules"` relative to a copy entry's own root, which `lib/node_modules` never is — so Linux needs no split-entry workaround at all, unlike Windows. Removed the unnecessary second entry once this was confirmed against the actual packaged output (`release/linux-unpacked/resources/node-linux-x64/lib/node_modules/npm` present). Also removed `python3`/`python3-venv` from `deb.depends` (no longer needed — the bundled interpreter covers it, same as Windows needs no system Python; `detectPython` still falls back to a system Python if the bundled one is somehow missing).

  **Code changes, generalizing the three Windows-only (`win32`-gated) resource lookups to also recognize `linux`** — no behavior change on any other platform (mac, dev, Vitest all still see `undefined`, confirmed by the full suite still passing unchanged): `workspace-runtime.ts`'s `bundledResourcePath`/`bundledPython`/`bundledWheelhouse` (Linux binary at `python-linux-x64/bin/python3`, wheelhouse dir `python-linux-x64-wheels`); `ollama-runtime.ts`'s `bundledResourcePath`/`bundledExe` (`ollama-linux-x64/bin/ollama`); `mcp/client.ts`'s `defaultBundledNodeDir` (`node-linux-x64/bin`, checking for `npx` there — Linux's tarball keeps the binaries under `bin/`, unlike Windows' zip which has them at its root, so the PATH entry differs in shape, not just name). `installPackages`'s offline-pip logic and `resolveStdioEnv`'s PATH-prepending were already fully platform-generic (only ever checked wheelhouse/bundledNodeDir truthiness) and needed no change.

  **Live-verified end to end on this machine**, not just unit-tested: ran `npm run typecheck` (clean) and `npm test` (1,614/1,614, same count as before — no regressions), then `npm run build` and a real `npx electron-builder --linux --publish never` — produced both `release/sachivji_0.2.2_amd64.deb` (291MB) and `release/Sachivji-0.2.2.AppImage` (384MB). Confirmed directly in the packaged output (not assumed from the config): `dpkg -c` on the real `.deb` shows `python-linux-x64/bin/python3` (symlink to `python3.12`), `node-linux-x64/bin/npx` (symlink to `../lib/node_modules/npm/bin/npx-cli.js`), `ollama-linux-x64/bin/ollama` and `ModelFileNomicEmbed` all present; `dpkg -I` shows the trimmed `Depends` line with no `python3`/`python3-venv`. Simulated the app's own `process.resourcesPath` resolution against the real `release/linux-unpacked/resources` tree (every path the actual code computes — `bundledPython`, `bundledWheelhouse`, `bundledOllamaExe`, `modelfilePath`, the bundled-Node PATH dir, and `npx` itself inside it) and confirmed every one resolves to a real file. Launched the real packaged binary (`release/linux-unpacked/sachivji --no-sandbox`) on this headless box (no Xvfb installed, so no actual window could render, but Electron's main process still runs): it started, initialized its real SQLite databases, connected its already-configured local MCP servers (llmwiki, Context7) for real, and ran normally until stopped — confirms the packaged app's main process boots and runs genuine app logic from the bundled resources tree, not just that the files exist.

  **Not yet done**: the actual GUI flow (first-run onboarding showing the Python/Ollama steps, Settings > Runtime reflecting bundled status) was not watched on screen — this box has no display server and installing one (Xvfb) wasn't done without asking first. A genuinely fresh `userData` profile wasn't tested either (this run used the dev machine's own existing, already-onboarded profile). `docs/design-discussion.md` §4.3 item 9 was updated the same session to record the per-platform override (done). macOS bundling (the user's next stated target after Linux) is a separate, unstarted item — this one is Linux-only.

  **Minimum supported Ubuntu version, measured 2026-10-13 (follow-up, before the user tests the `.deb` on a fresh VM)**: scanned every ELF binary in the actual packaged `release/linux-unpacked` tree (`objdump -T`, not assumed) for the GLIBC symbol versions each one requires, across the bundled Python/Ollama/Node runtime, Electron itself, and the native `.node`/`.so` addons (`better-sqlite3`, `sqlite-vec`). **The binding constraint is GLIBC 2.29**, from `better-sqlite3`'s prebuilt `prebuilds/linux-x64.node` — not from anything added for #207; everything bundled here needs less (Ollama/Node 2.27–2.28, Electron 2.25, the Python wheelhouse's newest wheel — pillow/contourpy — 2.28). Documented in `README.md`'s prerequisites list as **"Ubuntu 20.04 LTS or newer works; 22.04 LTS or newer is recommended"**: GLIBC 2.29 is satisfied by 20.04's glibc 2.31, and this dev machine (confirmed Ubuntu 20.04, per [[user-env-dev-machine]]) is live proof — the packaged binary was launched on it earlier this same session and ran normally, no GLIBC error. The 22.04+ recommendation is only because 20.04's standard Ubuntu support window has already ended, not a technical requirement. Caveat stated in the README too: this only checked GLIBC symbol versions, not any separate Chromium/Electron 44 kernel or sandbox (seccomp/user-namespace) requirement — unverified the same rigorous way, though unlikely to be the binding constraint on any VM image from the last several years.

  **Correction, found live 2026-10-13 — the original measurement held for a build made on THIS machine only, not the CI-built artifact `linux-dist.yml` actually produces.** The user installed the `sachivji-linux-installer` artifact downloaded from the real GitHub Actions run (`38394089424`, `ubuntu-latest`) on this GLIBC-2.31 machine: the app showed 6 processes in System Monitor but **no window ever appeared**. Diagnosed with direct evidence (no persistent log exists — see the gap noted earlier in this entry — so relaunched from a terminal with `--enable-logging=file --log-file=...`, the same technique used diagnosing #202 on the Windows VM): an `UnhandledPromiseRejectionWarning` from `getDb()` during IPC-handler registration — `better-sqlite3`'s prebuilt `linux-x64.node` requires `GLIBC_2.33`, not found. The main process crashes before ever creating a `BrowserWindow`, which is why GPU/zygote/network helper processes still start (they spin up independently) while no UI appears — not a display/rendering bug. **Root cause confirmed by hash, not assumed**: downloaded the pristine `better-sqlite3@13.0.3` npm package directly (`npm pack`, independent of any build machine) and checked its own shipped `prebuilds/linux-x64.node` — sha256 `6fd4292c...`, needing GLIBC up to **2.34** — **byte-for-byte identical** to what the CI build shipped. So CI did nothing wrong; it shipped exactly what upstream publishes. This machine's own "local build" (sha256 `8c8e204d...`, needing only GLIBC 2.29) was never a deliberate, reproducible packaging choice — it accidentally reused this machine's `node_modules`, which `fix-native-modules.sh` had already patched in place for `npm run dev` purposes (unrelated to packaging) before `electron-builder` ever ran, via a clean rebuild-from-source against this box's deliberately old `gcc-10`. A fresh `npm ci` anywhere else gets the real, unmodified 2.34-requiring upstream binary.

  **Decision (made with the user, 2026-10-13): keep the honest 2.34/Ubuntu-22.04+ floor for the distributed CI artifact; do not engineer a low-glibc rebuild into CI.** Considered and rejected: forcing `linux-dist.yml` to rebuild `better-sqlite3` from source inside an old-glibc Docker container (`ubuntu-20.04` hosted runners were themselves retired by GitHub in April 2025, so this would need a `container:` pin, e.g. `ubuntu:20.04`, plus manually installing build tools inside it) — real, untested engineering effort for a benefit (restoring literal Ubuntu 20.04 support) that isn't actually needed: 20.04 is already past its standard support window, and 22.04 LTS is an entirely normal baseline. The user's actual concern — "we need to develop and test on this 20.04 machine" — turned out to be already covered without any CI change: `npm run dev` never depended on the distributed package's glibc floor (it always used this machine's own already-patched `node_modules`), and building a package *locally* on this machine (`npm run dist:linux`) naturally keeps reusing that same patched `node_modules`, producing a GLIBC-2.29 artifact this machine can keep running for its own verification — exactly what happened today. **So: CI ships the honest, reproducible 2.34 floor for real distribution; local builds on this machine remain the way to test a packaged build here, unchanged from today's workflow.** `README.md`'s prerequisites bullet corrected to **"Ubuntu 22.04 LTS or newer"**, sourced directly from the pristine upstream prebuild, not from any one build machine's state.

  **Workaround used to unblock the user's test, same session**: `sudo apt remove sachivji` then reinstall from this machine's own local `release/sachivji_0.2.2_amd64.deb` (GLIBC 2.29) instead of the CI artifact — confirmed working, and became the live-verification build for the rest of this entry's confirmations below.

  **User's own follow-up confirmation**: "the app worked well with the Python program" — a real Python script run succeeded through the rebuilt bundled-interpreter venv, not just a "ready" status in Settings. Combined with the `pyvenv.cfg`/Ollama-UI/Context7 confirmations above, every bundled runtime (#207) is now proven working for actual use, not just present.

  **Full upgrade-path live verification, 2026-10-13, using the corrected (local) build on this machine** — closes out the main remaining gap from this whole #202/#207/#208 arc: whether the bundled runtimes actually work end to end, not just package correctly. User deleted the workspace's existing `venv/` (built from this machine's own system Python over months of dev use) specifically to force a rebuild from the bundled interpreter, then reinstalled and relaunched:
  - **Bundled Python, confirmed by direct file evidence, not inferred from a version number coincidence**: Settings > Runtime reported "Ready · Python 3.12.15"; checked the rebuilt venv's own `pyvenv.cfg` directly — `home = /opt/Sachivji/resources/python-linux-x64/bin`, `executable = .../python-linux-x64/bin/python3.12` — genuinely the bundled interpreter, and meaningfully so: this machine's own system `python3` resolves to a *different* install (`3.12.2` via a `~/.local/bin` pyenv-style shim), so `detectPython()` correctly preferred the bundled candidate over a real, available system one, exactly as designed.
  - **Bundled Ollama, confirmed via the Settings UI directly**: "Local embeddings (bundled Ollama) — Ready — nomic-embed-text embedding model loaded," matching the 262MB of real model blobs already observed on disk from the engineer's own earlier smoke test.
  - **Bundled Node, confirmed via live app logs**: the Context7 MCP server (configured via `npx`) connected successfully during this same relaunch.
  - All three bundled runtimes are now proven working **end to end on real hardware** for the Linux build, not just packaged correctly — the strongest verification level reached across #202/#207/#208.
- [ ] 208. **PRIORITY when next working on a Mac — crosscheck the Ollama Metal/MLX bundling choice on real Apple Silicon hardware.** Self-contained macOS build (Apple Silicon only; Intel Mac deliberately deferred) — user request 2026-10-13, extending #202 (Windows)/#207 (Linux) to macOS. **Implemented and wired this session on the Linux dev machine, but — unlike #207 — could not be executed or live-verified at all here**: this box is x64 Linux with no Rosetta/macOS emulation, so nothing arm64 could actually be run. Everything below is unit-tested/typecheck-clean/config-correct, not run.

  **The real complication, found by direct inspection (not assumed), that doesn't exist on Windows/Linux**: Ollama's official macOS release (`ollama-darwin.tgz`, 167MB compressed) ships a universal (`x86_64`+`arm64`) main binary, but its CPU backend library (`libggml-base.dylib`) is **x86_64-only** — there is no separate per-microarchitecture arm64 CPU library the way Windows/Linux have one per-arch CPU fallback. Apple Silicon's actual compute path instead appears to run through two large Metal/MLX shader bundles, `mlx_metal_v3/` (188MB) and `mlx_metal_v4/` (219MB) — likely tied to different Apple GPU generations (M1/M2 vs M3/M4-class). Because this machine cannot execute the result, there was no way to confirm by testing (the way CUDA-exclusion was confirmed by literally running `ollama --version` after stripping it on both Windows-shaped and Linux builds) which of these, if either, is safe to drop.

  **Decision, made explicitly with the user rather than guessed**: bundle conservatively — keep **both** `mlx_metal_v3` and `mlx_metal_v4` in full, and strip **only** the unambiguously x86-only per-microarchitecture CPU libs (`libggml-cpu-{alderlake,cannonlake,cascadelake,cooperlake,haswell,icelake,ivybridge,piledriver,sandybridge,sapphirerapids,skylakex,sse42,x64,zen4}.so` — every one named for an Intel/AMD microarchitecture, never loaded on Apple Silicon). Result: the macOS Ollama bundle is **~480MB**, far bigger than Windows' 66MB or Linux's 103MB — a deliberate, disclosed tradeoff for safety over size, not an oversight. **Follow-up, marked priority above**: once on real Apple Silicon hardware, verify the app actually works, then look for further safe reductions (e.g. whether only one of v3/v4 is ever loaded on the hardware being tested, via `/var/log` or Ollama's own logs during a real embed call) — but never strip anything without that live confirmation first, same standard as every other #202/#207 exclusion.

  **Answered a direct user question first: is a universal (arm64+x64) build possible?** Yes — electron-builder supports it via `@electron/universal` (lipo-merges a built x64 `.app` and arm64 `.app`; both `better-sqlite3`/`sqlite-vec` already ship `darwin-x64`/`darwin-arm64` prebuilds, and Ollama's own binary is already universal). But Python and Node have no universal macOS build from their upstreams — a universal Sachivji build would need both an arm64 and an x64 copy of each bundled side by side, plus a new `process.arch` branch in the runtime lookups, roughly doubling that part of the bundle. **User's explicit decision: Apple Silicon only for this round**, matching the original instruction; nothing built here is wasted if/when universal is revisited (Ollama's piece already covers both archs for free).

  **Built, three new scripts mirroring the Windows/Linux ones** (`scripts/fetch-bundled-{python,ollama,node}-mac.mjs`, using `scripts/fetch-utils.mjs` unchanged): Python from `python-build-standalone`'s `aarch64-apple-darwin-install_only_stripped` build (verified via `pip download` with `--platform macosx_11_0_arm64` that all 5 pinned packages + transitive deps resolve — 16 wheels, same count as every other platform); Node from `nodejs.org`'s official `darwin-arm64` tarball (confirmed via a streamed `tar -tz` listing, without downloading the whole archive first, that its `bin/`/`lib/node_modules` layout matches Linux's, not Windows'). All three fetched and inspected live on this machine (checksums verified, architectures confirmed via `file` — Python/Node are plain arm64 Mach-O, Ollama's main binary is universal) — the fetching and inspection work fine here; only *running* the arm64 code doesn't.

  **`electron-builder.yml`**: added `mac.target` as an explicit `{ target: dmg, arch: [arm64] }` (not the bare `dmg` string) so electron-builder can never default to the host arch or a universal build by accident; added `mac.extraResources` for the three fetched directories (no `node_modules`-split workaround needed here, same reasoning as Linux — Node's macOS tarball also nests `npm` under `lib/node_modules`, never a path literally named `"node_modules"` relative to the copy root).

  **Code changes**, generalizing the same three resource-lookup functions touched for #207 to also recognize `darwin`: `workspace-runtime.ts`'s `bundledPython`/`bundledWheelhouse` (`python-mac-arm64/bin/python3`); `ollama-runtime.ts`'s `bundledExe` (`ollama-mac-arm64/ollama` — flat layout, no `bin/` prefix, unlike Linux); `mcp/client.ts`'s `defaultBundledNodeDir` (`node-mac-arm64/bin`, same shape as Linux). `npm run typecheck` stayed clean and the full suite stayed at 1,614/1,614 (no new tests needed — same reasoning as #207: none of the three Windows-only gates had dedicated unit tests of the real `defaultRuntimeDeps()`/`defaultOllamaRuntimeDeps()`/`defaultBundledNodeDir()` implementations to begin with, only of the injected-harness-mocked behavior).
