- [x] 209. Right-click a misspelled word in the message composer to fix it — user observation 2026-10-13. The red squiggly underline the user sees is Chromium's own built-in spellchecker (on by default in Electron, not an OS feature — confirmed by checking: nothing in this codebase calls `session.setSpellCheckerEnabled`, so it's just Electron's default), but right-clicking did nothing because **no `context-menu` handler was wired up at all** (confirmed: zero matches for `context-menu`/`contextMenu`/`spellcheck` anywhere in `src/main`). **Implemented 2026-10-13**, same session: new `src/main/window/context-menu.ts` (same split-pure-logic-from-Electron-calls shape as `window/zoom.ts`/`window/bounds.ts`) — `buildContextMenuActions` is pure data (testable without mocking `Menu`), using Chromium's own already-correct `params.editFlags` (`canCut`/`canCopy`/`canPaste`/`canSelectAll`) rather than re-deriving them from `isEditable`/`selectionText`; `attachSpellcheckContextMenu` turns that into a real `Menu.buildFromTemplate(...).popup()` — each suggestion as its own item (`webContents.replaceMisspelling`), then "Add to Dictionary" (`webContents.session.addWordToSpellCheckerDictionary`), then Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All only where Chromium says they apply; returns no menu at all for a plain right-click on non-editable, non-misspelled, no-selection content, matching today's "nothing happens" there rather than popping up something empty. Wired into `createMainWindow()` alongside the existing zoom/bounds attachments. 5 new tests. Full suite 1,634/1,634, typecheck clean, `npm run build` clean. **Live-confirmed by the user 2026-10-13** on the local build.
- [x] 210. `python_run`/`shell_execute` hang when the script they run blocks on interactive stdin — user observation 2026-10-13, while live-testing the Linux self-contained build (#207). **Fail-fast fix implemented 2026-10-14 (explicit user decision: fail fast now, the full interactive terminal pane below is real but deferred to the next version — track it separately, not as part of 0.3.0).** **Live-tested 2026-10-14 on this machine against a real model** — the fail-fast behavior (stdin closed, actionable hint on `EOFError`) confirmed working, not just unit-tested. The interactive terminal pane itself remains a separate, deferred follow-up (see below).

  **Fixed 2026-10-14 — stdin is now actually closed instead of left as an open, unconnected pipe.** `execFile`/`exec` don't accept a `stdio` option (confirmed live: passing one is silently ignored, since they always pipe all three streams themselves to buffer output for the callback — this was tried first and the repro still hung the full timeout). The real fix: capture the `ChildProcess` both functions already return synchronously and call `child.stdin?.end()` immediately after spawning (`python-run.ts`, `shell.ts`). A script calling `input()` (or bash's `read`) now hits an immediate `EOFError`/empty read instead of blocking until the timeout kills it. `python-run.ts` also recognizes the resulting `EOFError`/closed-file traceback and appends an actionable hint ("python_run does not support reading stdin — rewrite as a parametrized function call") rather than leaving the model to interpret a bare traceback. New tests in both files confirm the failure is fast (well under the old timeout) and, for python_run, that the hint appears. Full suite 1,637/1,637, typecheck/build clean. **Not yet live-tested against a real model** — the exact repro from TASKS.md's own suggestion ("write a script that asks the user for two numbers and adds them") hasn't been re-run live to confirm the model gets an actionable failure rather than being confused by it.

  **Still genuinely open, deferred to the next version (not 0.3.0) per the user's own call:** the real fix below — a live interactive terminal pane — which actually lets the user answer a script's prompt rather than just failing fast. Keep this design sketch for when that work starts.

  **The problem, confirmed in the code, not just observed:** neither tool ever writes to or closes the spawned child process's stdin (`python-run.ts`, `shell.ts` — both just pass `{ timeout, maxBuffer, signal }` to Node's `child_process.exec`/`execFile`, which defaults every stream, stdin included, to an open, unconnected pipe). If the script the agent wrote calls Python's `input()` (or reads from stdin at all), the child process blocks indefinitely waiting for data that will never arrive — there is no path, today, for the user to supply it. This isn't a permanent app freeze: `python_run` has a timeout (`DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_S = 120`, up to `MAX_TIMEOUT_S = 600` if the model asks for longer) and `shell_execute` has a flat `TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000`, so Node's `timeout` option eventually kills the child and the tool call returns "timed out," unblocking the turn. But for up to that whole window, the user watches the app wait on something they have no way to answer, and whatever the script meant to do *after* the blocked `input()` call never runs — the turn just fails, not partially succeeds.

  **Why this is a real gap, not a misuse case:** the Developer role is told to run Python scripts for real work (charts, data processing, Office docs) and nothing in its prompt forbids reading user input — a model asked to "write a script that asks the user for two numbers and adds them" (a perfectly ordinary beginner-Python request) will naturally reach for `input()`, and the agent has no way to know in advance that doing so dooms the run. This will recur any time a script's logic is written as "ask interactively" rather than "take arguments," which is a common, idiomatic way to write small Python/shell scripts — not an edge case.

  **Proposed real fix (user's own suggestion, matches the right direction): a live, interactive terminal pane for `python_run`/`shell_execute`,** instead of today's "spawn, capture everything, return once it exits" model. Sketch of what this would need:
  - **Process model**: spawn through a pseudo-terminal (e.g. `node-pty`, not plain `child_process`) so the child gets a real interactive terminal (line editing, prompts that flush immediately rather than buffering, matching what a human would see running it themselves) — plain pipes are what make today's `input()` prompt invisible until the process already blocked.
  - **UI**: a live-streaming terminal view (likely a new tool-output display mode alongside the existing shown/collapsed/Hidden states — see #181) that appends output as it arrives and accepts keystrokes, wired via a new IPC channel pair (renderer → main: keystrokes in; main → renderer: output chunks out) rather than the current single request/response `invoke`.
  - **Lifecycle questions to settle before building**: does this replace the timeout entirely, or still auto-kill after some (much longer, or user-extendable) ceiling if the user never responds? Does the agent's turn stay "pending" the whole time a human might be typing, and if so how is that distinguished from a genuinely stuck/runaway process in the UI? Does Stop still work mid-interactive-session the same way it does today? Should this be offered only when a tool call's output *looks* like a prompt (heuristic, unreliable) or always available as a visible "attach" affordance on any running `python_run`/`shell_execute` call (simpler, more honest about the uncertainty)?
  - **Scope note**: this changes `python_run`/`shell_execute` from the stateless, fire-and-forget tool calls every other tool in this app is — worth deciding whether the confirmation-before-running flow still fully covers the risk surface once a human can keep typing into a running process indefinitely, though since the user already approved running this exact script, letting them answer its own prompts isn't a new privilege beyond what was already granted.

  **Lighter stopgap, implemented 2026-10-13 (same session)**: added one rule to the Developer role's system prompt (`roles.ts`, the `coding` role) banning scripts that wait on interactive input — names `input()`/bash `read` specifically, explains *why* (neither tool connects stdin to anything, so the run just hangs until timeout). **Refined on the user's own suggestion**: rather than a bare hard-coded constant, the rule now says to put the logic in a parametrized function and call it with the real values — more reusable (the same function can be called again with different values if the user asks), and more idiomatic than inlining values directly. Only the Developer role prompts `python_run`/`shell_execute` at all (confirmed by grep — no other role's prompt mentions either tool), so one edit covers every path. Bumped the `role:coding` prompt-budget ceiling (`prompt-budget.test.ts`) from 3120 to 3200 with a dated comment, same convention as every other deliberate-growth bump in that file — full suite 1,614/1,614, typecheck clean. **Does not fix** the cases this was always scoped to leave open: a user explicitly asking for an interactive script, or a pre-existing/user-supplied script that already reads stdin — those still need the real fix (the interactive terminal pane) above. Not live-tested against a real model yet (would need the exact repro — asking for "a script that asks for two numbers" — re-run to confirm the model actually follows the new rule rather than writing `input()` anyway).
- [x] 211. A mid-run steering comment on an already-completed delegated task triggers a wasteful full re-delegation, and leaves debris behind — found 2026-10-13 live-testing #201's "Approve & send comment" fix. **All three findings fixed and live-retested 2026-10-14 with the exact original repro — see #201's own entry for the full retest writeup.**

  **Prompts that triggered it, in order** (session `472f63f8-7f04-4e85-b02c-8663517c2cf1`, Agent picker on Auto): (1) `"Write a short Python script that prints the numbers 1 to 5, and run it."` — this got a `python_run` confirmation; (2) at that confirmation, clicked **Talk about it** → typed `"also save the output to a file called numbers.txt"` → clicked the new **Approve & send comment** (#201). #201 itself worked correctly: the approved call ran for real (`code/print_numbers.py` written and executed, printed `1`-`5`) and the comment reached the model as a `user_note` at the start of the next round, confirmed directly in `sachiv.db`.

  **Finding 1 — a stray leftover file, acknowledged by the model but not cleaned up.** Handling the follow-up comment, the Developer wrote a brand-new wrapper script (passed as `code`, not `script`) that re-runs `print_numbers.py` via `subprocess` just to capture its stdout into `numbers.txt`. `python_run` auto-saves any inline `code` block under a timestamped filename (`code/run_20261013-211153.py`) — confirmed in the tool's own result text: `"script: code/run_20261013-211153.py (saved from code; to re-run it UNCHANGED, pass script ALONE...)"`. The final reply even says so — *"The save step also left a helper file, `code/run_20261013-211153.py`, which you can delete if [not needed]"* — the model correctly notices the debris it just created but doesn't remove it itself.
  - **Fixed 2026-10-14, prompt steering (chosen over auto-deleting `python_run`'s own saved file — that would contradict the tool's deliberate design, "kept so the run is reproducible and the user can open, edit and re-run it," and risks deleting a script the user actually wanted).** New rule in the Developer role prompt (`roles.ts`): a follow-up that extends something already written this conversation is a small edit to that SAME script (`code` again, or `write_file` + `script`), not a reason to wrap it in a new throwaway one; and if it genuinely does create a one-off helper file, delete it itself (`shell_execute rm` / `os.remove`) rather than leaving it behind and only mentioning it. `role:coding` prompt-budget ceiling raised 3191 -> 3400 (measured 3342) with a dated comment. **Live-retested 2026-10-14 with the exact repro (see #201's entry) — confirmed: the Developer edited the same `code/print_numbers.py` in place and no new wrapper/helper file appeared.**

  **Finding 2 — the follow-up comment triggered a full re-delegation from scratch instead of a small addition.** Rather than treating "also save the output to numbers.txt" as a lightweight extension of work already done and confirmed, the orchestrator issued a brand-new `delegate_to_coding` call with the entire task re-stated (`"Save the output of the script code/print_numbers.py... Run the script to produce the output and write it to..."`). The Developer then re-read `code/print_numbers.py` (content already known from the first round) and re-ran it from scratch via the new wrapper script, rather than reusing the already-confirmed-working result. Functionally correct, but redundant work and an extra live confirmation the user didn't need to see again for something this small.
  - **Fixed 2026-10-14, prompt steering at the top level (`system-prompt.ts`).** `coding` isn't `isolated`, so the specialist already receives the full parent conversation (its own prior reply included) on a second delegation — the gap was purely in how the orchestrator *worded* the new task, restating everything from zero instead of framing it as an addition. New rule: a note arriving right after a specialist already finished and reported done is a small addition, not a new task — when delegating a follow-up back to the SAME specialist, say only what's new, naming the file/result it already produced. `routerPrompt`/`slimRouterBare` ceilings raised (1730 -> 1850, 4120 -> 4220) with dated comments. **Live-retested 2026-10-14 with the exact repro (see #201's entry) — confirmed: the follow-up `delegate_to_coding` task text explicitly referenced "code/print_numbers.py, which you already wrote and ran" rather than restating the original task.**

  **Finding 3 — the delegate's reply text is stored twice.** Confirmed directly in `sachiv.db`: the Developer's own reply ("I wrote the script to `code/print_numbers.py`...") appears as its own `assistant` message, and again, verbatim, wrapped as the `tool_result` for the orchestrator's `delegate_to_coding` call — same pattern repeated for the second delegation's reply too.
  - **Checked directly, not just taken on faith: the live chat view (`ChatView.svelte`) already never renders a resolved `delegate_to_*` call's `tool_result` at all** — confirmed by reading the actual markup (the `isDelegate` branch has no case for `result !== undefined`), with its own comment explaining why: the specialist's own attributed segment right after it already shows the answer. So this was never visibly duplicated live, only in storage, as the TASKS note here already suspected. **But Markdown export (`export.ts`) and the Print/PDF view (`PrintView.svelte`) had no equivalent exclusion** — a real, confirmed gap: in `toolDisplayMode: 'shown'`, both genuinely printed the specialist's reply twice back to back. **Fixed 2026-10-14**: both now build a `toolUseId -> isDelegate` map across the session (same pattern as the live view's `toolSegmentsById` in `App.svelte`) and omit a delegate call's `tool_result` block unconditionally, matching the live view. New test in `export.test.ts` covers it directly (asserts the reply text appears exactly once). **Checked against the real retest session 2026-10-14** (see #201's entry): `window.sachiv.listMessages` on that real session confirmed both delegate `tool_result` contents really are verbatim prefixes of the Developer's own `text` messages — the exact shape this fix targets, now proven against real data, not just the synthetic test. `PrintView.svelte`'s fix is still unit-verified only via typecheck/build — actual PDF rendering wasn't re-run live this session, only the underlying data shape was confirmed to match.
- [x] 212. Chat export drops images — found 2026-10-14 by the user testing the macOS build (#208). When a chat shows images (generated charts, stock images), the exported **PDF** and **Markdown** both leave them out. **Not started.** Requested behaviour: **PDF** — embed each image inline, at the place where it appears in the chat. **Markdown** — write the images into a folder next to the file with the same base name (`chat.md` → `chat/`) and reference them with relative links (`![alt](chat/image-1.png)`), so the export stays portable when the file and folder are moved together. The **JSON** export follows the Markdown approach (user decision 2026-10-14): images go to the same-named folder and the JSON references them by relative path, not embedded data. Code is in `src/main/sessions/export.ts` (+ `export.test.ts`). Open questions: how the chat refers to images (local workspace paths vs data URLs — the alt text carries the local file path, a known leftover), what to do with a missing/moved file, and file-name collisions in the image folder. Also affects the Save dialog: the Markdown and JSON exports now write a file plus a folder, so the dialog should say so.

  **Done 2026-10-15, verified live** (throwaway copy of the app with a copy of the user's DB, a stubbed save dialog, all three formats exported from the real session that has 3 `show_media` images; 1,644 tests, typecheck clean).
  - **Root cause of the PDF half:** `src/renderer/print.html` had its own Content-Security-Policy without `img-src data:` (the main window's has it), so every embedded image rendered as a broken-image box showing the file path. Added `img-src 'self' data:`; a test now guards it. Result: `pdfimages` finds the three 100x100 pictures in the PDF (before: none). The print page now also waits for `<img>.decode()` before `printToPDF` (a precaution; the CSP was the real cause).
  - **Markdown / JSON:** `collectImages` in `export.ts` writes each image block's payload to `<file name without extension>/image-N.<ext>` next to the export (numbered in transcript order, identical pictures share one file, extension from the mime type, no folder when there are no images). Markdown links `![Displayed image: <path>](<Folder%20Name>/image-N.png)` (percent-encoded); JSON keeps each image block minus `dataBase64`, plus `file: "<folder>/image-N.png"`. Without a saved copy (the pure functions called bare) the old text note is still produced.
  - **Not done:** the Save dialog does not mention the extra folder (no dialog text hook; the folder is created silently next to the file). An existing folder of the same name is reused and same-named image files are overwritten.

- [ ] 214. **Decide and fix: in packaged builds the "Ollama (local)" chat provider is routed to the bundled, embedding-only Ollama** — found 2026-10-15 while writing the Developer Guide (providers page). **Not fixed; needs the user's decision.** `ipc/register.ts` sets `setBundledOllamaBaseUrl(state.baseUrl)` on every `OllamaRuntime` change, and `providers/setup.ts` then points **every** `ollama` provider instance (chat in the registry **and** `getEmbeddingProvider('ollama')`) at that URL. The bundled server runs with `OLLAMA_MODELS=<userData>/ollama-models`, which only ever holds `nomic-embed-text`, so (1) models the user installed with their own Ollama (`~/.ollama`) are not listed or usable for chat in the installers; (2) the bundled server prefers port 11434 when free, so a user who starts their own Ollama afterwards finds the port taken while Sachivji runs (and with their Ollama already on 11434 the bundled one moves to 11435 and chat is *still* redirected to it). Intended for the sponsor build (no local chat model, TASKS #202/#207/#208), but it is wrong for a general user with their own Ollama. **Options:** (a) give the bundled server a dedicated port that is never 11434 and route **only embeddings** to it (e.g. a separate embedding provider id such as `ollama-bundled`, or a `bundled` flag), leaving `ollama` chat on the user's own default; (b) keep today's behaviour and document it (done in the User Guide, page 03 "known limitation"); (c) let Settings choose the Ollama base URL for chat. Also related: the stale comments/messages that still say the bundled Ollama exists only in the Windows sponsor build (`ipc/register.ts`, `ollama-runtime.ts`). **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.3.2. Second revision the same day: the bundled Ollama is a FALLBACK ONLY and provides only embeddings in 0.3.2 (my reading, to confirm: this is the original option (a)); it uses a non-standard port in the range 11450 to 11455 (first free, never 11434); the "Ollama (local)" chat provider is no longer routed to it; chat and embeddings keep separate code paths and no provider id is renamed, so the search index stays valid. The earlier idea of using the bundled server for chat moves to #222 (0.4.0) and #223. **BUCKET MOVED to 0.4.0 on 2026-10-19 (Deepak: "This all goes in 0.4.0 now", with #224 and #225; earlier 0.3.2). FINAL SCOPE decided 2026-10-19 (Deepak): the embedded Ollama serves ONLY embeddings, always, with `nomic-embed-text`, on a non-standard port in 11450 to 11455 (never 11434); the "Ollama (local)" chat provider uses the user's own Ollama; when it is not available it is hidden from the model selection and Settings shows the Ollama address and/or install instructions (address editable and may point to an Ollama on the network: confirmed 2026-10-19; availability refreshes without a restart: confirmed). #222 and #223 are closed; follow-ups #224 and #225.** See `docs/release_triage.md`.**
- [ ] 215. **Bug: a pinned specialist does not state its role or what it can do when greeted** — found 2026-10-15 while reviewing the User Guide (page 05). `docs/manual_testing.md` 11.6 expects: pin each specialist and say hello, and each states its own role. **Probed 2026-10-15 on `gpt-4o-mini`** (fresh single-message conversation per role, via `OpenAIProvider` + `Orchestrator` directly, no UI): all 12 pinned roles replied with a bare greeting (e.g. "Hello! How can I assist you today?"); and to "What can you do?" all 12 gave a generic "tell me what you need" reply, with no role statement and no capability list. Only **Auto** answered "What can you do?" well (a real list). **Re-probed the same way on `claude-haiku-5-5` (Auto, research, coding, data, document-creator; "Hello" and "What can you do?"): every one answered correctly** — "Hello" gave a role statement plus abilities (e.g. "Hello! I'm the Researcher. I can look up information on the web…"), and "What can you do?" gave a full, role-specific list. So the failure is **model-dependent**: test 11.6 passes on Haiku, and `gpt-4o-mini` (and probably other small models) ignores the role prompt for a bare greeting. Not run through the UI. **Not started.** **Owner's requirement (2026-10-15): not to be written off as "weak model".** The model is meant to learn what it can do from the tools (`describe_capabilities` / `get_environment`), not from its own knowledge, and the weakest supported models must handle a greeting or "what can you do?" well. To be investigated later (not scheduled): check whether pinned roles even have those tools, and whether the prompt makes a weak model call them. Likely direction (to help weaker models): the role prompt should say that, when greeted or asked what it can do, the specialist states its role and its main abilities (and may call `describe_capabilities`). **Docs follow-up (user decision 2026-10-15):** once fixed, add this tip back to `docs/user-guide/05-agents-and-roles.md` under Tips: pick each role in the Agent selector, send "Hello", and it replies with what it can do; fall back to "What can you do?". **Triage 2026-10-19 (Deepak): bucket 0.3.2 (role-prompt sentence, re-probe on small models, User Guide 05 tip).**

Requested: an **Add folder…** button (native folder picker) so the user can grant access to places like Downloads, Desktop or Documents in advance, instead of waiting for the in-chat confirmation. **Not started.** Points to settle when it is built: (1) a grant added here is the same *read-only* grant as *Always* (covers the folder and everything below it; creating a NEW file outside still asks each time; editing and deleting stay unavailable); (2) the existing blocked list (`.ssh`, `.aws`, keychains, the app's own data and so on, `BLOCKED_UNDER_HOME` / `BLOCKED_ABSOLUTE`) must still win, so the picker must refuse or warn on a blocked folder, and on the home directory or a drive root (too broad); (3) offer quick-add shortcuts for the usual folders (Downloads, Desktop, Documents) if cheap; (4) de-duplicate and normalise paths, and handle a chosen folder that is already inside a granted one; (5) update the settings text, User Guide pages 08 ("Folders outside the workspace") and 10, the Developer Guide, and add a `docs/manual_testing.md` case next to 14.3. - [ ] 217. **TODO (Deepak, 2026-10-15): external APIs that need both an API key and a session token; make the connection set-up introspect the service and configure itself.** Some web services want an API key (or user name and password) to log in, return a **session token**, and expect that token on the following calls, often with an expiry and a re-login. Today an external-API connection (Settings > External APIs) holds ONE static credential (**No auth / API key (header or query) / Bearer token / Basic auth**) sent on every call, and the optional OpenAPI/Swagger spec URL is used only to discover endpoints. **Not started.** Requested: enhance the interface so the app **introspects** the service (from the OpenAPI spec's security schemes, or by probing) and **configures the connection**, including the login step. Points to settle: (1) model the auth flow in the connection: login endpoint, request body or headers, where the token is in the response, how it is sent afterwards (header or query), expiry and automatic re-login; (2) both the key and the session token are secrets (OS keychain or the file store, never in chat, never in logs or exports); (3) calls that change things keep asking for confirmation, and the login call itself must be visible to the user; (4) fall back to a manual form for services that do not describe themselves; (5) update User Guide page 09 (the "Limit today" paragraph under External web services), the Developer Guide and `docs/manual_testing.md`.

- [x] 219. **Fixed two real bugs in the published `v0.3.0` GitHub Release, found 2026-10-16 when the user reported "Windows is not allowing to download the exe file."**

  **Finding 1 (the one reported) — the public Windows `.exe` asset is blocked/warned-on by browser download-reputation checks.** Confirmed via direct comparison: the sponsor build the user downloaded cleanly during #202's testing was a GitHub **Actions artifact** (`windows-dist.yml` → `actions/upload-artifact`), which GitHub always wraps in a zip for download regardless of contents — never a bare `.exe`. The `v0.3.0` tag release is different: `release.yml` runs `electron-builder --win --publish always`, which uploads the raw NSIS `Sachivji-Setup-0.3.0.exe` straight to the public Release page. Edge/Chrome's download-reputation checks now commonly block or heavily warn on an unsigned `.exe` from a low-traffic/unrecognized publisher — that's the actual mechanism behind "not allowing to download," not a GitHub-side restriction. **Fixed in `.github/workflows/release.yml`**: after electron-builder publishes, a new Windows-only step re-zips the already-uploaded `Setup.exe` locally, uploads the `.zip` as a release asset (`gh release upload --clobber`), then deletes the raw `.exe` and `.exe.blockmap` assets (`gh release delete-asset`) so only the zip remains on the Release page. The NSIS wizard inside is untouched — same install experience as before, just one extra extract step. `latest.yml` (used only for `electron-updater`'s version-check, which never auto-downloads — see `src/main/updater.ts`) is left as electron-builder generated it; harmless that it still names the now-deleted `.exe`, since nothing in this app ever calls `downloadUpdate()`. Updated `docs/windows_install.md` (download/reinstall steps) to point at the Release page's `.zip` asset instead of the expiring (14-day) Actions-run link it had been using.

  **Finding 2 (found investigating Finding 1, not what was reported) — `release.yml` never fetched the bundled Python/Ollama/Node runtime on ANY of the three platforms, so the published `v0.3.0` Windows/macOS/Linux installers are all missing every bundled runtime from #202/#207/#208.** Root cause: the bundled-runtime fetch only runs via each platform's `predist:*` npm script (e.g. `predist:win` → `fetch-bundled-{python,ollama,node}-win.mjs`), which npm's lifecycle only auto-triggers before `npm run dist:win` — never before a direct `electron-builder` invocation. `release.yml` calls `electron-builder` directly (`npm run build && npx electron-builder --win --publish always`), skipping `predist:*` entirely. electron-builder treats a missing `extraResources` source as a non-fatal warning, not a build failure, so the gap shipped silently. **Confirmed directly in the actual CI log** for the `v0.3.0` release run (`gh run view 38525128836 --log`): `file source doesn't exist  from=.../resources/python-win-x64` (and the matching `ollama-win-x64`/`node-win-x64` lines), plus the identical pattern for `resources/python-mac-arm64`/`node-mac-arm64` and `resources/python-linux-x64`/`node-linux-x64` on the other two platforms. **Fixed**: `release.yml`'s matrix now carries a `predist` script name per OS (`predist:mac`/`predist:win`/`predist:linux`) and runs it (`npm run ${{ matrix.predist }}`) before `npm run build`, matching the order `npm run dist:*` already uses.

  **Verifying this live surfaced two more bugs, both in the fix itself, caught before anything public shipped:**

  - **The Windows zip step's own filename pattern was wrong.** First real tag push (`v0.3.1`, run `38862656542`): `predist:win` ran clean (no missing-resource warning — finding 2 confirmed fixed for real), electron-builder published normally, but the new "re-zip" step failed — `Get-ChildItem release -Filter "Sachivji-Setup-*.exe"` (hyphens) matched nothing. The real on-disk NSIS output keeps a space, `release\Sachivji Setup 0.3.1.exe` (confirmed in the build log's own `file=` line); electron-builder only sanitizes the space to a hyphen for the *uploaded GitHub asset name*, not the local file. **Fixed**: match the local file with the space (`"Sachivji Setup *.exe"`), then derive the GitHub asset name to delete by replacing spaces with hyphens on the matched file's own name, instead of hardcoding either form.
  - **A pre-existing race condition, unrelated to anything in this task, that was never hit before by luck of timing.** Re-running the (now filename-fixed) workflow on a fresh `v0.3.1` tag, all three platform jobs succeeded individually, but `gh api repos/.../releases` showed **two separate draft releases both tagged `v0.3.1`**, created in the same second, assets split across them (one had the Windows zip + `latest.yml`, the other had the dmg/AppImage/deb + the other two `latest-*.yml`). Root cause, confirmed by reading `electron-publish`'s `gitHubPublisher.js` (`getOrCreateRelease()`): it lists existing releases and reuses one matching the tag, but only creates one itself if none exists yet — with three `--publish always` calls running in parallel against a brand-new tag, more than one job can hit the "doesn't exist yet" branch before any of them has finished creating it. (`v0.3.0`'s single run happened not to race this way — not a guarantee, just luck of relative job timing.) **Fixed**: added a `create-release` job that runs first (`gh release create "$GITHUB_REF_NAME" --draft`, idempotent — skips if the release already exists) and made the platform matrix `needs: create-release`, so every platform job's own lookup always finds the release already there instead of racing to create it.

  **Live-verified clean end to end on the real third run (`v0.3.1`, tag re-pushed after both fixes above)**: exactly one draft release for the tag, all 9 expected assets present on it (`Sachivji-Setup-0.3.1.zip` in place of the `.exe`/`.exe.blockmap`, `Sachivji-0.3.1-arm64.dmg`+blockmap, `Sachivji-0.3.1.AppImage`, `sachivji_0.3.1_amd64.deb`, all three `latest*.yml`), and the full CI log for all three platforms has zero `file source doesn't exist` lines — confirmed by grepping the raw log, not inferred from the jobs showing green. Not yet checked: actually downloading and running the zip/dmg/deb on real hardware (the `v0.3.0` sponsor-build live-install testing covered the mechanism being bundled; this round only verifies the release pipeline produces the right artifacts, not a fresh install smoke-test on each OS).

  **Practical consequence for `v0.3.0`**: anyone who already downloaded that Windows exe (if they got past the browser block) has an installer with no bundled Python/Ollama/Node — it only works if Ollama/Python happen to already be on that machine, unlike what `docs/windows_install.md` promises. None of this task's fixes are retroactive; `v0.3.0`'s own assets are unchanged. `v0.3.1` is the first release built with the fixed pipeline.

  - **Likely folded into #210** (live, pty-based interactive terminal pane for `python_run`/`shell_execute`, deferred past 0.3.0): with a real pseudo-terminal the user types the `sudo` password into the terminal pane themselves, so it never passes through the model, chat history or DB by construction. That makes (b)–(d) below largely moot, leaving (a), (e) and (f) — and the decision that the pane's keystrokes are never captured into tool output sent back to the model. Settle this when #210 is built.
  - Related: #210 (interactive terminal), #33 (shell tool), #107 (venv hard-wiring in `shell_execute`), #156 (OS/shell-dialect targeting gap).

Also update User Guide 03 (providers) and Developer Guide 08 once built. **CLOSED 2026-10-19 (Deepak's hard decision): the embedded Ollama is used only for embeddings (always, `nomic-embed-text`); chat uses the user's own Ollama when available. Sachivji does not download models or run Modelfiles; users manage models with their own Ollama. Not needed.**
- [x] 223. **TODO (Deepak, 2026-10-19), for 0.5.0: smarter local-model setup with an existing Ollama.** Spec: (1) the installer bundles the Ollama defined for 0.3.2 (CPU plus Vulkan, model download and Modelfile management, port 11450 to 11455); (2) it checks whether a local Ollama is installed and, if yes, asks it to download the embedding model and sets Settings to use the installed Ollama; (3) irrespective of that, the bundled Ollama is installed and available on a separate port between 11450 and 11455, depending on availability; (4) if the user turns off or uninstalls the existing Ollama, the bundled one works only for embedding operations. **Claude's assessment (not yet decided): good scheme, with four changes: detect inside the app (first run and Settings) and not in the installer; keep embeddings always on the bundled server so the search index never mixes vector sources; never switch silently and offer the bundled server for chat with one click when the existing one disappears; start the bundled server on demand. Full text and the open questions are in `docs/release_triage.md` (item #223 details).** **Deepak's reactions (2026-10-19): detect inside the app OK; embeddings need not stay on the bundled server (they are stored in SQLite, and running two Ollama servers loads memory), so the bundled one is a fallback only; never switch silently, inform the user and handle the settings; a setting that says which server the app uses and on-demand start are a DETAILED DESIGN ASPECT; when the existing Ollama is gone, embeddings fall back to the bundled one and chat uses a configurable default fallback model, which may be a cloud model. ## Documentation
- [ ] 92. Detailed product reference documentation — *(now also needs the redesign's user-facing behaviour: the managed Python runtime and `python_run`, the specialist roles and typed handoff, MCP Operator and server descriptions, writing your own role files, the experimental slim router — today these live only in the README bullets, `docs/design-discussion.md` and `docs/manual_testing.md`.)* a real user/reference doc, distinct from `README.md` (quickstart-shaped) and `docs/thouthts.md` (the original spec/requirements doc) and this file (build-tracking, not user-facing). Should cover: architecture overview (Electron main/renderer split, orchestrator + delegation model); every agent role and what tools/access it actually has (#28 and its since-landed additions); the full tool catalog by category (file ops, DB, API, MCP, office docs, email, decks, git/CLI, etc.) with what each does and its confirmation/mutation behavior; provider setup per provider (#14-20, #90 once landed); settings reference (workspace root, auto-approve rules, secret storage mode #75, data/API/MCP/email connections); and the extension points (adding a provider, adding a tool, adding a DB engine per #76) for anyone picking this project up later. Scope/format (single Markdown file vs. a docs folder, whether it lives in-repo or elsewhere) not yet decided.

  **Decided and started 2026-10-15 (user: reference docs are the top priority, to share the app with other users):** two separate documents, **User Guide** (`docs/user-guide/`) and **Developer Guide** (`docs/developer-guide/`), as Markdown folders in the repo with one numbered page per topic; `scripts/export-docs.mjs user|developer [--format md|pdf] [--out file]` (npm: `docs:user`, `docs:developer`) joins a folder into one Markdown file or a PDF (Electron `printToPDF`, same as chat export). Outline and page plan: `docs/docs-outline.md`. **Written (checked against the code), 2026-10-15:** the whole User Guide: 01-13 plus a generated tool catalog (`scripts/gen-tool-catalog.mjs`) and a glossary. **Developer Guide written 2026-10-15:** pages 01-12 plus the extension checklists (`docs/developer-guide/`). **Review status (2026-10-16):** the owner's review of the User Guide is finished, and the Developer Guide review was finished the same day (comments applied: diagrams on pages 03, 04, 07 and 13, step-by-step walkthroughs for adding a provider and writing a tool, Node version clarity, a Gmail example in both guides, and a new page 13, Memory and search history). **Pages brought up to 0.3.1 on 2026-10-16** (User Guide 02 and Developer Guide 11: Windows installer is now a zip and release.yml fetches the bundled runtimes first, #219; checked chat export images (#212), versions and the Linux note). **Remaining:** pages must still be updated whenever a user-visible change lands (standing rule, not a task); and the open design item #214 (bundled Ollama routing) which the providers page documents as a known limitation. Keep each page current when a user-visible change lands. **Screenshots (2026-10-15):** `scripts/export-docs.mjs` embeds images (PDF) or copies them next to the combined Markdown, and fails on a missing image file; 20 generated screenshots are in `docs/user-guide/images/` via `npm run docs:screenshots` (`scripts/docs-screenshots/*.mjs`, packaged build + throwaway profile + CDP; see `docs/user-guide/README.md`). Hand-taken screenshots all done (#213; macOS Gatekeeper shot dropped, Windows Finish page skipped on purpose).
